One is taken a minute after start-up and then every hour, only when something changed; pruning keeps everything from the last hour, the newest of each hour for 48 hours, the newest of each day for 30 days (and always the newest), and drops contents nothing uses. A `.git` folder is left out (it keeps its own history), as are temp files. The **Snapshots view** (the tray's Restore from a snapshot…, Trash's "Looking for an older version? Snapshots") lists them by day; one opens (folding) to its lists and notes, each "Changed since", "Deleted since" or "Same as now", the changed and deleted ones with Restore; "Restore everything…" asks inline. A restore takes a fresh snapshot first and is one undo step ("Restored Work", "Restored the whole Flint folder"); restoring one list brings back the pictures and files it names that are gone; restoring everything also removes what was added since (it is in the fresh snapshot) and reads everything again. **Export Flint folder…** (tray) saves a zip (`Flint 2026-11-04.zip` in Documents by default; the folder inside it named Flint; `.git` and temp files left out), written by Flint itself (no dependency), then shows it in Explorer.

### D-98 — Fixes after 0.4.29 and 0.4.30: the card hover's shadow, new lists in a group, the pinned Trash row, back to top, trash items opened read-only, Go to; the reminder sound setting
- **Date:** 4 Nov 2026 · **Status:** Decided by Davit (items 1 to 6 and the reminder sound); built (0.4.31) except the sound setting (M8); details marked **Proposed**. Davit confirmed D-96 (window state, the panel's scrollbar inset, the list chip's scroll, the primary hover, accepted as proposed) and D-97's step 1 (the chime file and its hash test, Trash and Archived), and accepted their Proposed choices ("(restored)" names, an archived task of a deleted list to Inbox, attachments under a day old never removed). Noted, not investigated: once, opening from a reminder took about half a second to a second longer than usual (to look at only if it shows again in measurements).
- **Reminder card hover, cause found (replaces D-96's explanation):** D-93 made the hover shadow smaller than the card's resting shadow, so on un-hover the card's large soft resting shadow grew back in: that was the second shadow over the card below (D-96's no-overshoot easing was right, but not the cause). `--fl-shadow-card-hover` is now the card's own shadow with only its ring brighter, so un-hovering changes nothing but the ring and the 2 px lift.
- **A new list or note inside a group (LIST-05):** a group's right-click menu starts with **New task list** and **New note**; the name field opens at the end of that group (opening a folded group), and the new list lands there. Creating it and placing it are one undo step; under a sort, it still goes into the group (membership isn't order).
- **The sidebar's foot (Trash, and Settings above it in M8):** pinned at the bottom; the lists and notes scroll underneath it, fading out just above it. Selected, Trash has the selection's look of its own (the sliding highlight stays with the scrolling rows).
- **Back to top (NAV-09, new):** in every page that scrolls (task lists, Today, notes, task pages, Trash), once scrolled down a little (**Proposed:** 320 px), a small round button at the bottom right of the main column (never over the side panel) rises in; a click scrolls smoothly to the top (at once under reduce-motion), and it fades out again near the top.
- **A trash item opened (DATA-02):** a click on a deleted task, list or note opens it **read-only**: in the side panel on wide screens (the row selected), as a page on narrow windows (a back arrow, Esc back). It shows the title, a list's tasks or a task's subtasks, and the text. Trying to edit it (a click on its title or text, a double-click) shows **"Restore to edit"** with **Restore**, which restores it and opens it where it now is, ready to edit (a task's title field open, a note's editor focused, a list open).
- **Restore from the list:** it stays in Trash as before; the toast offers **Undo** and **Go to <list>**, which opens the list with the restored task selected and flashed (a list or note simply opens).
- **Reminder sound (REM-06, for M8's Settings):** Flint's chime stays off by default. Settings gets **Reminder sound: Windows sound (default) / Flint chime**; choosing the chime makes the reminder toast silent (`<audio silent="true"/>`), so only one sound plays. An info card says why one would pick it (a distinct sound you recognise, the same volume every time) and what changes (the toast goes quiet; Windows' notification sound settings no longer apply to Flint's reminders). The full-screen Do Not Disturb case (D-95) is covered by mini mode's in-app alert (M8) and, until then, by the Overdue card. This answers D-95's "raised, not decided" point.
- **Motion (D-89):** the hover changes only the ring and the lift; the new list's field opens in its group as elsewhere; back to top rises in and fades out; the preview crossfades in the panel and rises in as a page; "Restore to edit" rises in; Go to flashes the task.

### D-99 — Quick notes, renaming in place, fixes after 0.4.31 and 0.4.32, the tray slimmed, snapshots compared with now
- **Date:** 4 Nov 2026 · **Status:** Decided by Davit (items 1 to 9 and the three answers below); built (0.4.33) except the snapshot settings (M8); details marked **Proposed**. Davit confirmed D-98 (the group menu, back to top, the Trash preview with Restore to edit, the restore toast's Go to) and accepted D-97's Proposed choices (`.git` left out of snapshots and exports; Restore everything removes newer files, recoverable from the fresh snapshot and with Ctrl+Z).
- **Quick notes (NOTE-08, new; supersedes "Permanent Scratchpad note" under "considered and not selected" in docs/01):** a **Quick note** row under Inbox and **Ctrl+Shift+J** (anywhere in the window) make an untitled note and put the caret in its editor, with no name to type. Quick notes save as they are written and are listed in a **Quick notes** section under Inbox, newest first, each labelled by its first line (or "Quick note · 15:30" while empty). **Decided (grill):** each is an ordinary top-level note file named after when it was made (`Quick note 2026-11-04 1530.md`, "(2)" added for a second in the same minute) with `quick: true` in its header (docs/04 §3); it gets a title from the **"Add a title"** field at its top (Enter) or by renaming it in the sidebar, which renames the file and drops the flag, one undo step; its menu also has **Keep as a note** (its time-based name) and **Move to group ▸**. An empty quick note goes for good when it is left (checked two seconds later, so an edit being saved as it was left keeps it). **Proposed:** a quick note isn't dragged out of its section yet (the menu does it); its menu has Delete (to Trash) too.
- **Renaming in place (LIST-01, LIST-05):** a list's or note's name in the sidebar is edited where it is, like a task's title: a double-click, or a slow second click (**Proposed:** 450 ms) on the list that is open; Enter saves, Esc cancels; **Proposed:** not while that list's task page is open (the click goes back to the list). A group renames on a **double-click** (**Proposed:** not on a slow second click, since a click on a group folds or opens it, and folding and unfolding again is two clicks on its name; the double-click's second click doesn't fold it back).
- **Reminder cards' shadow:** a close one (`--fl-shadow-card-close`: a 1 px ring, a 2 px and a 4 px / 10 px drop), at rest and on hover alike (the hover only brightens the ring), so nothing spills over the card below.
- **A new list in a group, cause found:** the field's group card eases its height, which moves the rows below by layout (no animation on them, nothing for the highlight's observer to see), and the highlight jumped to where the selected row would end; inside the group the card's own motion made the selected row seem to flash. The sliding highlight (lists and the sidebar alike) now follows its row's on-screen place, frame by frame, while anything in its card moves (a FLIP, a card's height, a fold), checked before the first frame is painted.
- **The pinned foot:** sticky positions count from the sidebar's content box, so its 14 px bottom padding showed lists through under Trash; the foot now reaches the window's bottom edge, solid.
- **"Restore to edit":** kept in view at the bottom of the panel (or the page) with the same gap for every kind (it fell below the fold on a long list, which is why it never showed there), and it fades away after **4 s** (**Proposed**), coming back at the next try.
- **The tray:** Open Flint, Quick capture, Mini mode, Settings, Quit Flint. Export Flint folder… and Restore from a snapshot… move to **Settings → Data** (M8) and to Ctrl+K commands; until then Trash's header links to **Snapshots** and to **Export Flint folder…**. **Proposed (deviation, for Davit to confirm):** "Hotkeys…" stays in the tray until Settings exists, since it is the only way to rebind the global hotkeys and the tray's conflict warning opens it. **Superseded by D-102:** hotkeys are recorded in Settings and "Hotkeys…" is gone.
- **Snapshots compared with now:** a click on a changed or deleted list or note in a snapshot opens its comparison (the side panel on wide screens, a page on narrow ones). A task list compares **by task ID**: added since, deleted since and changed (title, priority, reminder, notes, subtasks, completion), each with a checkbox; **Restore N tasks** puts the chosen ones as they were (a deleted one back after the nearest earlier task still there, a changed one replaced in place, one added since removed) and **Restore whole list** restores the file; one undo step, a fresh snapshot first. A note shows a **line diff** (added lines raised, removed ones struck through) with **Restore this note** or **Keep current**. A list or note deleted since shows its content read-only with **Restore**. **Later:** restoring single blocks inside a note.
- **Snapshot settings (M8, Settings → Data):** how often snapshots are taken, how long the hourly and the daily ones are kept, and the space they use, with **Clear old snapshots**; today's values stay the defaults (hourly; 48 hours; 30 days).
- **Motion (D-89):** quick notes ease into and out of their section; the highlight follows rows as they slide; "Restore to edit" rises in and fades out; the comparison crossfades in the panel and rises in as a page; checkboxes fill as they are chosen.

### D-100 — M8's order, and Settings as one page with a section index
- **Date:** 4 Nov 2026 · **Status:** Decided by Davit (the two answers below); step 1 built (0.4.34); details marked **Proposed**
- **Order (decided):** 1) **Settings** (APP-11) with all its sections; 2) the **Ctrl+K palette** and the **`?` cheat sheet** (NAV-01, NAV-02), from the same shortcuts list as Settings' Keyboard shortcuts (D-80); 3) **mini mode** (MINI-01) with the reminder alert (REM-06, D-94); 4) **onboarding**, the walkthrough, tips and What's new (APP-10, APP-13, APP-14, APP-15); 5) the **Clipboard section** (NAV-08), late in v1. One build per step, each with Davit's round of feedback.
- **Settings' layout (decided):** a **Settings** row above Trash in the sidebar's pinned foot (D-98) opens one scrolling page in the main column, its sections as cards: **General**, **Capture & hotkeys**, **Reminders**, **Today & lists**, **Data**, **Appearance**, **Keyboard shortcuts**, **About**; a slim **index** in the side panel's place jumps between them and marks the one in view (as a note's outline does); Ctrl+F finds a setting.
- **Step 1 (Proposed scope):** every setting that exists or was decided: start with Windows; the Flint folder (shown, Open folder); the two global hotkeys (shown; "Change…" opens the Hotkeys window of D-44 until its recorder moves into Settings); notifications on/off, the reminder sound (Windows sound / Flint chime with Play, its info card, the toast silent with the chime: D-98), hide task text in notifications (REM-10), the Focus Assist note (REM-06); the rollover prompt on/off (TODAY-02) and auto-archive (off / after N days, TASK-06); snapshots (how often, how long hourly and daily ones are kept, the space they use, Clear old snapshots, Restore from a snapshot…: D-99) and Export Flint folder… (DATA-05); the interface size (UI-12) and the animation speed (D-67); the keyboard shortcuts (D-80); the version and the logs folder. **Step 1b (next build):** the link-patterns editor (NOTE-07, D-84), changing the Flint folder (DATA-07), the hotkey recorder in place, tips on/off once tips exist.
- **Built in step 1 (0.4.34, Proposed details):** a row a setting (its name and what it does on the left, its control on the right): switches (the track lights, the knob slides), segmented choices, quiet buttons, keycaps. **Reminders:** notifications off means no notification and no chime (the Overdue card still lists them); "Play" previews the chime; an info card says why to choose it. **Today & lists:** auto-archive Off / 7 / 14 / 30 days. **Data:** a snapshot every 30 min / hour / 2 h / 4 h; hourly ones kept 1 day / 2 days / 3 days / 1 week; daily ones 14 / 30 / 60 / 90 days (other values in the file fall back to the defaults); the space used in MB, "Clear old snapshots" (asked inline; keeps the newest); Snapshots… and Export…. **Appearance:** interface size Smaller / 100% / Larger (the existing zoom, D-81); animation speed Relaxed (×1.35) / Normal / Quick (×0.7), scaling the main window's duration tokens at once (the capture popup keeps its speed: it is held to its latency budget, docs/05 §4); with Windows' "Show animations" off it says Flint doesn't animate. **Keyboard shortcuts:** from `shared/shortcuts.ts`, the one list Ctrl+K and `?` will read. **About:** the version, "Everything stays on this computer", the logs folder. The filter hides rows and sections that don't match (Esc clears it); the index marks the section in view and scrolls to one smoothly. The tray's Settings item is enabled and opens it. Every change is saved to `settings.json` and applied at once (start with Windows, the toast's silence, the rollover prompt, the snapshot timer and pruning, the animation speed).

### D-101 — Version numbers follow the milestone
- **Date:** 4 Nov 2026 · **Status:** Decided by Davit
- **Decision:** the next build is **0.8.0**. From now on the middle number is the milestone being built (M8 → 0.8.x, M9 → 0.9.x) and the last counts the builds within it, from 0. **1.0.0** was planned for the end of M9 (D-19). **Changed by D-132:** no build is 1.0.0 until the move to GitHub; M9's builds stay 0.9.x; 1.0.0 is the first release on GitHub Releases. Supersedes D-64's "0.4.x during M4" (Q-22); the builds before keep their numbers (0.4.0 to 0.4.34 span M4 to M8 step 1).

### D-102 — Fixes after 0.4.33 and 0.4.34: hotkeys recorded in Settings, a title keeps a quick note, two designs for the sidebar's foot, the comparison's action bar, exact interface sizes
- **Date:** 4 Nov 2026 · **Status:** Decided by Davit (the items); details marked **Proposed**. He confirmed renaming in place, the group-creation highlight (except the flash below), the reminder card hover, the Trash preview's message, the tray, the snapshot comparison (except its action bar) and Settings' basics.
- **Hotkeys in Settings (APP-08, decided):** the global hotkeys are recorded in **Settings → Capture & hotkeys**: click the keycap, press the new keys; Esc or clicking elsewhere cancels. D-44's rules and conflict detection stay: Ctrl, Alt or Win required, the two differ, Alt allowed with the push-to-talk note, the current hotkeys off while recording, and a combination another app owns keeps the old hotkey and says so. **The Hotkeys window is removed;** the tray is **Open Flint, Quick capture, Mini mode, Settings, Quit Flint**, and its warning item (a hotkey taken at start-up) opens Settings at Capture & hotkeys. **Proposed:** while it listens the keycap's ring lights and breathes; a taken hotkey is struck through and its note says so; each new message fades in.
- **Quick notes (NOTE-08, decided):** the "Add a title" field saves when it is left too (it saved only on Enter, so a typed title was lost). **A title no longer makes it a normal note at once:** the file takes the title and its flag becomes the day it was titled (`quick: 2026-11-04`, docs/04 §3); it stays under Quick notes, shown by its title, until **Keep as a note**, **Move to group ▸**, or the **next day change**, when titled quick notes become normal notes (checked at start-up and when the day changes; not an undo step). Untitled ones with text stay under Quick notes. Renaming one in the sidebar does the same. **Proposed:** a titled quick note is never discarded as empty when it is left (its title is something to keep).
- **The selected row's flash in a group (fixed):** a new list or note in the group of the selected row made that row flash. The group's move (FLIP) animated `transform`, which made the group card a stacking context painted over the selection's glide while it moved; groups now move by `top`/`left`, so the glide stays under the row throughout.
- **The sidebar's foot (for Davit to choose; D-103: Buttons chosen, Card and the setting removed):** two designs in one build, switched in **Settings → Appearance → Sidebar footer** (the setting goes once one is chosen): **Card** (the default): Settings and Trash in a rounded card inset from the sidebar's edges, the lists fading out above it; **Buttons**: Settings and Trash as two smaller rounded buttons side by side, Trash's count in its button.
- **The snapshot comparison (DATA-04):** in the side panel it scrolls (the panels in the context panel's place lacked D-93's check, so their overflow stayed hidden; the trash preview and Settings' index get it too), and its height fits the window from where it rests (on a page too short to scroll, its bottom used to sit below the window). Its action bar (Keep current / Restore this note, Restore chosen tasks…) is **sticky at the bottom of the card on a solid ground**, the diff fading out just above it, in the panel and as a page.
- **The reminder sound's purpose (REM-06, decided):** the Flint chime is for when **Windows' Do Not Disturb or Focus** holds notifications back and silences them. Flint plays the chime itself (app audio, not a notification sound), so it is still heard; the notification is silent with it (D-98), so there is one sound. Windows lets reminders through Do Not Disturb by default, but not when reminders are off in its priority list, and it turns Do Not Disturb on by itself in full-screen apps and games (Microsoft Support, "Notifications and Do Not Disturb in Windows"). The Reminder sound row, its info card and the former "Focus Assist" row (now **Do Not Disturb**) say so. **Check:** the chime is sent whether or not Windows shows the notification, and app audio isn't muted by Do Not Disturb; Davit confirms it by ear with Do Not Disturb on (the tests never change Windows' settings).
- **Exact choices (UI-12, fixed):** Interface size was three step buttons (Smaller / 100% / Larger) that moved one step from the current level, so from Smaller, Larger gave 100 %. It is now a segmented choice of exact levels, **80 / 90 / 100 / 110 / 125 / 150 %** (**Proposed**), the current one marked; pressing one sets exactly that level on this display; a level reached with Ctrl+= or Ctrl+− outside these marks none and the note says which ("Now 175 %"). Every other segmented choice already set the value pressed (checked: the animation speed, the reminder sound, auto-archive, the snapshot choices); the end-to-end test now jumps across options (Quick → Relaxed, 90 % → 125 %).
- **Version:** this build is **0.8.0** (D-101).

### D-103 — Fixes after 0.8.0: the sidebar's foot as two buttons, the panel's bottom always in the window
- **Date:** 4 Nov 2026 · **Status:** Decided by Davit (the items); details marked **Proposed**. He confirmed everything else in 0.8.0 and accepted the interface size levels (80 / 90 / 100 / 110 / 125 / 150 %, D-102), now **Decided**.
- **The sidebar's foot (decided):** **Buttons**: Settings and Trash as two rounded buttons side by side. The Card design and Settings → Appearance → Sidebar footer are removed (`sidebarFooter` in `settings.json` is ignored if present).
- **Their hover (fixed):** it must be exactly the button's shape. The highlight sat to the left of the buttons because the foot's design class was `row`, the task rows' class, so a task row's hover painted the whole foot strip from the window's edge; the foot has no such class now, and the hover and the selection are the button's own background and ring.
- **The fade above it (decided):** the lists blend out over 32 px above the foot (an eased gradient, **Proposed**) instead of being cut off; 26 px of space before the foot keeps the last list clear of the fade once the sidebar is scrolled to its end.
- **The labels (decided):** "Settings" and "Trash" with its count show in full on a normal screen at 100 %; each button is sized to its content and they share what is left. Where the count can't fit beside the name, the count is left out (it wraps onto a line the button doesn't show), never a cut word. **Proposed details:** 8 px sides, 6 px between the icon and the name; the count fits up to three digits at 100 %.
- **The context panel's height (decided, "never below the window"):** the context panel, and Today's column in its place, measure their height from where they are: resting at the panel's offset until the page scrolls them up to where they stick, their bottom stays 16 px above the window's (D-102 did this for the panels in the panel's place only). The page's scroll is passed to them only while it changes that (`--page-scroll`, clamped at the panel's offset).
- **Found on the way (fixed):** opening Trash, Settings or Snapshots kept the list open before them on screen until the next read arrived, so its header showed under theirs for a moment; it goes at once now.
- **Version:** 0.8.1 (D-101).

### D-104 — M8 step 1b: changing the Flint folder, and the link-patterns editor
- **Date:** 4 Nov 2026 · **Status:** Decided by Davit (grill); details marked **Proposed**
- **Changing the Flint folder (DATA-07, decided):** Settings → General → Flint folder → **Change…** opens Windows' folder picker. Flint then asks, based on the chosen folder's state, with the most likely option preselected and a confirmation that says exactly what will happen:
  - **An empty folder:** **Move my lists here** (preselected: everything in the Flint folder is copied there and checked file by file; then Flint switches; the old folder stays untouched as a backup, and Flint says where) or **Start fresh here** (a new Flint folder with an empty Inbox; the old folder stays as it is).
  - **A folder that already has Flint lists:** **Open these lists** (switch to them; nothing is copied). Merging the current lists into it is **not in v1** (Later, docs/06): both have an Inbox, task IDs are only unique within a folder, and Today's order, pins, groups, Trash and the archive would all need merging.
  - **Any other non-empty folder** (an Obsidian vault, Documents): **Use a Flint subfolder inside it** (preselected) or **Use this folder directly**, and then Move or Start fresh as for an empty folder.
  - The OneDrive warning (D-24) shows when the folder is inside OneDrive, and continuing is allowed. If copying or checking fails, Flint stays on the old folder, removes what it copied, and says why. Snapshots keep working: new ones come from the new folder; the old ones are kept until they expire.
  - **Switching restarts Flint (decided):** Settings says "Flint restarts to open it"; Flint saves the new path and relaunches itself; the main window comes back on Settings. Every watcher, reminder, the snapshot timer and the hotkeys start clean on the new folder.
  - **Proposed:** the folder itself, a folder inside the Flint folder, and one the Flint folder is in (as the folder used directly) are refused with a reason. "Empty" ignores Windows' `desktop.ini` and `Thumbs.db`. "Has Flint lists" means a `.flint` folder or a list with Flint's header. The copy skips `.flint/tmp` (atomic writes' scratch), copies everything else as it is, never overwrites a file (a name already there stops it, nothing changed), and checks every copied file's size and contents. "Use this folder directly" with Move is refused if a file of the same name is already there ("use a subfolder"). The choice is asked inline in the folder's row (as Clear old snapshots), not in a dialog.
- **The link-patterns editor (NOTE-07, D-84; decided):** its own Settings section, **Links**, after Today & lists (nine sections). **Proposed:** your patterns are listed as their short form and where it links (`AB#1234 → dev.azure.com/acme/web/…/edit/1234`), each with Delete (asked inline). **Add a link pattern** opens the editor in the card: paste an example link; a known kind (the presets: GitHub issues and pull requests, Azure DevOps work items and pull requests, Jira) is recognised at once and its short form filled in, which you can change; otherwise type the short form you want. A live preview shows both directions with another number ("Typing AB#1235 links to …", "Pasting that link shows AB#1235"). When it is ambiguous Flint asks for a second example; **Advanced** shows the generated pattern (read-only). Save is enabled once the pattern is clear; a pattern with the same short form as one you have is refused. To change a pattern, delete it and add it again (no Edit in v1). Saved patterns apply at once in titles, bodies and notes.
- **Built (0.8.2):** as above. **Found on the way (fixed):** the Settings index marked About when Settings opened, because the still-empty page counted as scrolled to its end; the end rule now needs a page that scrolls, and the index measures again as the page's content changes size.
- **Version:** 0.8.2 (D-101).

### D-105 — Fixes after 0.8.2: Trash without its count, link patterns edited, inferred between fixed text and guarded, the built-ins shown, links while typing
- **Date:** 5 Nov 2026 · **Status:** Decided by Davit (the items); details marked **Proposed**. He confirmed everything else in 0.8.2 (the foot's hover and fade, the panel on short lists, moving the folder with the restart, the folder cases, the OneDrive warning, the Settings index).
- **The sidebar's foot (decided):** the Trash button is just "Trash", the same size as Settings; the count shows inside Trash (its Deleted tab).
- **Edit (decided):** a saved pattern has **Edit**, the same editor as Add, filled in as it was left. Patterns keep the examples they were made from (`examples` in `settings.json`, docs/04); one saved before has an example made from it.
- **Inference (decided):** a part that changes can be a word between fixed text, not only a number: `https://dev.azure.com/acme/web/_git/unicorn-api` → `unicorn-api-repo` and `…/_git/platform` → `platform-repo` give `{name}-repo` → `…/_git/{name}` (a test fixture, with `DEV-123`/`DEV-200`). With two examples, when the parts that differ word by word don't make a pattern, Flint takes what differs between the two (the longest common start and end, never cutting a word) as the changing part, if the links differ in exactly that too.
- **The editor's second example (fixed):** once asked for, it stays while it is typed. It vanished at the first key of its short form (the examples disagreed for a moment), taking the caret, so a pattern like `DEV-123` couldn't be finished ("The examples don't follow one pattern").
- **A guard against over-broad patterns (decided):** a short form needs fixed text before or after the part that changes (**Proposed:** at least 2 characters, not spaces): `Qx7Lp-M42sTn` alone made almost every word a link. Without it Flint doesn't save, says why, and suggests one (the site's initials and a colon: `de:Qx7Lp-M42sTn`; **Use it** puts it in both examples). The preview also says how many places in your lists and notes it would link now, and warns when that looks too many (**Proposed:** more than 20); saving is still allowed then.
- **The built-ins (decided):** Settings → Links lists them read-only (Azure DevOps work item `AB#42`, pull request `PR 42`, GitHub issue `repo#42` and pull request `repo!42`, Jira `KEY-42`), each with a switch; one turned off no longer shortens pasted links of its kind (`builtInLinksOff`, docs/04).
- **Links everywhere text shows (decided):** the user's patterns were already applied in task titles, task notes (inline, in the panel, on a page), notes, Today and Up next; checked end to end with `DEV-123`, which was missing only because the pattern couldn't be saved (above). **New:** while a task is typed, the composer and the capture popup show a **link chip** for each link it will show (a pasted URL as its short form, a typed short form), and the capture popup now knows the patterns (sent with each opening); in the editors a pasted URL shows its short form as soon as the caret isn't inside it (just after a paste, at once), the URL itself while the caret is inside it (**Proposed**; other markdown keeps D-79's cursor-line rule). A title being edited in place stays plain text.
- **Version:** 0.8.3 (D-101).

### D-106 — M8 step 2: the Ctrl+K palette and the `?` cheat sheet
- **Date:** 5 Nov 2026 · **Status:** Decided by Davit (grill); details marked **Proposed**
- **The palette's look (decided):** a centred overlay about 640 px wide, 12 % from the top, styled like the capture popup (a solid floating card with its deep shadow), over at most a very light dim of the window (no heavy darkening, no blur or translucency). A search field, then results grouped **Go to**, **Tasks**, **Notes**, **Commands**; ↑/↓ and Enter, Esc closes. It opens with **Ctrl+K** anywhere in the main window or the **"Search or run a command · Ctrl K"** pill in the header of lists, Today and Settings (mockup 01), with D-75's motion (it rises in quickly and goes the same way; input is never held).
- **What it searches (decided):** lists, notes and groups by name; open tasks of every list by title and notes, then completed ones lower down; the lines of notes; and commands. Not Trash or the archive: with a query, the **last row** offers **Search Trash and Archive** (also when nothing else matches); choosing it shows those results, marked deleted or archived, and opening one goes to its Trash or Archived view. Before anything is typed: recent places, the selected task's actions, and the most used commands. Matching is fuzzy: every word of the query as a piece of the text (best at a word's start), or one word as its letters in order (word starts and runs count more).
- **Commands (decided):** Go to Today, Inbox, Settings (and each Settings section), Trash, Snapshots; Create: New task list, New note, New group, Quick note; the selected task: its right-click menu's actions (Complete, Edit, Open as page, Priority, Reminder, Pin, Add, **Move to…**, Duplicate, More, Delete); this list: the temporary sorts, Find in list, Rename; the app: Show/hide side panel, Interface size, Open Flint folder, Export Flint folder…, Restore from a snapshot…, Keyboard shortcuts, Quit Flint. Each shows its shortcut on the right. A command with choices is a **second step** inside the palette with a breadcrumb (Move to ›, Priority ›); Backspace in an empty field goes back. **Rule for the steps after this one (decided):** every new feature with an action adds its palette command at the same time (Mini mode in step 3; Replay walkthrough and What's new in step 4).
- **The cheat sheet (decided):** `?` in Navigate state (never while typing) opens a card in the palette's style, about 780 px wide: every shortcut in two columns, grouped as in Settings, from the same list (D-80), with a filter field (typing narrows it). `?` in the empty field or Esc closes it; the palette's Keyboard shortcuts command opens it too.
- **Proposed details:** the selected task's actions come from its right-click menu (one source: a submenu becomes a step; Move to… lists the task lists); a result opens where it is (a task selected and flashed in its list, a note opened, a deleted item opened in Trash, an archived task on the Archived tab); results are searched in the main process a moment after the last key (70 ms) and capped; "most used" is counted on this computer (the window's local storage, a convenience only); the pill shows its icon and key alone on narrow windows. **Ctrl+K in an editor with text selected** makes that text a link (`[text]()`, the caret where the address goes; docs/03 §3's open conflict, resolved this way); with nothing selected it opens the palette.
- **Version:** 0.8.4 (D-101). **0.8.5 (fixed):** a new note's editor (Ctrl+Shift+J, a new note) sometimes didn't get the caret: the request was cleared at once while the editor only took the caret when it was made; it now takes it whenever asked, and again over the next frames if the view took it back as it settled.

### D-107 — Fixes after 0.8.3–0.8.5; the end-to-end tests' fixed clock; the day change at midnight
- **Date:** 5 Nov 2026 · **Status:** Decided by Davit (his 0.8.3–0.8.5 feedback and the test clock); details marked **Proposed**
- **Confirmed:** 0.8.3's six fixes (D-105); Ctrl+K for links in notes, the `?` cheat sheet, the quick-note caret fix and the palette's task actions (D-106).
- **The empty quick note's tip (NOTE-08, decided):** when an empty quick note is removed on leaving it, a toast says "Empty quick notes are removed when you leave them", the first three times only, then never again (counted on this computer, the window's local storage; **Proposed**).
- **Renaming in place (LIST-01, LIST-05, decided):** clicking or tabbing out of a list's, note's or group's name field saves the new name, like a task's title; only Esc cancels. **Proposed:** the same for naming a new list, note or group (a typed name is never lost); a field left empty or unchanged just closes; a name that can't be used, when the field is left, closes it with the reason as a toast (on Enter the reason stays under the field, as before).
- **The palette's steps (NAV-01, decided):** a step (Move to…, Priority, Interface size, and Trash and Archive) has a back arrow before its breadcrumb; Esc goes back one step and closes the palette only from the top; Backspace in the empty field goes back too.
- **One order (decided):** Go to → Commands → Tasks → Lists and notes by name → lines inside notes, always last (a query like "quick" shows the Quick note command before any note). "Go to" is the fixed places (Today, Settings and its sections, Trash, Snapshots); lists, notes and groups are their own group after the tasks. **Proposed:** the selected task's actions ("This task") sit just before Commands.
- **No header pill (decided; supersedes D-106's pill and mockup 01's):** the "Search or run a command · Ctrl K" pill leaves the headers of lists, Inbox, Today and Settings (it looked like a search field but was only a button). Instead a small search icon button at the end of the sidebar header's row, next to the Flint mark, with the tooltip "Search or run a command (Ctrl+K)". The palette has a footer: ↑↓ navigate · Enter open · Esc back/close (as it applies), and "Ctrl K opens this anywhere" on the right.
- **A note's line (decided; replaces D-106's Proposed "at the top"):** choosing a line inside a note opens the note scrolled to that line (a third from the top), with the highlight flash. "Most used" counted on this computer: accepted.
- **For step 4, onboarding (decided, recorded):** the first-run walkthrough includes opening the palette with Ctrl+K; and someone who opens the palette with the mouse several times, over time (not just in a row), gets a one-time tip that Ctrl+K opens it.
- **The test clock (decided):** the end-to-end tests run on a fixed clock: `smoke.spec.ts` on Tuesday 10 March 2026 from 10:00 local, so results never depend on when the suite runs (0.8.4's and 0.8.5's night-time CI failures came from the real clock). The test hands Flint the offset from the real time (`FLINT_E2E_CLOCK_OFFSET`, test mode only, never in an installed build); the main process and every window (through their preloads) read the time through a `Date` shifted by it, so the clock still runs and timers, countdowns and the day change behave as for real (`src/shared/test-clock.ts`). A date in the past keeps file times (real) after the test clock, so nothing counts as old by accident.
- **The day change, on purpose (decided):** `day-change.spec.ts` starts Flint at 23:59:30 with Today open and crosses into Wednesday: the date and greeting, the rollover card (and Roll over), done pins losing their pin (in the file too), a reminder moving from Tomorrow to Today (its card counting down, the timeline, Up next), the Overdue card's "Yesterday".
- **A real bug they found (fixed):** at midnight the open Today showed the new date over the old day's data for up to two minutes: no rollover card, the done pin still pinned, tomorrow's reminder not yet today's. Flint checked the day once a minute, counted from when it started, and the window only re-read its views on its own minute's refresh. Now the main process acts at midnight itself (a timer to the next local midnight, with the minute's check and waking from sleep as fallbacks): done pins lose their pin, then the window reads every view again at once.
- **Two more bugs found while making the suite steady (fixed):** (1) the window's reads overlap (a file written outside Flint, a change, the minute's refresh), and one that came back after a newer one put its older lists on screen: a list that had just appeared looked deleted, and the window went back to the place before it (D-86). Clicking a new list, or another one right after a rename, could bounce back. An older read is now dropped. (2) A new note's "take the caret" request was given up on the editor's first render, before the caret had arrived; an editor made again as the view settled could open without it (0.8.5's fix narrowed this, not closed it). The request now stands until the editor has the focus, and is forgotten when another place is opened. Also, the field's blur as Enter removes it never saves a second time.
- **The suite's own steadiness:** the submenu-aim check (a 300 ms grace between real mouse moves) gets a few tries on a machine busy enough to stall the moves; a broken aim still fails every try.
- **Version:** 0.8.6 (D-101).

### D-108 — Fixes after 0.8.6
- **Date:** 5 Nov 2026 · **Status:** Decided by Davit (his 0.8.6 feedback); details marked **Proposed**
- **Confirmed:** the quick-note tip, the palette's back step and Esc, the result order, the note-line jump, the onboarding notes, the handoff, the fixed test clock and the midnight tests. D-107's Proposed choices are accepted: a new list's, note's or group's name is also saved on click-away; "This task" sits just before Commands.
- **A rename saved on leaving looks like Enter (decided):** the row no longer plays the delete animation (its group shrinking) and comes back. The cause, in both cases: the field closed before the new name was read, so the row showed its old name for a moment and then "arrived" again. Now a rename gives way to its row in place, in the render that brings the new name (as a group's rename did, D-80), and the field carries its row's motion key, so the row neither leaves nor arrives. **Also fixed:** a click on another list in the sidebar didn't save a rename at all (a sidebar row keeps the focus on mouse down, so the field never blurred, and starting another rename dropped it); a press anywhere outside the field now leaves it (not one in the field's own right-click menu).
- **The search button's hover (decided):** reliable. It sat in the window's 40 px drag strip at the top of the sidebar, where Windows takes the pointer, so its hover only showed on its lower edge; it is carved out of the strip (`-webkit-app-region: no-drag`).
- **The note line's flash (decided):** a little brighter (`--fl-ground-hover` with an `--fl-edge-float` ring), held a moment, 1.6 s in all (the other flashes stay as they are). Off under reduce-motion like all motion.
- **The greeting (decided):** "Good evening" until 04:00, then morning (to 12:00), afternoon (to 18:00), evening.
- **Version:** 0.8.7 (D-101).

### D-109 — M8 step 3: mini mode
- **Date:** 5 Nov 2026 · **Status:** Decided by Davit (grill, Q1–Q7); built in 0.8.8; details marked **Proposed**
- **Mini mode and the main window (decided, Q1):** both can be open at once. Opening mini mode doesn't hide the main window; **Open** on a task or reminder in mini brings the main window forward at that place without closing mini; × on mini hides it (to the tray). Both show the same data and stay in sync.
- **A third tab, Note (decided):** next to Today and Inbox, for a sticky note pinned in a screen corner. It is a quick note (NOTE-08): it shows under Quick notes in the main app and follows the same rules (an empty one is removed when it is left; a title; Keep as a note). Mini shows one quick note at a time: the most recent one, or a new one if there is none, with a small menu to switch to another quick note or start a new one. A compact editor with the same live preview as other notes.
- **The reminder alert (REM-06, decided, Q2):** the Windows reminder toast always fires as now, also when mini is open; mini's alert card (mockup 05: Done · Snooze 10m · Open) is an additional place to act. With mini open the card shows at the top of whichever tab is showing until it is acted on; several at once queue as "1 of 3". Acting in either place clears the other: Done, Snooze or Open in mini removes the toast and its Action Center entry, and acting on the toast removes the card from mini. With mini closed nothing opens by default. A new setting in Settings → Reminders, **Show reminders in Flint**: "In mini mode when it's open" (default) / "Always, opening mini mode" (for someone with Windows notifications off). No card in the main window (Today already shows reminders).
- **How a toast is taken back (Proposed, checked against Electron 45):** Flint keeps each reminder's `Notification` until its reminder is acted on and calls its `close()`, which on Windows dismisses it and removes it from Action Center. The static `Notification.remove()` / `removeGroup()` are macOS-only, so a toast left from before Flint restarted can't be taken back; it stays until Windows or the user clears it (its buttons still act on the task).
- **Opening and closing it (decided, Q3):** "Mini mode" in the tray menu (D-98); a **Mini mode** palette command that shows or hides it (D-106); a small button in the main window's title-bar strip, left of the side-panel toggle (tooltip "Mini mode"); and a third global hotkey, **Show/hide mini mode**, default **Ctrl+Alt+Space** (it pairs with Ctrl+Shift+Space for capture), recordable in Settings → Capture & hotkeys with the usual conflict handling (if Windows or another app holds it, the warning and a rebind in Settings, D-44, D-102). No in-app key (Ctrl+Shift+M is Monospace in notes). × on mini, the command or the hotkey hides it to the tray; it stays alive, hidden, so it reopens at once.
- **A deliberate exception to D-44's no-Alt rule (decided):** the default Ctrl+Alt+Space includes Alt, which keys a push-to-talk for a moment (Discord); Davit accepts that brief activation for this hotkey. No other default uses Alt, and the tests still never press Alt for real.
- **The toast limit after a restart (accepted):** as above.
- **The Today and Inbox tabs (decided, Q4):** **Today**: the open pinned tasks first (Today's order), then the tasks with a reminder later today that aren't pinned, as compact rows with the bell and the time, soonest first; no high-priority group. **Inbox**: its open tasks in the main window's order. Both composers take the capture shorthand ("Add to Today…" adds to Inbox already pinned, like Today's composer; "Add to Inbox…" adds at the top). A row: **a single click selects it** (like the main window); **double-click or Enter opens it in the main window**, at that task, and mini stays (no single-click open, so a mis-click during a call never throws the main window in front). The checkbox completes with the completion animation, then the row leaves (no Completed section in mini); Ctrl+Z undoes. Right-click: Complete, Pin/Unpin, Open in Flint. The priority bar on the left (mockup 05). No title editing in mini.
- **The Note tab (decided, Q5):** with no quick note, an empty "Write here" editor; the quick note is made on the first keystroke (looking at the tab never makes and removes files). "Leaving" it is switching tab or hiding mini: an empty one is removed then, with the same tip (its three showings shared with the main window). The header: the "Add a title" field (saved on Enter or when left, D-102) and a ⋯ menu: the other quick notes, newest first, to switch to; New quick note; Keep as a note; Open in Flint (the main window opens at this note; mini stays). After Keep as a note the note stays shown until the tab is left, with a quiet "Kept in Lists"; the next time, the tab shows the most recent quick note. A compact editor with the same live preview (links, checklists, code, images small), tighter padding, no outline or panel.
- **One window edits a note at a time (decided, Q5):** the window where Davit is typing owns the note; the other shows it updating live, read-only, with a quiet line, "Editing in mini mode" or "Editing in the main window". Clicking into the other window's note moves the editing there: the first window saves first, then hands over. Two windows' unsaved edits can never overwrite each other. **Proposed mechanism:** the main process holds who edits each note; the owner sends its text as it is typed (about every 100 ms) and the main process passes it to the other window, which shows it read-only; saving to disk stays the owner's (0.6 s after typing stops, on blur, on leaving); a hand-over asks the owner to save, waits for that save, then grants the editing to the window that asked.
- **The window (decided, Q6):** first opened at the top right of the screen the mouse is on, 24 px from the edges, at mockup 05's size (about 440 × 500); after that where it was left, remembered per monitor setup (APP-05). Dragged by its header, it snaps to a screen corner when let go within 48 px of one, and stays in that corner when resized or when the screens change. Always on top by default, also over a full-screen Teams call (like the capture popup); the header's pin turns it off and on (remembered). Resizable from 320 × 260 to 720 × 900; its tab, scroll and note shown are remembered. It takes the keyboard focus only when Davit opens it (hotkey, command, tray, button), never when a reminder opens it (the "always" setting: shown without focus). No taskbar button (the tray, like the capture popup). The floating look of 09 (the popup's card, radius and deep shadow), as in mockup 05. The Proposed note-ownership mechanics above are accepted.
- **Hidden from screen sharing (decided):** mini mode is left out of screen shares and recordings by default (window content protection: `setContentProtection(true)`, which on Windows 10 2004 and later is `SetWindowDisplayAffinity(WDA_EXCLUDEFROMCAPTURE)`: still visible on Davit's screen, absent from a shared screen, not blacked out). The capture popup gets the same, also on by default. Two switches: Settings → Mini mode → **Hide from screen sharing** (on) and the same for the capture popup in Settings → Capture & hotkeys (on). **To verify when built:** a real Teams screen share (whole screen and a window), as Electron 35 had a report of protected windows captured black instead of left out (electron/electron#45990); if Electron 45 shows that, Flint calls `SetWindowDisplayAffinity` with `WDA_EXCLUDEFROMCAPTURE` itself (`main/win32.ts`). Some direct GPU capture paths can still see such a window on some drivers (IOActive); Teams, Zoom, OBS and PrintScreen honour it.
- **The keyboard (decided, Q7):** the main window's keys where they mean the same: ↑/↓ move the selection, Space completes or reopens, P pins or unpins, Enter opens the task in the main window (mini stays), N goes to the composer, Ctrl+1/2/3 Today/Inbox/Note, Ctrl+Z/Ctrl+Y undo/redo, Esc clears the selection and then leaves the composer or the note, Ctrl+Alt+Space hides mini. **Esc never hides mini.** The alert card takes D (Done), S (Snooze 10m) and O (Open), shown as key hints on its buttons. Not in mini: 1/2/3/0, R, Delete (those edits stay in the main window, Q4). **Every single-letter key (Space, P, N, D, S, O) acts only when no text field is focused** (the main window's Navigate/Edit rule): an alert arriving while Davit types in the composer or the Note tab leaves the keystrokes in the text; its keys work once the field is left (Esc) or by mouse. The shared shortcuts list gets a Mini mode group (the `?` cheat sheet and Settings show it).
- **Plan (grill finished, 5 Nov 2026):** built as **0.8.8**. Main process: `main/mini.ts` (the window: made on first use, hidden never destroyed, its place per monitor setup, corner snapping, on top, content protection), the third hotkey in the registry, the alert queue beside the toasts (each reminder's `Notification` kept to be closed), the note-editing owner per note, the tip counts in app data (shared by both windows). Renderer: `renderer/mini/` (React, its own `app://mini` host so zoom and storage stay its own). Main window: the title-bar button, the Mini mode command, read-only notes while mini edits them. Settings: Mini mode (Hide from screen sharing, Show reminders in Flint) and the popup's Hide from screen sharing, the third hotkey's row. Tests: `e2e/mini.spec.ts` on the fixed clock (mini kept off-screen and never focused, as the main window in the suite); unit tests for the corner snapping, the hotkey registry's third name and the settings.
- **To verify by hand (Davit):** a real Teams screen share (mini and the popup absent, not black); Ctrl+Alt+Space with Discord running; a toast's Action Center entry gone after Done in mini.
- **Built (0.8.8), with these Proposed details:**
  - **The window's look:** opaque, frameless, with Windows 11's own rounded corners (8 px) and shadow, on the floating ground (`--fl-ground-float`), not the popup's 22 px transparent card: a transparent window can't be resized reliably on Windows. Mockup 05's 22 px radius is the one deviation.
  - **Opening it:** Today and Inbox open with no field focused (the keys work at once; N goes to the composer); the Note tab puts the caret in the note. A reminder opens it without the focus.
  - **Inbox:** its open top-level tasks (subtasks stay in the main window). A pinned task shows a small pin there.
  - **The Note tab:** the title field works once the note exists (after the first keystroke); "New quick note" shows an empty editor until typed into; a note made in mini always counts as shown, even before the lists are read again.
  - **The reminder card:** a card whose task is done, snoozed, muted or deleted elsewhere goes too. Its keys D, S, O show as keycaps on its buttons.
  - **Small messages** in mini (Completed · Undo, the empty-note tip) as a toast at its bottom.
  - **The tips' count** moved from each window's local storage to app data (`tips.json`), so the main window and mini share it (it starts again once).
  - **The palette:** "Mini mode" shows or hides it; Go to → Settings: Mini mode ranks above it for "mini mode" (D-107's order).
- **Bugs found while building it (fixed):** (1) `window-state.json` was written by a separate copy per window, so a second remembered window (mini) and the main window would have overwritten each other's places; one shared copy now. (2) A change that came into an editor from outside (a save, an undo, the other window's live text) counted as typing: the receiving editor saved it again, which could have put an older text over a newer one; such changes are marked and never saved back. (3) While building the hand-over: a note made on the first keystroke claims the editing once it has its ID, and only then (claiming again whenever the editing moved took it straight back).

### D-110 — Mini mode rebuilt on the main window's parts (0.8.9)
- **Date:** 6 Nov 2026 · **Status:** Decided by Davit (grill, Q1–Q4, after his look at 0.8.8); built in 0.8.9; details marked **Proposed**
- **Why:** 0.8.8's mini mode worked but didn't look or move like Flint: about 1.5 times mockup 05's size, a lighter ground, Windows' scrollbar, its own selection colour without the glide, no drag, tabs switching without motion, a pin that didn't show its state, Windows' 8 px corners. It drew its own copies of the rows, checkbox, composer and toast.
- **The rule (decided):** mini mode is built from the main window's own components, tokens and behaviours (task rows, checkbox and completion animation, selection highlight and its glide, the drag, scrollbars, menus, the composer with its shorthand chips, the editor, toasts, motion); where it needs a smaller size, that is a size variant of the shared component (`.fl-compact`, `variant="compact"`), never a copy.
- **Q1, the extraction (decided):** first, as its own behaviour-neutral commit, the shared parts moved out of `App.tsx` into `renderer/ui/` (Row, Glide, the completion ghosts, row drag, Composer, Toast, QuickTitle, presence and motion helpers) and the stylesheet became `ui/app.css`, loaded by both windows (renamed, not reordered). Gate: unit tests, typecheck, lint and all 52 e2e tests unchanged, and before/after screenshots of Inbox, Today, an expanded task, the side panel with a task selected, a frame mid-drag and a completion mid-animation. Result: pixel-identical except a few pixels of the sidebar logo, which vary the same way between two runs of the unchanged code (raster noise). The same screenshots were taken again after the mini work: still identical.
- **Q2, the window (decided):** transparent like the capture popup, the card with mockup 05's look: 22 px radius, `--fl-shadow-float`, on `--fl-ground-card` (the mockup's ground; 09 §6's "floating ground" for D-109 is superseded). Sizes from mockup 05: the card 364 px wide (300 to 520), its height fitting its content up to a 460 px limit (180 to 760), then its body scrolls; remembered per monitor setup. The card hangs from its snapped corner and grows away from it. Resize handles in the margin. Davit judges the real size after use.
  - **Proposed, how:** Electron can't resize a transparent window natively (broken since Electron 39, electron#48554), so the window is a stage: the largest card's area (520 × 760) from the corner the card hangs from, with the shadow's margin (72 px at the sides, 36 above, 108 below, as the popup's). The card's resizing and height are the page's own (CSS), so the window never moves for them; the handles sit on the edges facing away from the hang corner. Clicks pass through the stage except over the card, its handles and open menus. Unlike the popup, mini doesn't forward mouse moves (that watches every mouse move on the system and made the cursor flicker over Flint's other text fields, D-69; mini is open for hours): main checks the cursor against what the page reports, every 16 ms near mini and every 120 ms elsewhere, only while it is shown; a button held in the page keeps the clicks. Snapping into another corner hides the card for a frame while the window moves, then it settles in (fade and scale, `--fl-dur-base`). Dragged by a 32 × 4 px grip at the top centre (the popup's) and by the header.
- **Q3, the reminder card (decided):** on Today, the next reminder still to come today (bell tile, time, title, live countdown); when a reminder fires, the same card turns into the alert (Done · Snooze 10m · Open, D/S/O outside text fields, "1 of N"); on Inbox and Note the alert rises in at the top. A click on the upcoming card selects its row, a double-click opens it. In the Note tab it never takes the focus or moves the caret; typing goes on into the note, and the note slides down as the card folds in. **Proposed:** its label stays the toast's ("Reminder", D-68), not mockup 05's "Reminder · 15:00".
- **Q4, the composer (decided):** the shared composer in its compact size: no tool buttons (the shorthand does it), the `##` menu and list card opening above the field, the two placeholders ("Add to Today…" adds to Inbox pinned, "Add to Inbox…"), and the main composer's hint (`or type !h ##list @15:00 · Shift+Enter for notes`) shown faintly under the field only while it is focused and empty, going as typing starts. **Proposed:** the send arrow beside the field (mockup 05), strong when there is text.
- **Accepted Proposed (Davit):** Alt+↑/↓ moves the selected task in mini as in the main window; Today's reminder rows keep their time order and don't drag (they nudge).
- **Also built (Proposed):** rows are the main window's (13 px, 17 px checkboxes, 9 px padding), showing the pin, the bell and the time (`15:00`, not `Today 15:00`) as indicators, not buttons, and no home list or body toggles; tabs are the main window's segmented control, the content arriving like a page (`view-in`, `--fl-dur-page`), the card's height easing on a tab or note change; the pin filled while on top and an outline when not (the fill eased); the animation-speed setting reaches mini too (it never did); a secondary button style (09 §8) for Snooze and Open.
- **Note tab fixes:** (1) the "Add a title" field works before the body has text: a title makes the note; (2) an empty note removed when it was left (2 s later) while the tab was shown again left no note to show, and 0.8.8 then showed no editor and a disabled title: a removed note is never chosen again, and with none left the tab shows a new one; (3) New quick note is disabled while the current note is completely empty. The title is the main window's `QuickTitle`.
- **To verify by hand (Davit):** a Teams and a Discord full-screen share (mini and the popup absent, not black); Ctrl+Alt+Space with Discord running; Action Center cleared after Done in mini; and now also the card's edges and shadow on a light and a dark desktop, a click just outside the card reaching the app below, resizing from the handles, and snapping into each corner.

### D-111 — Fixes after 0.8.9: editing in the main window, link patterns with two parts, mini mode's size and motion (0.8.10)
- **Date:** 6 Nov 2026 · **Status:** Decided by Davit (his look at 0.8.9; grill on the open points); built in 0.8.10; details marked **Proposed**
- **Accepted from D-110's Proposed details:** the send arrow solid with text, rows showing `15:00`, the fade when snapping into a corner, the height easing on a tab or note change.
- **Link patterns (not a bug):** the "regression" was a built-in short form typed by hand (`PR 56` can't link: it lacks the repository); the patterns work, and an e2e check now guards them (a pattern from the settings at start, in a task title, its notes and a note). **Two changing parts (decided: add it):** the engine already matched several placeholders; Settings → Links now also infers two from two examples, with fixed text between them (`unicorn-api-PR-45` and `platform-PR-12` give `{name}-PR-{id}` for `…/_git/{name}/pullrequest/{id}`), and the fixed-text guard (D-105) counts text between the parts too.
- **Main window (decided):** (2) a note's text selection covers only the characters, like text fields: the editor uses the browser's own selection (styled by UI-11) instead of CodeMirror's drawn one, which filled whole lines. (3) The composer's notes field takes a click after the title had it (the card sent every click to the title). (4) Each composer keeps its unsent draft (title with its pasted stretches still inert, notes, pastes) per list for the session, in each window, like the popup's drafts; sending clears it. (5) An empty new row that is left (in a list, or Add subtask on a task's page) plays the remove animation where it was. (6) A note is renamed from its title on its page: a click (it is the open note, so this is the sidebar's slow second click, decided) turns the title into a field in its own type; Enter saves, a problem shows under it and the field stays; leaving saves, a problem then closes it with a message; Esc cancels. (7) Shorthand typed into a task being edited shows on its row at once: `!h` the priority stripe (easing in, and out when deleted), `@15:00` the time where a reminder sits, `##list` a small list chip first in the indicators, its text changing in place as the name is typed; saving works as before (the task moves to that list).
- **Mini mode (decided):** (8) one size for the whole window, a width and a height limit shared by every tab: Today and Inbox fit their content up to the limit, the Note tab always takes it; switching tabs never changes the size, resizing in any tab changes it. (9) A Reset size button left of the pin while the size differs from the default (364 × 460); it eases the card back and goes; a resize let go within 8 px of the default snaps to it (**Proposed**), so the button goes then too. (10) A slow second click on a selected row opens the task in the main window (mini has no title editing), as a double-click does. (11) The bell on a row turns its reminder off and on with the main window's strike (decided: a muted reminder that isn't pinned stays in mini's Today, struck, to turn back on; Today's data carries today's turned-off reminders for this, `TodayExtras.muted`, which nothing else shows). (12) D/S/O work whenever mini has the focus and no text field does: mini now takes the foreground like the popup when Windows' foreground lock refuses it (it could show without the keyboard). (13) Mini and the capture popup appear and disappear with motion: the card fades and scales in on show and out on hide; hiding gives the focus back at once and lets clicks through, and the window hides after the fade; the popup's card waits slightly faded while hidden, so its first frame after the hotkey already shows it with the cursor in the field.
- **From the real machine (decided):** the bottom shadow is tighter (`--fl-shadow-desktop`: the float ring and highlight, `0 1px 3px` and `0 12px 30px -10px` drops), soft on a white desktop too; the window's margins follow it (16 px above, 32 at the sides, 48 below). Nothing above a card in a top corner takes the mouse: the height is resized from the edge away from the corner (decided): a top corner's bottom edge, a bottom corner's 3 px top edge. Both side edges always resize: the stage keeps room on the hang side (the card's hang point moves, the window doesn't), so resizing works from every side after any move or snap. Verified by Davit on 0.8.9: Discord full-screen share (mini and popup absent), Ctrl+Alt+Space with Discord running, Action Center cleared after Done in mini, resizing and corner snapping. Still to check: a Teams share.
- **Proposed, not changed:** the capture popup keeps its own shadow (`--fl-shadow-float`); it could take the desktop one too.

### D-112 — Fixes after 0.8.10; Replay walkthrough in Settings (0.8.11)
- **Date:** 6 Nov 2026 · **Status:** Decided by Davit (his look at 0.8.10); built in 0.8.11
- **Confirmed by Davit on 0.8.10:** D-111's items 1 to 4, 6 and 7 in the main window, mini's 8 to 13, the shadow, the top edge and resizing after a move; D/S/O right after Ctrl+Alt+Space. A Teams share is still to test.
- **Fixes:** (1) the side panel's subtasks move as the list's rows do: a new one opens its place, a deleted one and an empty new one that is left fold away where they were (`row-in`, `row-remove`); another task's subtasks just appear. (2) Mini's Note tab sits a little lower under the tabs (10 px more above "Add a title" and the editor together, the space between them unchanged). (3) No scrollbar while mini's height eases to another tab's (the body doesn't scroll during it). (4) The capture popup takes mini's softer shadow (`--fl-shadow-desktop`), so both windows over other apps match (D-111's open point).
- **For step 4, onboarding (decided):** Settings gets **Replay walkthrough** (for testing, and for anyone who skipped it or wants a refresher), as well as the tray (APP-10). The first-run walkthrough says it can be skipped now and replayed any time from Settings.

### D-113 — M8 step 4: Inbox triage into v1, then onboarding (walkthrough, tips, What's new)
- **Date:** 6 Nov 2026 · **Status:** Decided by Davit (grill); details marked **Proposed**
- **Order (decided):** step 4 is now **4a, Inbox triage** (TASK-14, moved from Later into v1), built before **4b, onboarding**, so the walkthrough and the tips can include it.
- **Triage (decided):** a mode in the main column: the Inbox page becomes one large card (the task, its notes, when it was captured) with its actions and keycaps below, "3 of 18" and a thin progress line at the top; Esc leaves. Started from a "Triage · N" button in Inbox's header, Today's "Sort Inbox · N" button, the palette ("Triage Inbox") and **T** in Inbox when nothing is being typed. **Oldest first** (new captures during triage join at the end). Leaving Inbox advances: **M** move (the list picker: type, Enter), **Space** done, **Delete** delete go to the next task at once; **3 / 2 / 1 / 0** priority (high, medium, low, none: Flint's keys everywhere, shown as keycaps on the card), **P** pin and **R** reminder change the card in place; **→** or **Enter** keeps it in Inbox and goes on, **←** goes back; **Ctrl+Z** undoes the last action, its card coming back. At the end a short summary ("Inbox sorted: 7 moved · 3 done · 2 deleted · 4 kept") with Done (Esc too); Esc partway leaves at once. Not in mini mode in v1. **Proposed:** top-level tasks only (subtasks go with their parent); "oldest" by when Flint first saw the task, file order for the rest; **E** (or a click on the title) edits the title in place; the card slides out to the left and the next in from the right (back: the other way), the progress line eases, the summary rises in.
- **Walkthrough (decided, replaces D-61's 5–6 steps):** 8 steps, each advancing by itself when you do the thing (optional), otherwise Next: (1) Welcome: your Flint folder (plain markdown, Obsidian-compatible; Change… with the OneDrive and vault warnings), "skip now, replay any time from Settings" (D-112); (2) capture from anywhere: Ctrl+Shift+Space and Ctrl+Shift+X; (3) shorthand while writing: `!h`, `@15:00`, `##list`, Shift+Enter for notes, pasting screenshots and code; (4) task lists and notes, and quick notes (Ctrl+Shift+J); (5) Today and pins; (6) reminders: Windows notifications (also during Do Not Disturb), missed ones in the Overdue card; (7) the Ctrl+K palette and `?` for every shortcut; (8) mini mode (Ctrl+Alt+Space). Everything else is taught by one-time tips. Triage joins the walkthrough or the tips once built (4a).
- **Existing installs (decided):** a fresh install gets the walkthrough; one that already had settings gets What's new (which mentions Replay walkthrough in Settings).
- **What's new (decided):** one floating card (the spotlight's card, no dim) with the release notes of every version since the last one seen, scrolling when long; Close. Later from the tray and the palette (the latest notes). No rings on the UI.
- **Tips (decided, the first set; each once, at most one per session, counted locally):** priority, list or reminder set with the composer's buttons 3 times → type `!h`, `##list`, `@15:00`; the palette opened with the mouse 3 times → Ctrl+K (D-107); five tasks added with the main window's composer while Flint wasn't already in front → Ctrl+Shift+Space captures from any app; tasks completed by clicking the checkbox 10 times → ↑/↓ and Space; the first time a note is opened → "Type / for headings, code blocks, tables and more"; the first delete → "Deleted items stay in Trash for 30 days, restore them any time"; the first paste of an Azure DevOps, GitHub or Jira link → "Make your own short links in Settings → Links"; Inbox with 20 or more open tasks → triage; the first time the window is wide enough for the side panel → "Select a task to see and edit its details here".

### D-114 — Fixes after 0.8.11 and 0.8.12: mini's Note spacing and scrollbar, focus after mini, Today's Timeline, the sidebar, triage (0.8.13)
- **Date:** 6 Nov 2026 · **Status:** Decided by Davit (his look at 0.8.11 and 0.8.12); built in 0.8.13; details marked **Proposed**
- **Confirmed by Davit:** the side panel's subtask motion and the popup's shadow (D-112); triage as a whole (D-113), the progress line especially.
- **Mini mode:** (1) D-112's 10 px move is reverted: the "Quick note" line sits where it was (2 px lower at most); only "Add a title" and the editor move down a few pixels together (5 px, the gap under the "Quick note" line), the space between them unchanged. (2) No scrollbar and nothing moving sideways when another tab comes in: the body keeps the scrollbar's place (as the sidebar does, D-80), so a long Today or Inbox gaining or losing its scrollbar shifts nothing, and it doesn't scroll while the new tab rises in. (6) Hiding mini (× or the hotkey) gives the focus back to the app that was in front before, also when mini got the focus by a click (it notes the window in front while it is shown); if that window is gone or minimized, the next app window in the z-order gets it; never a minimized window and never Windows' own pick (which could restore the minimized main window). The capture popup hands the focus back the same way.
- **Main window:** (3) Today's Timeline always shows; without reminders today it is a quiet line ("No reminders today."), as Done today and Captured today (D-93); the rail fades in when the first reminder comes. (4) A new list, note or group whose name field is outside the sidebar's visible part scrolls into view, clear of Settings and Trash at the bottom, also when the folded group it goes into opens first. (5) A list or note in a group has **Move to top of group** next to **Move to top** in its right-click menu; both are palette commands for the open list ("Move list to top", "Move list to top of group"). **Proposed:** in a group, **Move to top** now takes it to the top of Lists, out of the group (D-87 had it move to the top of the group, which is now Move to top of group).
- **Triage:** (7) *(reverted by D-115: the link under the title again)* the "Triage · N" link under Inbox's title is now a small pill at the end of the title row (a sort icon, "Triage" and the count), the primary white one from 5 open tasks, the quieter secondary one below that; at 0 it eases away. (8) The card's height eases between tasks (and when the notes unfold); the buttons move: a key's action shows on its button as a press, the priority highlight glides between the four, Pin morphs to Pinned, Back fades when disabled, the actions fold away under the summary and come back with a card. (9) The place is kept for the session: left on task 7 (Esc, the back tile, another list), triage opens on task 7 again; **Start over** (beside the count, and on the summary) goes back to the first task with fresh counts. **Proposed:** reaching the summary and leaving it ends the session's place (the next triage starts at the first task). (10) A click on the card's notes shows the whole task: on a wide window in the side panel (which then follows the card from task to task; *D-115: no, Up next again with each card*), on a narrow one the notes unfold on the card (a click folds them again). **Proposed:** starting triage clears the list's selection, so the panel shows a task only after that click. (11) The title is edited in a field in exactly the title's type and place (it wraps as the title does), so nothing moves when editing starts or ends. (12) The progress line keeps its look; hovering it shows the task at that point ("7 of 18") and a click goes there. (13) Triage comes in and goes out as a page does (sliding in, and back out to Inbox), whichever way it is left.

### D-115 — Fixes after 0.8.13; the walkthrough's spotlight is live where it points (0.8.14)
- **Date:** 6 Nov 2026 · **Status:** Decided by Davit (his look at 0.8.13, and onboarding Q1); built in 0.8.14 (the fixes)
- **Confirmed by Davit on 0.8.13:** D-114's items 1 to 6, 8, 9 and 11 to 13; closing mini with × gives the focus back to the app before. Accepted: finishing triage (the summary, then leaving) clears its place; starting triage clears Inbox's selection; leaving Inbox keeps the place.
- **Triage:** (1) D-114's pill is reverted: the quieter "Triage · N" link under Inbox's title again (D-113). (2) The side panel follows one rule, every time: Up next until the card's notes are clicked; that task while its card is shown; Up next again with every other card and after a click elsewhere (replaces D-114's "the panel then follows the card"). The narrow window's unfolding on the card is unchanged.
- **Sidebar:** in a group, **Move to top of group** (it stays in the group) and **Move out to top of Lists** (it leaves it), so it is clear which is which; outside groups, **Move to top**. The palette's commands say the same ("Move list out to top of Lists" for a list in a group).
- **Mini mode:** (3) menus and pickers open where they fit on the screen, flipping left or up, never cut off: mini's window reaches past the screen's edge beside its card (room to resize into), so main tells the page which part of its window is on the screen, and menus and pickers stay within it (the main window: its own edges, as before).
- **Quick notes (mini and the main window):** (4) in "Add a title", **Enter**, **Shift+Enter**, **Tab** and **↓** save the title and put the caret into the note's text; the title field never makes a new note. Causes found: the focus dropped to the page after the title was saved; giving a title renamed the file, after which "Quick note · 10:00" showed the file's new time (the rename) as if the note were new (now it keeps when Flint first saw it); in the main window the note's editor was rebuilt on a rename (it now stays the same editor while it is the same note); in mini, a title typed first and the first keystroke right after it could each make a note (now one).
- **Walkthrough, Q1 (decided, M8 step 4b):** while a step's spotlight is up, only the ringed target is live (typing in the composer, clicking the pin); everything else under the dim ignores clicks. The keys the walkthrough teaches work everywhere (Ctrl+Shift+Space, Ctrl+K, `?`, Ctrl+Alt+Space). The dim lifts while the capture popup or mini mode is open and the step goes on when you are back (*D-122: no, the dim and the card stay*). **Esc** ends the walkthrough (as Skip all) with a short toast: "Replay it any time from Settings". The card never takes the keyboard.

### D-116 — Settings work right after an update; the walkthrough's steps; Import from Obsidian recorded (0.8.15)
- **Date:** 6 Nov 2026 · **Status:** Decided by Davit (his look at 0.8.14, and onboarding Q2); built in 0.8.15 (the fix)
- **Confirmed by Davit on 0.8.14:** all of D-115's fixes, and the labels Move to top of group / Move out to top of Lists.
- **Bug: link patterns off after an update until saved again in Settings.** Not the stored patterns (the format hasn't changed since M5 and the tolerant reader keeps them all) but the start: main began loading the main window's page, then read the Flint folder, and only then registered what answers the page's calls. On the first start after an update the folder is read from a cold disk, so the page asked for its link patterns first; the call failed unseen ("no handler") and the page went on without them. Saving in Settings set them again. The same race could lose the built-in links switch and the animation speed. **Fix:** the pages' calls to main (main window and mini) wait until main has set everything up: main answers "started?" from before the page loads and says so once every handler is registered; calls keep their order. The capture popup is unaffected (its handlers come before its page). Test: `e2e/startup.spec.ts` starts Flint on a `settings.json` an older version saved, with main's start held 3 s (`FLINT_E2E_SLOW_START`, development builds only); it failed before the fix.
- **Walkthrough, Q2 (decided, M8 step 4b):** where each step's ring points and what moves it on (otherwise Next):

  | # | Step | Ring on | Moves on when |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | 1 | Welcome, the Flint folder | nothing (a centred card: the folder's path, Change…) | Next |
  | 2 | Capture from anywhere | nothing (the card shows Ctrl+Shift+Space and Ctrl+Shift+X) | a capture is saved from the popup |
  | 3 | Shorthand while writing | Inbox's composer | a task is added with typed `!h`, `@time` or `##list` |
  | 4 | Lists, notes, quick notes | the sidebar's + and the Quick note row | a list, note or quick note is made |
  | 5 | Today and pins | the first task's pin (Today in the sidebar when Inbox is empty) | a task is pinned |
  | 6 | Reminders | the composer's reminder button | a reminder is set |
  | 7 | Ctrl+K and `?` | the palette button in the sidebar | the palette opens (key or click) |
  | 8 | Mini mode | the mini-mode button in the main window's title bar (the card shows Ctrl+Alt+Space) | mini opens |

  It runs in the main window, on Inbox for steps 3 to 6; no sample content (D-64). After step 8 a **You're set** card with **Done** and **Remove what I created during the tour**: the tasks, lists, notes and quick notes made while the walkthrough ran go to Trash as **one undoable step** (Ctrl+Z or the toast's Undo brings them all back). Without that click everything stays as real data. **Proposed** (*decided by D-117*): only what was made during the tour (by ID), not edits to what was already there; a capture saved in step 2 counts.
- **Recorded for late v1 (low priority; to be grilled then): DATA-08, Import from Obsidian.** The first-run welcome offers it (skippable: "you can import later from Settings → Data"): pick a vault, and Flint copies all its notes into the Flint folder as plain notes (no checklist-to-task conversion: notes often mix text and checklists). It never moves or changes the vault. Notes are copied flat (a duplicate name gets " (2)"); turning the vault's top-level folders into sidebar groups is to be considered. Embedded images (`![[image.png]]`) are copied into `attachments/` and the embeds rewritten so they still show. Not to be confused with putting the Flint folder inside a vault, which already works (D-23).

### D-117 — Fresh install or update; What's new in plain language (onboarding Q3)
- **Date:** 6 Nov 2026 · **Status:** Decided by Davit (onboarding Q3; the tour-removal detail); to be built in M8 step 4b
- **Confirmed on 0.8.15:** settings saved by an older version work right after an update (D-116).
- **Remove what I created during the tour (decided, was Proposed in D-116):** only what was made while the tour ran (by ID), including a capture saved in step 2; edits to what already existed are kept.
- **Fresh install or update (decided):** a fresh install is one with no `settings.json` in app data; its first start runs the walkthrough. An existing install gets no walkthrough: on the first start of a new version it shows **What's new** once in the main window (when Flint started hidden in the tray, the next time the window opens). Settings keep `lastSeenVersion`; several versions skipped show every one since, newest first, scrolling when long. Esc or × closes it. Later from the palette ("What's new") and **Settings → About**; **no tray item** (the tray stays at five items; replaces D-113's "from the tray").
