- **What it shows (decided):** not `docs/release-notes.md` (the detailed developer log stays as it is) but a separate, short, user-facing **`docs/whats-new.md`**: per version 2–5 highlights in plain language, what's new and how to use it ("Mini mode for calls: Ctrl+Alt+Space"), no decision numbers, tests or files. Written with every version bump (CLAUDE.md); the app bundles it.
- **Proposed** (*decided by D-118*): an existing install without `lastSeenVersion` (every install before What's new ships) is shown only the current version's entry, and that first entry sums up the 0.8 line (mini mode, triage, Settings, the palette); a build with only small fixes may have a single line.

### D-118 — When a tip shows and how long it stays; Reset tips (onboarding Q4)
- **Date:** 6 Nov 2026 · **Status:** Decided by Davit (onboarding Q4; D-117's Proposed details); to be built in M8 step 4b
- **D-117's Proposed details (decided):** an install from before What's new sees only the current entry, which sums up the 0.8 line; a build with only small fixes may have a single line.
- **When (decided):** once its pattern has happened, a tip waits for 2 s without typing, with no menu, picker or dialog open. Never while the walkthrough, triage or What's new is up, and not in the session the walkthrough ran.
- **Where (decided):** the bottom right of the main window, above where toasts appear, fading in (03 §5). Never in mini mode or the capture popup; never takes focus.
- **How long (decided):** it stays until closed (× or **Got it**) or the window is hidden; no timeout. It **counts as shown once it has been visible for about 3 s, or was closed**; hidden sooner, it can show again later.
- **Don't show tips (decided):** on the card and in Settings. **Reset tips** sits next to **Replay walkthrough** in Settings and makes every tip eligible again (testing, or anyone who wants the hints again); both are palette commands too (D-106). **Proposed** (*decided by D-119*): Reset tips also resets the earlier counted hints in `tips.json` (the empty quick note's, D-107) and turns Don't show tips off.

### D-119 — A restart during the walkthrough (onboarding Q5)
- **Date:** 6 Nov 2026 · **Status:** Decided by Davit (onboarding Q5; D-118's Proposed detail); to be built in M8 step 4b
- **D-118's Proposed detail (decided):** Reset tips also clears the earlier counted hints in `tips.json` (the empty quick note's) and turns Don't show tips off.
- **Changing the folder in step 1 (decided):** step 1's Change… is Settings' flow (D-104); on a fresh install the folder holds only an empty Inbox, so it offers Move my lists here or Start fresh. Flint restarts on the new folder and the walkthrough **goes on at step 2** (not back on Settings). The step and the IDs of what the tour made are kept in the settings, so You're set's Remove what I created during the tour still works.
- **Any other quit or crash during the tour (decided):** the next start does not resume it (a dim on a later day, out of context); it counts as skipped. On that first start a quiet one-time toast: **"The walkthrough was interrupted · Replay"**; Replay starts it from step 1. What the interrupted tour made stays as real data.

### D-120 — The walkthrough's words (onboarding Q6)
- **Date:** 6 Nov 2026 · **Status:** Decided by Davit (onboarding Q6); to be built in M8 step 4b
- **Each card:** a title, one or two lines, a "3 / 8" counter in mono, **Next** (primary) / **Skip** / **Skip all** (quiet). A step with a trigger moves on by itself when it is done and never says "click Next"; Next stays for anyone who would rather not try. Second person, plain, no exclamation marks.

  | # | Title | Text |
  |---|---|---|
  | 1 | Welcome to Flint | Your lists and notes are plain markdown files in `C:\Users\…\Flint`, readable in Obsidian or any editor. Nothing leaves your computer. **Change…** · *Skip now, replay any time from Settings.* |
  | 2 | Capture from anywhere | In any app, press **Ctrl+Shift+Space**, type, Enter: it lands in Inbox. **Ctrl+Shift+X** captures the text you've selected. Try it now. |
  | 3 | Shorthand as you type | Add a task here: `!h` sets priority, `@15:00` a reminder, `##Work` a list. Shift+Enter adds notes; paste screenshots and code straight in. |
  | 4 | Lists, notes, quick notes | **+** makes a task list, a note or a group. **Ctrl+Shift+J** opens a quick note for a thought that isn't a task yet. |
  | 5 | Today | Pin a task and it shows on Today, whatever list it's in. Click its pin, or press **P**. |
  | 6 | Reminders | Give a task a time (type `@15:00` or use the bell) and Windows reminds you, by default even during Do Not Disturb. Missed ones wait in Today's Overdue card. |
  | 7 | Everything, one key away | **Ctrl+K** finds any list, task or command. Press **?** (outside a text field) to see every shortcut. |
  | 8 | Mini mode | A small Flint that stays on top, for calls and meetings, and is hidden when you share your screen: this button or **Ctrl+Alt+Space**. |
  | ✓ | You're set | Replay this tour any time from Settings. Flint will point out a few more things as you go; you can turn tips off in Settings. **Done** · *Remove what I created during the tour* |

- Step 1's path is the real Flint folder. The claims were checked against the code: reminders use Windows' reminder scenario, shown during Do Not Disturb with Windows' default priority settings (REM-06); the composer's reminder button is the bell; mini is hidden from screen sharing by default (`miniHideFromSharing`, D-109).

### D-121 — The walkthrough, the spotlight and What's new built (0.8.16)
- **Date:** 6 Nov 2026 · **Status:** Built in 0.8.16 (M8 step 4b, first part; the tips follow in 0.8.17); details marked **Proposed** are open for Davit
- **Built as decided (D-113, D-115 to D-120):** the spotlight (the dim with the step's targets cut out and ringed; only they take clicks; the card beside them never takes the keyboard; the dim and rings lift while the capture popup or mini mode is open, the card stays); the 8 steps with their words, rings and triggers; You're set with Remove what I created during the tour (one undo step, toast with Undo); Esc and Skip all end it with "Replay it any time from Settings"; a fresh install runs it once the window shows; step 1's Change… (Settings' folder flow) goes on at step 2 after the restart; a tour cut short says so once ("The walkthrough was interrupted · Replay"); What's new once after an update, from `docs/whats-new.md`; **Replay walkthrough** (Settings → General, the palette) and **What's new** (Settings → About, the palette).
- **How "what I created" is known (Proposed):** when the tour starts, Flint notes every list, note, quick note and task that exists (their IDs). Remove what I created takes what exists now and wasn't there then, so every way of making something counts (the popup, the composer, the sidebar's +, quick notes, paste, duplicate) and nothing that was already there is touched. A new task in a new list goes with its list.
- **Where the tour's progress lives (Proposed):** `tour.json` in app data (not `settings.json`; D-119 said "the settings"): the step, the restart flag and those IDs; gone once the tour ends. `lastSeenVersion` is in `settings.json`. A fresh install is one with no `settings.json` when Flint starts.
- **The card's buttons (Proposed):** **Next** and **Skip all**; no separate Skip, which in the walkthrough did the same as Next (09 §8's Next / Skip / Skip all stays for the tips, if they need it).
- **Step 5 (Proposed, a question for Davit):** a task that isn't pinned has no pin in its row (the pin shows only on pinned tasks, D-86), so the ring is on the first open task in Inbox (Today in the sidebar when there is none) and the card says "Select it and press **P**, or right-click it and choose Pin." instead of D-120's "Click its pin, or press P."
- **Esc (Proposed):** a menu, a picker or the palette takes Esc first (closing itself); Esc with none of them open ends the walkthrough or closes What's new.
- **What's new from the palette or Settings:** every entry up to this version, newest first, scrolling.
- **Tests:** `e2e/onboarding.spec.ts` (4: the whole walkthrough with each trigger, the dim taking clicks, the popup lifting the dim, Remove and Ctrl+Z; Esc, a cut-short tour and Replay; going on after the restart; What's new, the palette and Settings). Onboarding is off in every other end-to-end suite (each starts as a fresh install); `FLINT_E2E_ONBOARDING=1` turns it on (development builds only). Unit: `whats-new.test.ts`, `onboarding.test.ts`, `lastSeenVersion`.

### D-122 — Fixes after 0.8.16: the hover pin, the walkthrough's dim stays, a real step 4, What's new readable; mini's Open a note… (0.8.17)
- **Date:** 7 Nov 2026 · **Status:** Decided by Davit (his look at 0.8.16); built in 0.8.17
- **D-121's Proposed points (decided):** what the tour made is what didn't exist when it started (by ID); its progress in `tour.json`; the card's buttons Next and Skip all; a menu, picker or the palette takes Esc first.
- **The hover pin (TODAY-01, new):** an open task's row shows a faint pin on hover, in the place where a pinned task's pin shows (its place kept, so nothing moves); a click pins it and the pin settles in with a pop. Main-window rows (mini's rows keep P and the menu). The walkthrough's step 5 rings that pin on the first open task (shown on every open row while the step is up): "Pin a task and it shows on Today, whatever list it's in. Click its pin, or select it and press **P**." (replaces D-121's Proposed text).
- **What's new (1):** a proper reading size (620 px wide, up to about two thirds of the window high, scrolling when long), with **Got it** at the bottom; Esc and × still close it.
- **Settings (2):** **Replay walkthrough** moves to Settings → About, next to What's new; Reset tips goes there too (0.8.18).
- **The dim stays (3, changes D-115):** while the capture popup or mini mode is open, the walkthrough's dim and its card stay in place (they float above); the step still moves on when the capture is saved.
- **Step 4 only for something real (4):** a named list, note or group, a quick note given a title, or a quick note with text in it; an empty quick note doesn't count and stays open to be typed in (the note and the sidebar's name field are live under the dim, not ringed). When it is the text being typed that counts, the step moves on once the typing pauses (1.5 s), so a note isn't left mid-sentence.
- **Mini mode (5):** the Note tab's ⋯ menu has **Open a note…**: a picker with a search field and the notes (quick notes and notes), newest first (when made), five in view and the rest scrolling. The chosen note opens in mini's Note tab (one window edits a note at a time, as always); a note that isn't a quick note shows "Note · <when made>" with its name as the title, and Keep as a note is off for it.
- **Found while building:** the tour's progress could be written twice at once (its start and the first Next), leaving the older one on disk; writes now go one at a time.
- **Versions:** the fixes are 0.8.17; the tips (D-118) follow in 0.8.18.

### D-123 — The tips built (0.8.18)
- **Date:** 7 Nov 2026 · **Status:** Built in 0.8.18 (M8 step 4b, last part); the words are **Proposed**
- **As decided (D-113, D-118, D-119):** the nine tips, each once, at most one a session, none in a session the walkthrough ran (or started), none with Don't show tips. A tip waits 2 s after its pattern, with no typing for 2 s and no menu, picker, palette or dialog open, and never while the walkthrough, triage or What's new is up; it shows bottom right (above the toasts), never taking the focus; it stays until × or **Got it** or the window is hidden. Seen after about 3 s on screen, or when closed; hidden sooner, it comes back the next time it can. **Don't show tips** on the card; Settings → About has **Tips** (a switch, Show tips) with **Reset tips** next to Replay walkthrough; Reset tips is a palette command and clears the earlier counted hints too.
- **The patterns (as D-113):** the composer's priority, list or reminder button used on a task added (3); the palette opened with its button (3); a task added within a minute of bringing Flint to the front from another app (5, once each time); a task completed with its checkbox (10); a note opened (1); a delete of a task, tasks, a list or lists (1); a paste with an Azure DevOps, GitHub or Jira link (1); Inbox with 20 or more open tasks (1); the window wide enough for the side panel (1). Counted in app data's `tips.json` (main window only; mini's and the popup's actions don't count).
- **The words (Proposed):** "Type `!h`, `##list` or `@15:00` as you write a task: no buttons needed." · "Press `Ctrl+K` to search or run any command, from anywhere in Flint." · "`Ctrl+Shift+Space` captures a task from any app, without opening Flint." · "`↑` `↓` move between tasks and `Space` completes one." · "Type `/` in a note for headings, code blocks, tables and more." · "Deleted items stay in Trash for 30 days: restore them any time." · "Make your own short links in Settings → Links." · "Press `T` in Inbox to sort it one task at a time." · "Select a task to see and edit its details here." (keys as keycaps).
- **How a hidden window is known:** main tells the page when the main window is shown or hidden (its page can't tell by itself here).
- **Tests:** `e2e/onboarding.spec.ts` (the Ctrl+K tip after a quiet moment, never focused, seen after 3 s; one a session; Reset tips; the delete's tip hidden with the window and back; Don't show tips); unit `tips.test.ts`. Tips are off in the other end-to-end suites, as onboarding is.

### D-124 — Pickers: Enter takes what was typed for, under a resting pointer too (0.8.19)
- **Date:** 7 Nov 2026 · **Status:** Built in 0.8.19 (found by CI on 0.8.17)
- **The bug:** a picker's highlighted row was dropped when typing narrowed the rows under a resting mouse pointer (Chromium's synthetic "pointer left", with no movement), so Enter did nothing: on CI, mini's Open a note… stayed on the note before. Every picker with a search (lists, notes) had it.
- **Fix (UI-08):** only a pointer that really moves away takes the highlight off; and with something typed in the search, Enter takes the highlighted match, the first when none shows. The end-to-end test now checks a row is highlighted before Enter.

### D-125 — What's new dimmed; the walkthrough's card never covers where you type; tips two a session (0.8.20)

A pattern that happens inside the ten minutes is still counted; its tip comes the next time the pattern happens after them. Reset tips also clears this session's count.

### D-127 — Clipboard history: read through koffi, guarded against crashes, proved before the section (M8 step 5, 0.8.22)
- **Date:** 7 Nov 2026 · **Status:** Decided by Davit (step 5's grill, Q1); the check built in 0.8.22, the section waits for it to work on his machine
- **The bridge (NAV-08, resolves "chosen under D-30 when built"):** Windows' clipboard history is read through its WinRT interfaces with **koffi**, already a dependency (`main/win32.ts`): no compiled add-on, no C++ tools in CI, nothing to rebuild per Electron version, no new dependency. Considered and not selected: a compiled add-on (C++/WinRT or Rust: toolchain in CI, rebuilt per Electron), NodeRT (unmaintained), a PowerShell or .NET helper (another process, which Windows may refuse as not in front).
- **Scope of the first build:** **text items only** (pictures later); **read-only**: Flint never writes, clears or deletes anything in the history and never records the clipboard itself; Windows' clipboard sequence number (`GetClipboardSequenceNumber`, a cheap check) decides when to read again, so the list reloads only when something new was copied.
- **The calls (`main/clipboard-history.ts`):** `RoGetActivationFactory` for `Windows.ApplicationModel.DataTransfer.Clipboard` (`IClipboardStatics2`), `GetHistoryItemsAsync`, the result's status and items, each item's timestamp and content, `Contains("Text")` and `GetTextAsync`; asynchronous operations are polled through `IAsyncInfo` (the main thread stays free) with a 3 s limit. The method positions were read from Windows' own metadata (`C:\Windows\System32\WinMetadata`, 7 Nov 2026) and are written down in the module; only the reads are ever called (`ClearHistory`, `DeleteItemFromHistory` and `SetHistoryItemAsContent` sit next to them and never are). Every result is checked and every object released.
- **Crash safety (Davit):** direct calls can take the main process down instead of failing, so a marker file (`clipboard-reading` in app data) is written before every read and removed after it. Found at a start, the last read never finished: reads stay off, the section says why, and only a deliberate **Retry** reads again, so it can never cause a crash loop. A quit in the middle of a read removes the marker (not a crash). A hang inside a call can't be guarded this way; the 3 s limit covers waiting on Windows.
- **Proved first (Davit):** before the section's UI, a check proves the reading works in the installed Flint (NSIS, not a Windows package): Ctrl+K → **Check clipboard history** reads it once and says how it went in a toast (how many items, how many text, how long, the start of the newest text), or that history is off, that Windows refused, that the last read never finished (with Retry), or that it couldn't be read (the log says why). The section is built only once this works on Davit's machine. **Found:** from a console process Windows answered too (five text items in 115 ms), so its "only in the foreground" rule seems looser than documented for desktop apps; the section still reads only while the window is active.
- **Tests:** they never read the real history (D-93): main's test hook says what it holds. `e2e/clipboard.spec.ts` (the check's toasts; a marker left by a crash keeps reading off until Retry); unit `clipboard-guard.test.ts` (the marker during a read and after it, after a failure, a crash and Retry, a quit) and `clipboard-history.test.ts` (the binding loads and asks whether history is on, a setting only).

### D-128 — The Clipboard section and Paste from clipboard history… (M8 step 5, 0.8.23)
- **Date:** 7 Nov 2026 · **Status:** Decided by Davit (step 5's grill, Q2 to Q8); built in 0.8.23
- **D-127's check on Davit's machine:** the installed 0.8.22 read 25 items (22 text) in 289 ms; the newest matched what he had copied last. The section was built on it. **His rule:** a read never blocks the UI: it runs in the background, the last list stays while it runs, and it runs again only when the clipboard's sequence number changed. **Built:** the items' texts are read all at once (about 40 ms for 25 items here, was 289 ms one by one); the main thread is busy at most about 5–7 ms at a time during a read (measured with `setImmediate`, Windows' 15.6 ms timer tick hides less), and the window's page, a separate process, never waits on it.
- **Where (Q2):** docked at the foot of the side panel in every state (Up next, a task's details, a note's or page's outline), staying in place while the panel's content scrolls behind it (`position: sticky` inside the panel card). Folded (by default): the header "Clipboard · 22" and the newest **3** items, one line each (the first line with text, trimmed) with a faint age; open: every item, scrolling, up to about 45 % of the panel; open or folded is remembered (this machine). Text items only (pictures and files aren't listed; the count is text items). No panel (a narrow window, the panel hidden): no section.
- **Screen sharing (Q3):** at rest a row is a **placeholder bar** sized to its text plus its kind ("Text", "Link": one URL, "Code": as pasting into the capture popup judges it, CAP-06) and its age; none of the text is drawn, nor put in the page. The text shows **after the pointer rests on the section about 300 ms** (Davit: a pointer passing across shows nothing) and goes the moment it leaves. **Always show contents** (the header's eye, Settings, the palette's Show / Hide clipboard contents) shows it at rest; turned on, a one-time note: "Clipboard contents will be visible when you share your screen". The count and the ages stay visible.
- **A click (Q4):** nothing in the section takes the focus; the item goes into the field that has the keyboard **as a paste**: a synthetic paste event (D-93: the clipboard is never touched), so the field's own paste rules apply (the note editor's, the composer's plain-text paste, D-45) and Ctrl+Z undoes it in one step; a field that doesn't handle pastes gets the text typed (line breaks as spaces in a one-line field). No field: the toast "Click where it should go, then pick it again". A brief press flash. **Right-click (Davit):** Insert, Capture as task, Copy.
- **Copy (Davit):** the item becomes the clipboard's content again, like picking it in Win+V: the only time Flint writes the clipboard, and only on that choice. **Proposed:** written with Electron's clipboard (text), not Windows' history call (the bridge stays read-only); whether Win+V then lists it twice is for Davit's look on his machine.
- **Capture as task (Q5):** the capture popup, prefilled exactly as by Ctrl+Shift+X (D-39: a closed popup's draft is set aside as the previous draft; D-45: one line becomes the editable task line, its `!`, `##` and `@` plain text; code to the selection area). From the menu only.
- **The keyboard (Q6):** **Paste from clipboard history…** (the palette; **Ctrl+Shift+H** in the main window, in Settings → Keyboard shortcuts and the cheat sheet): a picker with a search field and the items' **text** (it was opened on purpose), newest first, five in view. **Enter** inserts into the field that had the keyboard before (it gets it back first), **Shift+Enter** captures as a task, **Ctrl+C** copies (Davit; the search field's selected text instead when there is one, as any field), shown in its footer. It works without the panel. Main window only. The palette also has **Show clipboard contents** and **Hide clipboard contents**.
- **States (Q7):** off in Windows: "Clipboard history is off in Windows. Turn it on to see what you copy here." with **Open clipboard settings** (`ms-settings:clipboard`; Flint never changes it); turned off by the "Allow Clipboard History" group policy (registry, read through koffi): "Clipboard history is turned off on this PC, possibly by your organisation." (no button); the last read never finished (D-127's marker): "Reading clipboard history stopped Flint last time, so it's paused." with **Try again**; couldn't be read: "Clipboard history couldn't be read." with **Try again** (the last items stay; the reason in the log); Windows refused: nothing changes; no text yet: "Copy some text and it shows here." Notes only in the open section; folded, the header says a quiet word ("off", "paused", "unavailable") instead of the count. The picker shows the same note.
- **Settings (Q8):** a new section **Clipboard** between Links and Data: **Clipboard history** (on by default; off: no calls to Windows' clipboard history at all, no section, and the picker says "Clipboard history is off in Flint" with **Turn on**) and **Always show contents** (off by default). Found by "clipboard", "Win+V", "paste".
- **Proposed details:** ages "now", "4m", "2h" (today), "yesterday", then "7 Nov"; motion (D-89, off under reduce-motion): the fold eases its height, the text fades in over the bars, a new item slides in at the top, a click flashes its row; the section asks main when it appears, when the window comes to the front and every second while it is on screen (main reads only on a new sequence number, and looks at "off", "policy" or an error again at most every 5 s), and only the section renders on a poll (never the whole window); the check command of 0.8.22 (Check clipboard history) is gone: the section and the picker show every state.
- **Found:** Electron 45's `clipboard.writeText` returns a promise (awaited).
- **Tests:** `e2e/clipboard.spec.ts` (bars at rest and no text in the page; a pointer passing shows nothing, resting shows the text, leaving hides it; open and folded; Always show contents and its one-time note; a click into the composer and the note editor with one undo, a two-line item in a one-line field, no field; Copy; Capture as task; the picker's Enter, Ctrl+C, Shift+Enter and the palette; paused and Try again, off with Open clipboard settings, the policy, off in Flint with Turn on); `smoke.spec.ts` (Settings has 11 sections); unit `shared/clipboard.test.ts` (preview, ages, bars).

### D-129 — The Clipboard section after 0.8.23: pictures, motion, Delete and Clear all, Copy as Win+V's pick (0.8.24)
- **Date:** 7 Nov 2026 · **Status:** Decided by Davit (his look at 0.8.23); built in 0.8.24
- **D-128's Proposed points (decided):** the ages, the 460 px picker, the once-a-second check, no content in the page while hidden.
- **Pictures (1):** picture items show as thumbnails, as in Win+V; at rest a picture is a blank tile with "Image" and its age (D-128's screen-sharing rule), its thumbnail only while the pointer rests on the section or with the eye. A click inserts it as a pasted picture (a note or the composer saves it to attachments/, as pasting does; a field that takes no picture says "A picture goes into a note or the composer"); Capture as task puts it in the capture popup as an attachment chip; the count includes pictures. The picker shows "Picture" with its thumbnail.
- **Motion (2, D-89):** items arriving slide in at the top; items going (deleted, cleared, fallen off the history) fold away where they were; with Always show contents off, the content crossfades with the placeholders both ways (it stays drawn while it fades out, then leaves the page).
- **Delete (3):** right-click → **Delete (also from Win+V)** removes it from Windows' history too (Windows' own `DeleteItemFromHistory`); the row folds away and a toast says "Deleted, also from Win+V (pinned or not). Undo adds it back as the newest" with **Undo**: Windows can't put an item back in place, so Undo adds it back as the newest item (text, or the picture as PNG). What Undo needs is kept in main's memory only while it is offered (**Proposed:** 10 s, the 5 s toast and a margin), never on disk. **Clear all** (left of the eye) asks inline, with no undo: "Clear clipboard history? This also clears Win+V and can't be undone. Pinned items in Win+V are kept." (Windows' `ClearHistory`; Microsoft Learn: it "doesn't clear pinned items from the clipboard history"). These are the bridge's only writes besides Copy, each on an explicit action, behind D-127's crash marker.
- **Win+V's pins (4), checked:** Windows' API doesn't report them: `ClipboardHistoryItem` has only its id, timestamp and content, nothing documents a pinned flag, and the formats of the 25 items on Davit's machine carry no marker. So Flint shows no pin marker and has no clipboard pins of its own. **Delete removes a pinned item too** (as Win+V's own Delete does), which Flint can't tell beforehand: the menu says "(also from Win+V)" and the toast "pinned or not".
- **Copy (5):** Windows' `SetHistoryItemAsContent`, the call Win+V's pick uses: the item becomes the clipboard's content again with no new entry (0.8.23's text write added a duplicate). An item no longer in the history says so.
- **Settings (6, 7):** Always show contents leaves Settings (the section's eye and its palette commands are the controls); Clipboard history's description: "Shows Windows' clipboard history (Win+V must be on) in the side panel and in Paste from clipboard history… (Ctrl+Shift+H). Windows only lets Flint read it while Flint's window is in front, so new copies appear when you switch back to Flint. Flint never stores or sends it."
- **Screen sharing (Davit):** Windows hides whole windows from a share, not parts of one, so the reveal on a resting pointer stays.
- **Found:** Windows hands pictures over as BMP files (32-bit, uncompressed on Davit's machine), which Electron's `nativeImage` can't open: `shared/bmp.ts` reads them (24 and 32 bits, bit masks, either row order; an all-zero alpha counts as opaque) and shrinks them for thumbnails (averaging); a 225 × 403 picture: 49 ms to read from Windows, 3.6 ms to decode, 3 ms to shrink. The picture is read through `GetBitmapAsync`, `OpenReadAsync` and `shcore`'s `CreateStreamOverRandomAccessStream` (positions from Windows' metadata). Electron 45's clipboard has no `writeImage`: Undo writes a picture with `clipboard.write` and a PNG `ClipboardItem`.
- **Proposed details:** thumbnails made once per item (at most 400 × 112, shown at up to 200 × 56) and dropped when the item leaves the history; picture rows 64 px high; folded, the section is as high as its first three rows.
- **Tests:** `e2e/clipboard.spec.ts` (pictures: a tile at rest, the thumbnail revealed, inserted into a note as an attachment, captured as a chip; the text fading out before it leaves the page; Delete with the row folding away, its toast and Undo; Clear all's question and the empty section; Copy as Win+V's pick; Settings without Always show contents); unit `shared/bmp.test.ts`.

### D-130 — A place's scroll position on a slow machine; nothing scrolls under the Clipboard section (0.8.25)
- **Date:** 7 Nov 2026 · **Status:** Built in 0.8.25 (found by CI)
- **Scroll memory (NAV-04, D-91):** a place shown again was scrolled to where it was left once, as its content arrived; on a slow machine its rows were still being drawn, the page wasn't tall enough yet, and it landed at the top. Now, when the position can't be reached yet, it follows the page as it grows, until it gets there, 1.5 s pass, another place is shown, or the user scrolls, types or clicks (**Proposed** limits).
- **The docked Clipboard section (NAV-08, D-128):** what the side panel scrolls into view (an outline entry, a subtask, a test's click) could land under the section docked at its foot; the panel now keeps the section's height as its scroll padding.
- **Attachments' temporary folder (MEDIA-01, 0.8.26, found by CI on 0.8.24):** a pasted picture (and a snapshot restoring attachments) was written through a temporary folder made inside `attachments/` (`attachments/.flint/tmp`), left there afterwards; such writes now use the Flint folder's own `.flint/tmp`, as notes do. An empty `attachments/.flint` left by earlier versions is harmless (Flint doesn't remove it). The end-to-end test now counts the pictures in `attachments/` and checks no `.flint` folder is there (it had passed by catching the moment the folder existed before the picture).

### D-131 — After 0.8.24–0.8.26: the highlight in a group, an empty quick note gone back to; density and Import from Obsidian later (0.8.27)
- **Date:** 7 Nov 2026 · **Status:** Decided by Davit (his look at 0.8.24–0.8.26); fixes built in 0.8.27
- **Accepted:** the seven clipboard points of D-129 as built (no pin markers: Windows doesn't expose them); D-129's Proposed 10 s Undo memory for a deleted item.
- **The sidebar highlight (UI-04, bug 1):** after deleting a quick note, the list opened instead had its highlight a little off its row when that list sat in a group. A group card slides into its new place by its own `top` offset (D-102), and the highlight measured the row mid-slide and stayed there. It now measures where the row will rest (a group card's sliding offset left out), so it lands exactly on the row. Test: `e2e/sidebar.spec.ts` (it failed by 29 px without the fix).
- **An empty quick note gone back to (NOTE-08, D-99, bug 2):** leaving an empty quick note starts its removal 2 s later; going back to it didn't call that off, so it went while it was open. Now opening a quick note (in the main window or in mini) calls off a removal waiting for it, and each leave starts the 2 s anew (a second leave doesn't inherit the first one's moment); a note a window is editing then is never removed either. Test: `e2e/sidebar.spec.ts` (it failed without the fix).
- **Density (Q1):** moved to **Later** (comfortable / compact, APP-11): Flint keeps its one tuned density for v1; mini mode is the compact size.
- **Import from Obsidian (DATA-08):** moved to **Later (after v1)**. Recorded for then: the user chooses to import at the top level of the sidebar or into a group (named "Obsidian" by default, renamable); the vault's folders and subfolders need a decision then (Flint's groups are one level only: flatten them, or consider sub-groups at that time).

### D-132 — "Your notes never leave your machine" in Settings; after v1, GitHub; 1.0.0 kept for that (0.8.28)
- **Date:** 7 Nov 2026 · **Status:** Decided by Davit (Q2 of the rest of M8, and the plan after v1); the statement built in 0.8.28
- **D-131's fixes confirmed:** the sidebar highlight after deleting a quick note in a group, and an empty quick note's removal called off on return.
- **The statement (APP-09, D-16):** a **Privacy** row first in Settings → About: "Your notes never leave your machine. Flint sends no telemetry, needs no account and makes no network requests of its own." Found by "privacy", "telemetry", "network", "data", "account", "offline". The version row below it keeps only the version (it said much the same before). The walkthrough's step 1 keeps its words (D-120). No palette command (a statement, not an action). **The installer doesn't state it in v1:** it is a one-click installer (D-19) with no page to put it on, and adding one only for this would slow every install; revisited if the installer gets a page. **If auto-update comes after v1, this sentence is updated** (Flint would then make requests of its own: checking for and downloading updates).
- **After v1 (plan, not built now; 06 §5):** once v1 is stable, the repository moves to Davit's personal GitHub (DavitKhachaturovi). Distribution then moves to GitHub Releases, likely with auto-update (electron-updater). Before the repository goes public, private references are removed from the docs and the code. **Brought forward by D-135.** Code signing later through the SignPath Foundation (if open source) or a Certum certificate. **Nothing in v1 may block this.**
- **Versioning (changes D-101):** no build is numbered 1.0.0 until that move. M9's builds stay **0.9.x**; **1.0.0 is reserved for the first release on GitHub Releases**. "v1" in the docs stays the name of the feature scope.
- **The docs tidy:** the checklist's older M8 rows that later sections record as built now say Done, with where they were built.

### D-133 — The motion pass: a census of every action, kept as a test (UI-10, 0.8.29)
- **Date:** 7 Nov 2026 · **Status:** Decided by Davit (Q3 of the rest of M8); built in 0.8.29
- **D-132 confirmed:** the Privacy row as built; the plan after v1 and the versioning recorded.
- **The census (`e2e/motion.spec.ts`):** its own Flint drives every kind of action step by step (something appearing, going, changing state): moving between lists, notes, Today, Settings, Trash, Snapshots and triage; selecting, completing, pinning, prioritising, editing, deleting and undoing tasks; the composer and its list menu; every right-click menu, picker and overlay (palette, cheat sheet, find, What's new, lightbox); the sidebar (name field, group fold, quick note); the side panel (hide, show, extra sections); Settings (switch, index, link pattern editor); Trash (the inline question, Restore); mini mode's tabs; the capture popup's chips and fade. For each it records what animated (CSS animations and transitions, animations started from script, smooth scrolls; a colour change on what the pointer is over is its hover, not counted) and checks that the action happened. A step that should move and didn't fails the test, so a new action without motion is caught. The table goes to `apps/desktop/test-results/motion-census.md`.
- **Sorted as Davit asked:** (a) clearly needs motion per D-89, fixed without asking; (b) instant by a documented decision, listed; (c) unclear, for him to decide. **(c) came out empty.**
- **(a), fixed:** a task pinned with P, the menu or the panel's Pin chip now settles in with the pop the faint pin had (D-122); the note's slash menu fades in, and a copy of it fades out where it was when it closes (CodeMirror removes it at once); Settings' link pattern editor and the Flint folder panel fade out on Cancel or Save, as a copy (as a menu's does), so the save itself isn't delayed (D-75); Trash's buttons fade back when its inline question is cancelled (the link patterns' Delete it? too); a Trash day or Archived list whose last item goes folds away, and "Trash is empty." fades in.
- **(b), instant by decision:** everything under reduce-motion (D-75, 03 §5); typed text and the caret (keypress → screen in one frame, 05 §4); the capture popup's first frame (drawn at once on the hotkey, 05 §4; its fade never delays typing, D-111); opening a list (the page fades in, its rows don't each animate, UI-04).
- **Not reached by the census:** dragging rows and lists, the snapshot comparison, the rollover and overdue cards, the reminder alert in mini, a hotkey being recorded in Settings; each was given its motion when built (D-89 since 0.4.x) and has its own checks in `smoke.spec.ts`.

### D-134 — A list opened while another program was still writing it (0.8.30)
- **Date:** 7 Nov 2026 · **Status:** Built in 0.8.30 (found by CI on 0.8.28)
- **The bug (DATA-06):** another program saving a list in two steps (the file first, empty, then its text) makes Flint see an empty file for a moment; with no `id` in it yet, the list goes by its file name (`file:<name>`). Opened then, it stayed on that name: once the text and its `id` arrived, the name was gone, and the window went back to an earlier place (Inbox) instead of showing the list. Flint never writes to a file it finds this way, so nothing was at risk on disk.
- **Fix:** the list opened by its file name stays open under its `id` once that arrives (in place: the Back step is the same list). Test: `e2e/sidebar.spec.ts` (a file written empty, opened, then filled; it failed without the fix).
- **Also seen, not reproduced:** in one full local run the clipboard picker's end-to-end test failed once as the suite's very first test (a cold start); it passed in five repeats and in the next full run (91 of 91). Noted here in case it returns.

### D-135 — Flint is a personal, open-source project on GitHub, from now (M9, 0.9.0)
- **Date:** 7 Nov 2026 · **Status:** Decided by Davit (Q1 to Q3 of M9); the scrub built in 0.9.0
- **What Flint is:** Davit Khachaturovi's personal project, open source under the **MIT licence** (`LICENSE`, copyright 2026 Davit Khachaturovi; `"license": "MIT"`; the fonts keep their OFL licences), public at **github.com/DavitKhachaturovi/flint**. **Supersedes D-19** (handed out by hand, open source out of scope) and brings D-132's plan after v1 forward: the move happens now, in M9.
- **Why public:** the updater reads GitHub Releases without a token only from a public repository (a token inside the app is not an option), and the SignPath Foundation signs only open-source (OSI-licensed) projects. Alternatives not chosen: Apache-2.0 (an explicit patent grant, heavier), no licence (source visible, all rights reserved; rules out the SignPath Foundation), a private repository with a separate public releases repository (more moving parts).
- **The scrub (0.9.0):** nothing private stays in the code, tests, fixtures, mockups, screenshots or docs: no employer, client or colleague names, internal URLs, work emails, private ticket, pull request or CI run numbers. Examples are neutral: the list **Unicorn** (it starts like the fixtures' `Units` and holds "corn" and the typo "unicron", so the shorthand tests keep their intent; `in` now also reaches it by letters in order, which covers that tier), **Alex**, `acme`, `Contoso`, `tracker.example`, `drop.example` (whose initials still give the `de:` short form). Public products stay: the Azure DevOps (`AB#`, `PR 42`), GitHub and Jira link recognisers and their `dev.azure.com/acme` examples, Teams, Discord, OneDrive. The screenshots are re-rendered from the cleaned mockups (`scripts/render-mockups.mjs`). CLAUDE.md keeps the rule from now on. Q-07 (the CLI's name) is **Proposed** as `flint` (the earlier name came from a working title).
- **The history (Q3):** rewritten once, before the first push, by Davit from a kit kept **outside** the repository (`C:\Forge\flint-github-move\`; it lists the strings it removes, so it is never committed): git-filter-repo replaces the same strings in every file of every commit and in every message, maps the author to Davit's GitHub noreply address (`335920609+DavitKhachaturovi@users.noreply.github.com`, the form GitHub links for accounts created after 2017), removes the files that only made sense on the old host (its pipeline file), and strips the screenshots' older versions by blob id (their pixels showed the old names; only the re-rendered ones stay). A verification step searches every commit's files, every message and every author and fails on any listed string; gitleaks scans the history for secrets.
- **One branch:** `main` (the rewritten `m4-lists`; `m1-core` to `m3-capture` and the old `main` are its ancestors), no other branches and no tags until `v1.0.0`, the first release. Work continues in a fresh clone at `C:\Forge\flint-repo` (`C:\Forge\flint` is Davit's own Flint folder); the old working copy's push is disabled so it can never push the unrewritten history.
- **Versions:** 0.9.0 (this scrub), 0.9.1 (auto-update and GitHub Actions), 0.9.x for the rest of M9; **1.0.0 is the first release on GitHub Releases**, and an installed 0.9.x updating to it is the updater's first real check.
- **Trademark (Q-11):** accepted risk; Davit checks WIPO's Global Brand Database for "Flint" in class 9 before the first push. **Checked (8 Nov 2026):** 58 "Flint" records for software, several active, in unrelated markets; the name stays as an accepted risk.
- **Hand checks:** the Teams full-screen share (mini and the popup absent, D-109) is taken as passed.

### D-136 — Auto-update from GitHub Releases; GitHub Actions for CI and releases (APP-06, APP-09, 0.9.1)
- **Date:** 7 Nov 2026 · **Status:** Decided by Davit (the M9 brief; Q2 of M9: how updates behave); built in 0.9.1. **Supersedes** D-19's "no auto-update" and 06 §5's "after v1".
- **How it updates:** electron-updater (6.8.12, with electron-builder 26.18.1) and the GitHub provider (`DavitKhachaturovi/flint`). Checks 30 s after start (off the startup path), then every 4 hours while Flint runs, and on waking when a check is due; stable releases only, never a downgrade; downloads in the background. When ready, a quiet toast in the main window, "Update ready · Flint 1.0.1 · Restart now / Later", once a session (a hidden window shows it when opened; never in mini or the popup; never a Windows notification); gone by itself after 12 s, it counts as Later. **Later:** installs when Flint quits; Restart to update stays in Settings → About, the palette and the tray. **Restart now:** the normal quit path (drafts and windows saved), a silent install, Flint starts again with What's new.
- **The tray (Davit):** "Restart to update" is an accepted exception to the five-item tray, there only while an update is ready, gone once it is installed.
- **Settings → About:** the version with one status line (Up to date · checked 14:02, Checking for updates…, Downloading 1.0.2 · 42%, 1.0.2 is ready · it installs when you restart Flint, Couldn't check for updates (offline)), its button (Check for updates, or Restart now), and **Automatic updates** with the switch "Check for updates automatically" (on by default; off, Flint contacts GitHub only when Check for updates is pressed). Errors show only there, never as a toast. The status line and button fade in as they change (D-89; in the motion census).
- **Palette (D-106):** Check for updates (opens About, where the check shows its progress) and, while an update is ready, Restart to update (Flint 1.0.1).
- **Privacy (APP-09, D-132):** the row now reads "Your notes never leave your machine. Flint only contacts GitHub to check for updates, which you can turn off." Flint's network guard lets through only HTTPS to GitHub's release hosts and only from electron-updater's own session, in the installed build; development, test and benchmark builds never update (test runs use a fake updater, so the end-to-end network test still allows no endpoint, D-31).
- **Unsigned, for now:** `verifyUpdateCodeSignature: false` (no publisher name to check; electron-builder 28 refuses a missing one otherwise); integrity is HTTPS plus the SHA-512 in `latest.yml`, which catches a damaged download but not a compromised account: GitHub 2FA (a passkey or hardware key) and a tag ruleset (only Davit creates `v*` tags, none moved or deleted) are the trust boundary. **Follow-ups:** signed update manifests (Ed25519 in electron-updater 7, still alpha, so not adopted) and code signing (SignPath Foundation or Certum). Installer asset name without spaces: `Flint-Setup-<version>.exe`.
- **GitHub Actions:** `ci.yml` on every push and pull request (windows-latest; Node from `.node-version`; `npm ci` with `.npmrc`'s rules; npm audit, release age, the embedded-Node guard, lint, format, typecheck, unit and end-to-end tests). `release.yml` on a tag `v*`: the tag must be the app's version and `docs/whats-new.md` must have its section (`scripts/release-notes.mjs`); the same checks; `npm run release` uploads the installer, `.blockmap` and `latest.yml` to a draft release; then the release is published with that section and the SmartScreen note as its text. Actions pinned by commit SHA (checkout v7.0.1, setup-node v7.1.0, upload-artifact v7.0.2), read-only token by default, `contents: write` only for the release job. The Azure pipeline stays on the old host until the move (the history rewrite drops it).
- **Not chosen:** a Windows notification when an update is ready while the window is hidden (less quiet); asking before downloading (one more click every release).

### D-137 — The performance budgets, checked on every run (M9, 0.9.2)
- **Date:** 8 Nov 2026 · **Status:** Decided by Davit (Q4, Q5 and Q6 of M9); built in 0.9.2
- **How (Q4):** `apps/desktop/e2e/performance.spec.ts` in the normal suite, on the test clock, the windows off-screen: keypress → screen (main window and popup), Enter → popup hidden and focus back, Enter → durably on disk (50 captures after 10 warm-up ones, the first 10 after a start recorded apart), switching to a 1,000-task list and opening a 400-line note, Ctrl+K results per keystroke and the main-process search, idle CPU over 30 s, memory right after start (Electron's process metrics). A check fails only past its hard limit; the budget is recorded. **On CI** a check proved flaky there is listed in `ADVISORY_ON_CI` and only logs; locally every check stays strict (none listed so far). Results: `test-results/performance.md` and 06's Measurements.
- **Memory (Q5):** the budget becomes **~300–350 MB private after start, hard limit 400 MB**; D-04's 150–200 MB left out Electron's GPU process (121–149 MB here). Measured 305–333 MB. Private bytes are the measure (what Task Manager shows); the working set, which counts shared pages once per process, is recorded beside it.
- **A 1,000-task list (Q5):** keeps its 200 ms hard limit; the 100 ms budget is missed (p95 161–167 ms), a **known issue**, fixed by virtualising long lists after 1.0 (05 §4 rule 6).
- **Enter → durably on disk (Q6):** recorded on every run, never failing a test: it is the disk's flush time (every write is flushed, and Enter waits for two, the draft and the list), which on this machine swings between runs (p95 41–47 ms on most, 102–136 ms on some). The half you feel is checked strictly instead: **Enter → popup hidden and focus back < 50 ms** (p95 6–8 ms), which never waits for the disk (D-27). Not chosen: writing the draft and the list at once (a crash between them could bring the text back as a draft after the task was saved); a strict check that sometimes fails locally.
- **On CI (8 Nov 2026):** a hosted Windows agent passed every check but the 1,000-task list (p50 373–378, p95 474–520 ms; this machine 131–141 / 161–167 ms) and the 400-line note (p50 150, p95 470 ms; here 39 / 66 ms); the agent is 2.5–3× slower (Enter → disk p95 116 ms) but its memory is lower (213 MB: a software GPU process of 21 MB). By the rule above those two checks are advisory on CI (`ADVISORY_ON_CI`, CI sets `FLINT_CI=1`) and stays strict locally.
- **Locally, under load (8 Nov 2026):** with Davit's machine busy the 1,000-task list reached p95 296, 218 and 155 ms in three runs; an occasional red local run is accepted (Davit) until long lists are virtualised; the check stays strict.
- **Also found:** 24 Flint processes left behind by the quit hang (D-138) had been slowing the machine and some measurements; with them gone the numbers above hold run after run.

### D-138 — A quit while the popup faded out left Flint unable to exit (0.9.2)
- **Date:** 8 Nov 2026 · **Status:** Built in 0.9.2 (found as the clipboard picker test's failure, D-134 and CI on 0.9.1)
- **The bug:** Esc or Enter in the capture popup gives the focus back at once and then fades the card out for 140 ms, after which a timer hides the window. A quit in those 140 ms (the tray's Quit, Restart to update, a test closing Flint) closed the window first; the timer then touched the closed window, the error that threw went to Electron's default handler, which shows an error box ("A JavaScript error occurred in the main process … Object has been destroyed"), and Flint did not exit until it was closed. In the test runs these boxes appeared on Davit's screen, one per stuck run; it was taken for an invisible hang until he reported them (8 Nov 2026). Mini mode's hiding and sizing timers had the same gap.
- **Fix:** the popup's fade timer is cancelled when the window closes and does nothing to a closed window; mini mode's timers check the window too. The quit's stages are logged ("quitting", "quitting: windows closed"), so a quit that stops says where.
- **Tests (`e2e/quit.spec.ts`):** the popup opened from clipboard history the way the hotkey opens it (its foreground fallback too), Esc keeping a draft, then a quit inside the fade: Flint exits and the draft is on disk (it failed about two runs in five before the fix, ten in ten pass after); Restart to update with a draft in the popup quits cleanly (the test updater now quits Flint as electron-updater does, D-136). The clipboard spec, which hung one run in three, passes five in five.
- **The walkthrough's step 6 on CI:** the test typed the reminder before the picker had opened on the slower agent, so the task was added without one and the step never moved on. It now waits for each real trigger: the picker open, its "Tomorrow 10:00" row, the reminder in the composer, the task with its reminder in Inbox.
- **The README** (written before the move): its claims checked against what is built (mini mode's Note tab and Ctrl+Alt+Space, the popup also left out of screen shares, inserting into a field in Flint, reminders during Do Not Disturb with Windows' default settings, Windows 11 tested and 10 not, `PROJ-42` as the example, the Electron install step); its screenshot taken from the real app on neutral demo data; and the end-to-end tests described honestly: the capture popup's tests bring it to the front for a moment.
- **After Davit saw the boxes (0.9.3):**
  - **Errors nothing caught in the main process** are always logged ("main process error …"). While Flint quits they change nothing, the quit goes on (an error box then would keep Flint from exiting); in a test run they are recorded and never shown as a box on the screen; otherwise, in normal use, the same box as before. Test: an error thrown on purpose in the middle of a quit is logged and Flint exits (`e2e/quit.spec.ts`); the quit tests also check that nothing was logged as an error.
  - **A quit waits for what is still being written,** at most 3 s: a capture handed over a moment before, the draft Esc just sent, a list change in the queue, the pins and Today's order (written 400 ms after a change). Found by the Restart to update test: a quit right after Esc could exit before the draft was on disk (CAP-08). `settled()` on the drafts, the workspace, the pins and Today's order; tested (`today-file.test.ts`, and the draft after Restart to update).
  - **The remaining gaps closed:** the popup's drag and mini mode's messages never touch a closed window.

### D-139 — On GitHub, public; 1.0.0 the first release (M9 complete)
- **Date:** 8 Nov 2026 · **Status:** Done (Davit's brief: the move end to end, then 1.0.0)
- **The history:** rewritten from the kit (D-135) on a fresh mirror of the old host's latest state: 287 commits on one branch `main`, the old screenshot versions stripped, `VERIFIED` (no listed string in any file, message or author; one author, Davit's GitHub noreply address; the tip's files unchanged), gitleaks clean. The old host's `main` held only a merge of M1, whose files `main` already has; it was dropped.
- **On GitHub:** `DavitKhachaturovi/flint`, created private, the rewritten `main` pushed by Davit, the first CI run on GitHub's Windows runner green (104 end-to-end tests; the 1,000-task list and the long note only log there, D-137), checked through GitHub's own view (README with the logo and screenshot, MIT detected, only `main`, no tags, no listed string), then made **public**.
- **Settings** (applied with the GitHub CLI): issues on, wiki and projects off, squash merges only, branches deleted after merge; Actions limited to GitHub's own actions, **pinned by commit SHA (required)**; workflow token read-only by default, no pull-request approvals by Actions; outside contributors' workflow runs need approval; Dependabot alerts on (version updates off: the 7-day rule decides); secret scanning with **push protection**; private vulnerability reporting. **Rulesets:** `main` (no deletion, no force push, linear history; nobody bypasses), `main CI` (the `checks` job required, Davit as repository admin may bypass: GitHub applies required checks to direct pushes too, so a solo direct push stays possible), `releases` (`v*` tags created, moved or deleted only by Davit). Rulesets and secret scanning need a public repository on this plan, so they came right after the switch.
- **Davit's part:** the GitHub CLI login (device code), creating the repository and the first push (Claude Code's guard declined to push the old host's history to an outside account itself), the social preview upload (no API), and 2FA with a passkey (an unsigned installer makes his account the trust boundary, D-136).
- **The work folder** is `C:\Forge\flint-repo` (`C:\Forge\flint` is Davit's own Flint folder); the old working copy's push is disabled; Claude Code's memory was copied to the new folder. gh CLI: the portable build in `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Programs\gh` (the installer needed admin rights).
- **The logo** heads the README; `docs/readme/social-preview.png` (1280 × 640, `scripts/render-social-preview.mjs`, the bundled Onest) is the repository's social preview.
- **1.0.0:** the first release on GitHub Releases (D-132); nothing changes in the app since 0.9.3. **After 1.0.0 (Proposed):** 1.0.x for fixes, 1.x.0 for features (semantic versioning; D-101's milestone numbering ends with M9).
- **The first release run:** electron-builder's parallel uploads each made a draft release for the tag (none existed yet), so the blockmap landed in a second draft; it was moved to the release and the stray draft deleted, and the release workflow now creates the draft first (`gh release create --verify-tag --draft`), so electron-builder uploads into it.
- **Noted:** Dependabot reports a medium, development-only advisory (minimatch through the lint tooling, never shipped); fixed with the next toolchain update once its versions pass the 7-day rule.

### D-140 — Restart to update: nothing frozen on screen, and an honest minute (APP-06, 1.0.1)
- **Date:** 8 Nov 2026 · **Status:** Built in 1.0.1 (found by Davit's first real update, 0.9.3 → 1.0.0)
- **What he saw:** after Restart now the window stopped responding, closed, and Flint came back about a minute later.
- **What the log showed:** Restart now at 23:53:56.7; electron-updater starts the installer at once; Flint's quit began only 23.6 s later (the main thread had been blocked); the quit itself took 50 ms (D-138's wait for pending writes added nothing); Flint 1.0.0 started 82 s after that.
- **Why (measured here):** Windows Defender checks a large unsigned executable the first time it starts, and starting a process waits for that check. The same 250 MB executable, never seen before: 34.2 s on its first start, 0.17 s after. The 125 MB installer paid it while Flint was starting it (the frozen window), and the new `Flint.exe` and its libraries paid it before 1.0.0 could start. Unpacking the installer itself takes 2.2 s. Defender remembers a file where it is, not by its content (an identical copy elsewhere took 44.7 s again), and an on-demand scan doesn't count (4.3 s, then 31.1 s at the first start), so the check can't be done ahead of time.
- **Fix:** Restart now (and the tray's Restart to update) hides every Flint window at once and shows a quiet notification, "Updating Flint to 1.0.1: Flint reopens by itself in about a minute (Windows checks the new version the first time it runs)", then starts the installer 200 ms later. The wait is the same, but nothing frozen is on the screen and it says what is happening. Tested: the unit test (the installer starts only after the windows are hidden) and `e2e/quit.spec.ts` (the notice is logged before the installer starts).
- **Not chosen:** a visible installer progress window (it covers only the install, not Windows' check of the new Flint afterwards); pre-starting the new version in the background (Defender checks it again at its installed place).
- **The lever left:** code signing (D-135: the SignPath Foundation or Certum). Defender may check signed files from a known publisher faster; to be measured when Flint is signed.

### D-141 — The note editor's live preview made reliable: the plan (NOTE-01, NOTE-09, NOTE-10; grill Q1–Q14)
- **Date:** 8 Nov 2026 · **Status:** Decided (Davit, grill Q1–Q14, complete); **not built yet**. Planned as two releases (Q14): **1.1.0**, the live preview made reliable (with Source mode), and **1.2.0**, tables. **D-142 (Davit, after testing the 1.1.0 preview) reverted Q4, Q6 and Q7, changed the numbers' look (Q3) and the tick's size (Q8), fixed the caret beside a picture, dropped Q2's committed scrambled copies (and the scrambler), and made text formatting's absence from the palette a deliberate exception to D-106.**
- **Davit's brief (daily use of 1.0.0):** (1) list markers, checkboxes and bullets show raw while the cursor is on their line; (2) the checkbox's tick sits right of centre; (3) an image's raw `![](attachments/…)` shows beside it on the cursor's line; (4) numbered lists are unreliable, especially after renumbering or reordering; (5) tables show as raw text. ProseMirror, Milkdown and Tiptap are ruled out: their markdown round-trip reformats files (byte-exact files, Obsidian, task fields).
- **Audit (the causes, verified):** the live-preview layer (`editor/live-preview.ts`) already walks CodeMirror's lezer syntax tree; only task-field chips and link short forms use regexes. (1) and (3) come from the **line-based reveal rule** (`activeLines` / `isActive`, as D-79 decided), and numbered markers have no rendered style at all (they look gray in mono only on the cursor's line, `cm-fl-mark`). (2) the tick is a rotated L placed with hand-tuned margins. (4) has three structural causes: CommonMark lets an ordered list interrupt a paragraph only when it starts at 1, so `text` followed by `10. fixed` stays paragraph text (43 of about 186 numbered lines in Davit's real notes); Tab indents 2 spaces, while an item nested under `1.` needs 3 (4 under `10.`), so it stays a sibling; only Enter renumbers (`@codemirror/lang-markdown`'s `renumberList`), not deleting items or moving lines (Alt+↑/↓). (5) lezer already parses GFM tables (`Table`, `TableHeader`, `TableRow`, `TableCell`, `TableDelimiter`); nothing draws them.
- **Q1, the path: rebuild Flint's layer on the syntax tree (path B), no new dependency.** Root-cause work, not patching: per-marker reveal, the list parser extension with renumbering and correct indents, a table widget that writes back only the bytes that changed.
  - **Not selected, A: a CodeMirror live-preview library.** *Atomic Editor* (`@atomic-editor/editor` 0.6.2, MIT, last release 11 Jul 2026, one maintainer): reveals list markers, images and rules for the whole cursor line (problems 1 and 3 stay); its table edits re-serialize the whole table (`serializeTable`), dropping alignment (`:---:`) and padding (not byte-exact); a React component with its own theme, find bar, lists and code highlighting that Flint's chips, link patterns, code cards, menus, Ctrl+F, outline, paste rules and one-editor-at-a-time would have to override. Its table widget is read for ideas only. *codemirror-live-markdown* (0.5.1-alpha.1, MIT, last release 18 Jan 2026): alpha and inactive, line-based reveal for block marks, rescans the whole document on every update, bundles highlight.js (9 MB unpacked) beside Flint's grayscale highlighting. Neither fixes any of the five problems as is.
- **Q1 addition, an editor test corpus (built as part of this work):** (a) the CommonMark and GFM spec examples as fixtures, each rendered without errors, its file byte-exact, its decorations where expected; kept in their own fixtures folder with the spec's licence (CC BY-SA 4.0) and attribution, test data only, never shipped. (b) Davit's notes folder as a real-world corpus (Q2). (c) Randomised editing tests (typing, deleting, moving lines, renumbering at random positions), checking round-trip and no crashes.
- **Q2 (layer 1 dropped by D-142), real notes in a public repository: two layers.** (1) **Scrambled shape-only fixtures, committed:** markdown syntax, whitespace, line breaks, indentation and Flint's tokens (field keys, date shapes, `attachments/`, `[x]`, `http(s)://`) kept; every letter replaced by a random one of the same case and script (Georgian stays Georgian), digits by digits, URL hosts by `*.example`, file and attachment names by `note-01.md`, `image-01.png`; committed only after Davit reviews the diff. (2) **The real folder, local only:** the same tests run read-only on it when `FLINT_CORPUS` points at it; skipped on CI. **Also (local only, not in the repository):** a rules file for Claude Code and local git hooks refuse any commit whose files, file names or message contain a private word from a git-ignored list.
- **Q3, the reveal rule (supersedes D-79's "raw markdown only on the cursor's line" and NOTE-01's "on the cursor's lines the raw markdown shows"):**
  - **Block markers** (bullet `- `, checkbox `- [ ] `, number `2. `, heading `# `, quote `> `, task-field chips) and **embedded objects** (image, video, file link) stay rendered always, also with the caret right before or after them; they show raw only while the caret is **strictly inside** them.
  - **Inline formatting** (`**bold**`, `*italic*`, `` `code` ``, `==highlight==`, `~~strike~~`, `[text](url)`) shows raw while the caret is inside the span or at its edge (as in Obsidian).
  - **Deliberate entry:** ← from just after a marker or object steps into it (raw, the caret before its last character), → from just before likewise, further presses walk through the raw text; Backspace at the start of an item's text removes its marker, as before. **Clicks don't reveal** (a bullet click puts the caret after it, a checkbox click toggles, an image click opens the viewer). **Selections never reveal**: only an empty caret inside a marker does; copying copies the raw markdown.
  - **Numbered markers** keep the gray mono look (today's `cm-fl-mark`: Geist Mono, faint) as their rendered look at all times (Davit's brief).
  - **Addition: Edit markdown.** A picture's right-click menu gets **Edit markdown**, which reveals its raw link with the caret inside. **Proposed, accepted:** every embedded object (images, videos, file chips) has it; no palette command (it acts on the item under the pointer, like Copy image).
  - **Addition: Source mode (NOTE-09).** A per-note toggle showing the whole note as raw markdown in the same editor, with Flint's font, as an escape hatch; in the palette and the note's right-click menu, remembered per note on this machine like Monospace (UI state; the file never changes). **Proposed, accepted:** a checked item next to Monospace; also in mini mode's Note tab; Monospace still applies in it; switching crossfades; not for task bodies. Its shortcut: see Q5.
- **Q5, Source mode's shortcut: Ctrl+Shift+E.** Ctrl+E was first proposed in the grill, but docs/03 already gives Ctrl+E to inline code (D-106). Ctrl+Shift+E is free (03 §3, `shared/shortcuts.ts`, the editor's keymap) and pairs with Monospace's Ctrl+Shift+M. **Also built with this work:** the missing **Ctrl+E for inline code** (D-106; it wraps the selection or the word at the caret in `` ` `` and unwraps it when it already is code, like Format ▸ Code), in the cheat sheet's Notes group and as Format ▸ Code's hint. (Corrected at the build: this entry first said "and the palette", but the palette has no text-formatting commands at all, Bold and Italic neither, and a palette command would have to act on an editor that just lost the focus; whether formatting gets palette commands was an open question for Davit: no, a deliberate exception to D-106, D-142.)
- **Q6 (reverted by D-142), renumbering (docs/04 §4):** Flint renumbers only the list it edits, in the same undo step, after its own structural edits: Enter, Backspace or Delete removing an item or its marker, Alt+↑/↓, cutting or pasting whole items, Tab / Shift+Tab, Paragraph ▸ Numbered list. The list's first number and its delimiter (`.` or `)`) are kept (11 of the 33 numbered lists in Davit's notes start above 1; none uses `1. 1. 1.` or `)`). Never on opening a file, while typing inside an item's text, in other lists, or after a change from outside Flint; numbers typed by hand stand until that list's next structural edit (the one list with a gap in Davit's notes gets closed up then: accepted). **Addition (accepted):** when renumbering changes a marker's width (`9.` → `10.` and back), the lines nested under that item move by the same number of spaces in the same step, so they stay nested (otherwise children indented 3 spaces under `9.` fall out of `10.`, which needs 4).
- **Q7 (reverted by D-142), nesting indents (docs/04 §4):** Tab nests a list item under the item above it at that item's **actual text column** (2 spaces under `- `, 3 under `1. `, 4 under `10. ` or under `1.  text`), so it really becomes a child (today's Tab adds 2 spaces, leaving an item under `1.` a sibling). Tab and Shift+Tab move the item **with everything nested under it**; with a selection, every selected item moves. Shift+Tab puts the item back at its parent's own marker column, a sibling of its parent. A nested numbered item joins the parent's existing numbered children as the next number, otherwise starts a nested list at 1; the list it left renumbers (Q6). Flint writes spaces, but a tab where the item's siblings or parent already use tab indents (Obsidian's default), so a list never mixes the two; existing tab indents are read as they are (Davit's notes have none: 55 nested bullets at 2 spaces, two at 4, one at 3, one numbered child at 3). **Tab on a list's first item** (nothing above to nest under) changes nothing in the text and **nudges the line right**, the same nudge as indenting the first task (D-87, `ui/motion.ts`: 6 px and back, 260 ms, off with reduce-motion), so the key is clearly registered (Davit's addition). **Proposed (the same rule):** Shift+Tab on a top-level item nudges it left. Outside lists, Tab stays as it is.
- **Q8 (the tick's size changed by D-142), the checkbox:** the note's checkbox is the task row's shared checkbox (`.check` in `renderer/ui/app.css`) in its existing **compact size** (`.fl-compact`: 17 px, 6 px radius, 10 px tick; today's editor box is 16 px), not a new variant (D-110). The tick is the same `CheckIcon` path from one shared constant, centred by flexbox, replacing the rotated CSS L with hand-tuned margins that sat right of centre; the box sits on the text's middle by `vertical-align: middle`. It has the row's hover ring, press scale and check-pop on ticking; the checked line's text fades to muted (D-89; off with reduce-motion).
- **Q9, editing a table (NOTE-10):** a click on a cell edits its text in place (inline formatting follows Q3 inside it). **Tab / Shift+Tab** go to the next / previous cell; **Tab in the last cell adds a row** and goes to its first cell; **Enter** goes to the cell below, on the last row adding a row (same column) (both as Obsidian's table editor since 1.5); **Shift+Enter nudges** (a GFM cell can't hold a line break; Flint never writes `<br>`, one already in a file shows as a break); **↑ / ↓** move between rows and, from the first or last row, out to the line above or below (and from those lines into the cell nearest the caret); **← / →** at a cell's edge go on into the neighbouring cell; **Esc** leaves the table, the caret on the line after it. Only the active cell has an editor (the rest are static); its keystrokes are changes to the note's own text, so Ctrl+Z / Ctrl+Y and the note's undo history work across cells and the rest of the note; **Ctrl+F** finds text in cells and going to a match opens that cell; a selection that crosses a table takes the whole table (copying gives its raw markdown, Delete removes it, undoably); cells wrap their text and a table wider than the column scrolls sideways in its own frame. The raw table only through Source mode or **Edit markdown** in the table's right-click menu. **Where:** notes, mini mode's Note tab, and (Davit's choice) **task bodies** too: edited the same way in the body editor, and drawn as a table by the rendered (read-only) body in the list, the page and the context panel. Source mode stays notes-only.
- **Q10, writing a table back (docs/04 §4):** byte-minimal. A cell edit replaces only that cell's text, keeping its padding as it was; an empty cell (only spaces, like Insert ▸ Table's `|  |`) gets its text with one space on each side. Pasted line breaks in a cell become spaces; every `|` typed or pasted is written `\|` (also inside inline code, as GFM requires) and shows as `|`. A row with fewer cells than the header grows only up to the edited cell, in the row's style; cells beyond the header's count stay in the file untouched (GFM hides them; Flint never destroys text). A new row copies the row above's edge pipes and padding with empty cells; a new column has an empty header, a `---` delimiter and empty cells. The file's line endings are kept. Nothing is ever re-padded or re-aligned on its own, so a table aligned in the raw text can go ragged in Source mode (it renders the same everywhere; Davit's two tables are padded with one space and not raw-aligned). **Davit's addition: Align table**, a deliberate command in the table's right-click menu and the palette (D-106; offered while the caret is in a table) that re-pads the whole table's columns in the raw text in one undo step. **Proposed details:** each column padded to its widest cell, the delimiters' dashes stretched to match, `:---:` and `---:` columns padded on the matching side, the edge-pipe style kept, no cells added or removed (cells beyond the header left as they are); as the rendered table looks the same, a short toast "Table aligned" with Undo confirms it.
- **Q11, a table's size and shape:** a handle appears on hover at the table's right and bottom edge. Each step of a drag adds or removes one column (the average column's width) or row (the row's height), at the right end or the bottom only; the columns or rows about to go show dimmed during the drag, Esc cancels it; the drag stops (and resists) at one column and the header row, so it never deletes a table. If what a drag removes holds text, letting go shows an **inline warning** ("This deletes content in 2 cells", Delete / Cancel; not a dialog), and Ctrl+Z works afterwards. **Commands** (the table's right-click menu and the palette, D-106; on the cell under the pointer or the caret): Add row above, Add row below, Add column left, Add column right, Delete row, Delete column, Delete table, Align table (Q10). Deliberate deletes act at once with an Undo toast, like deleting tasks; the warning is only for drags, which overshoot. No new shortcuts. **Not in 1.1:** dragging rows or columns to reorder them (Obsidian 1.5 has it), Later.
- **Q12, motion (D-89, D-75):** **instant** (recorded as instant in the motion census, `e2e/motion.spec.ts`, naming this decision): revealing and hiding markers and raw links (Edit markdown too; typing never waits), renumbering (a consequence of the edit), Align table (only its toast moves, as every toast). **Animated**, on the duration tokens (so the animation-speed setting applies), interruptible and off with reduce-motion: Tab / Shift+Tab in a list glides the line to its new indent (visual only; the text is already there); Tab on a first item nudges (Q7); the checkbox's pop and the checked text fading (Q8); a table's new row or column easing in (height or width with a fade), a deleted one fading and collapsing, the parts about to go dimming during an edge drag, the active cell's ring moving to the next cell, the inline warning fading in and out; Source mode crossfading on and off. Every new action gets its entry in the census.
- **Q13, the test corpus (details of Q1's addition):** the decoration builder becomes a **pure function** of the editor state and the caret, returning plain ranges and kinds, so Vitest tests it without a window (widgets' DOM stays with the end-to-end tests). **The spec examples** (CommonMark 0.31.2, the latest, 28 Jan 2024; GFM 0.29-gfm; both CC BY-SA 4.0) are committed in `apps/desktop/src/renderer/editor/fixtures/spec/` with their licence and an attribution README: test data only, never imported by the app, never in the installer, excluded from Prettier. The spec's expected HTML can't check decorations, so each example's decorations are checked **against lezer's syntax tree** (every emphasis node has the italic decoration, every list mark its rendered form…), and the caret is moved through **every position** of every example checking invariants: no exception, the document unchanged, decorations ordered, Q3's reveal rule. Where the `N.` extension (Q4) deliberately differs from CommonMark, the examples are listed with the reason. **The scrambler** (`scripts/`, seeded) reads `FLINT_CORPUS` read-only and writes outside the repository for Davit's review; it fails if any word of four or more letters from the input survives in its output (the private-word hooks are the second guard). **Tests on the real folder** print no note text: a failure names only a file number, an offset and the node kind. **Randomised editing** (fast-check 4.10.2, already pinned) generates typing, Enter, Backspace, Tab / Shift+Tab, Alt+↑/↓, cell edits and Align table, and checks: bytes outside the edited range unchanged, renumbering only in the edited list (Q6), undo back to the exact text, tables still parsing with the same cells; runs from `FLINT_FUZZ_RUNS` as in core, the seed printed on failure; about 20 s or less added to normal CI. **Davit's addition: long fuzzing apart from every push:** a nightly scheduled GitHub Actions run of the randomised tests with many more runs, and a local command for a long session on demand. **Proposed details:** `npm run fuzz` runs core's format fuzz and the editor's for a time budget (`FLINT_FUZZ_MINUTES`, default 15; fast-check's time limit) instead of a count; `.github/workflows/fuzz.yml` runs it at 02:00 Tbilisi (`schedule`) and from the Actions tab (`workflow_dispatch`), actions pinned by SHA with a read-only token (D-139), a new random seed each night, always printed; a failure reaches Davit as GitHub's usual failed-workflow email. GitHub pauses scheduled runs on a public repository after 60 days without activity.
- **Q14, the releases: split in two.** Tables (Q9–Q11) are the largest and riskiest piece, and Davit's daily problems (1)–(4) shouldn't wait for them. **1.1.0, the live preview made reliable**, built in this order: (1) the test corpus and the pure decoration builder (tests before the rewrite); (2) the reveal rule (Q3), Edit markdown and the image; (3) numbered lists: the parser extension (Q4), renumbering (Q6), nesting (Q7); (4) the checkbox (Q8); (5) Source mode and Ctrl+E (Q5), long fuzzing (Q13). **1.2.0, tables** (NOTE-10: Q9–Q11, their motion in Q12): rendering, cell editing, byte-minimal writes, Align table, the edge drag and the commands, in notes, mini's Note tab and task bodies. For each: the full checks, then an installer built locally for Davit still labelled with the current version (nothing bumped, tagged or pushed before his OK; his test install then updates itself to the real release from GitHub); after his OK the version bump, release notes and What's new (D-117), the push, and the tag `v<version>`, which only Davit can create (D-139).
- **Built, 1.1.0 step 1 (8 Nov 2026; not released):** the live preview's decisions are a pure function, `buildPreview()` in `editor/preview.ts` (plain items: hide, mark, line, bullet, checkbox, image, field, copy, short link), which `live-preview.ts` turns into decorations; today's behaviour unchanged except the fixes below. The parser's additions are in `editor/syntax.ts` (`NOTE_SYNTAX`), for the editor, the tests and (with Q4) the outline. **Spec fixtures:** `editor/fixtures/spec/` (CommonMark 0.31.2, 652 examples; GFM 0.29-gfm's 24 extension examples), made by `scripts/editor-spec-fixtures.mjs`, with an attribution README (CC BY-SA 4.0; the licence by its link, the changes listed). **Tests:** `preview.test.ts` (without focus, every example's items against the syntax tree; with focus, the caret at every position: items inside the text, line classes at line starts, nothing hidden or replaced across a line break, which CodeMirror refuses to draw), `corpus.test.ts` (the same on `fixtures/notes/` and, locally, `FLINT_CORPUS`: all 55 of Davit's notes pass; failures name a note's number only), `editing.fuzz.test.ts` (randomised typing, deleting, Enter, Backspace, Tab / Shift+Tab, Alt+↑/↓, undo and redo; the preview drawable after every step; undoing everything gives the exact text; `FLINT_EDITOR_FUZZ_RUNS`, default 300, so CI's `FLINT_FUZZ_RUNS=10000` doesn't apply to its multi-step sequences; it was checked to catch a reintroduced bug), `line-endings.test.ts`; together under a second. **Bugs the spec examples found, fixed:** a link whose address starts on the next line (example 510) made the preview hide across a line break, which crashes CodeMirror's drawing (hiding is now split at line breaks); formatting inside a link's text (`[*foo* bar](/url)`, an image inside a link) showed raw away from the caret; an image whose markdown spans lines keeps its raw text with the picture after it. **Proposed (byte-exact files, an obvious gap):** the note editor keeps a note's line endings (`editor/line-endings.ts`): CodeMirror works with `\n`, so the editor converts at its edges and gives its text back with the note's dominant line ending (as core does for task lists); before, a note with Windows line endings had all of them rewritten on its first edit, and opening one with the caret at the end could throw.
