(Davit's 55 notes all use `\n`.) **The scrambler** (`scripts/scramble-notes.mjs`, seed 141 by default): besides Q2's rules, an ordered list's number is kept (only `1.` interrupts a paragraph, so scrambled numbers changed 10 notes' structure), a code fence's language and an HTML tag name are kept only when on its fixed list of known names (a fence labelled with a private word is scrambled), and a surviving word is reported by its length and the character before it, never the word; it refuses to write inside the repository. On Davit's notes: 0 surviving words, and all 55 copies parse to exactly the same syntax-node sequence as the originals. **The copies wait for Davit's review** (outside the repository) before they are committed to `fixtures/notes/`. **Long fuzzing, built:** `npm run fuzz` (`scripts/fuzz.mjs`: every test file using fast-check, found by scanning, in rounds with a new printed seed; `FLINT_FUZZ_MINUTES`, default 15; a failure prints the command that repeats its round), `scripts/fuzz-seed.mjs` (Vitest setup: `FLINT_FUZZ_SEED` fixes fast-check's seed), `.github/workflows/fuzz.yml` (nightly 22:00 UTC, 02:00 in Tbilisi, and on demand with a minutes input; Windows like CI, actions pinned by SHA, a read-only token, no Electron download). **Found along the way:** a colleague's name had reached the public repository in `smoke.spec.ts` (three times, as `\nName` inside strings, which the private-word hook's `\b` pattern misses after an escape's `n`); replaced with `Alex` (history and the hook's pattern: Davit's call).
- **Built, 1.1.0 step 2 (8 Nov 2026; not released): the reveal rule (Q3).** One definition of a block marker's span, `blockMarkers()` in `preview.ts`: the marker and the space after it (`- `, `- [ ] `, `12. `, `## `, `> `), a rule, a task field, or a one-line embedded object; the preview and the tests both use it. Only an empty caret reveals; a selection never does (also for code cards and bare URLs, which revealed for selections before). Block markers and objects show raw only with the caret strictly inside their span; inline formatting (emphasis, strong, strikethrough, highlight, inline code, links) while the caret is inside or at its edge; a code card opens while an empty caret is in it, as before; numbered markers always have the gray mono look (`cm-fl-mark cm-fl-number`). ← from just after a drawn marker or object and → from just before it put the caret one character into it (`stepInto`); a click landing before or inside a hidden line prefix (bullet, checkbox, heading, quote) puts the caret after it, at the text (`clicksDontReveal`; a click on a bullet places the caret now, it was ignored before). **Problem 3 fixed:** a picture stays drawn with the caret beside it; with the caret inside its markdown the raw link shows with the picture after it (**Proposed**: the picture helps while editing its link). **Edit markdown** in the right-click menu of a picture, video or file (the editor's `Image` nodes) puts the caret where the address starts (**Proposed**). Tests: `reveal.test.ts` (Q3 at every caret position of every spec example; selections reveal nothing; stepping in), `e2e/live-preview.spec.ts` (bullets, checkboxes, headings, numbers, the picture, Edit markdown, a bullet click, Select all), a census step (← into a heading's marks: instant, D-141 Q3, D-75).
- **Built, 1.1.0 step 3 (8 Nov 2026; taken out again by D-142): numbered lists (Q4, Q6, Q7).** **Q4:** `NumberedLines` in `editor/syntax.ts`, in `NOTE_SYNTAX`, so the editor, its tests and the outline (`panel/outline.ts`) read lists alike. As the decision says, the number must be **followed by a space** (the audit's regex also took a bare `2.` at a line's end, which would flip a line into a list while `2.` is being typed, and changed CommonMark example 285); `syntax.test.ts` pins the one CommonMark example Flint reads differently, 304 (`…is\n14.  The number of doors…`), and fails if any other changes. On Davit's notes CommonMark reads 143 of 184 numbered lines as list items, Flint all of them. **Q6:** `renumberAfterEdits` in `editor/lists.ts`, a transaction filter: a transaction counts as a structural edit when it has a user event (so never a change from outside, nor undo or redo) and either adds or removes a line break, or is one of `delete` (Backspace removing a marker), `move.line` (Alt+↑/↓), `input.indent` / `delete.dedent` (Tab / Shift+Tab) or `input.format.line` (Paragraph ▸ items, now their own event so bold or italic in an item never renumbers); the lists its changes touch (the innermost ordered list at each changed range; for Tab and Shift+Tab also the one a level up) are renumbered in the same transaction, so one undo step. **Clarified:** "the list's first number is kept" means the number the list started at before the edit (Alt+↑ moving `2. b` above `1. a` gives `1. b`, `2. a`, not `2.`, `3.`); a list the edit made (Paragraph ▸ Numbered list, Tab's new nested list) starts where its first item says. A wider or narrower number moves the lines nested under its item by as many spaces (lazy continuation lines and tab indents stay). **Q7:** `nestItems()` for Tab / Shift+Tab (`listTab` in `live-preview.ts`): the item and everything under it move to the item above's text column (or back to the parent's marker column); a numbered item under an item without numbered children becomes `1.`; a tab where the item's or its neighbour's indent has one; on the first item, or Shift+Tab on a top-level item, the line nudges (`nudgeElement`, shared with D-87's row nudge in `ui/motion.ts`); outside lists and in code, Tab indents as before. The moved lines glide to their new indent (Q12; `--fl-dur-base`, off with reduce-motion). Tests: `lists.test.ts` (renumbering, the start kept for a moved line, width shifts, other lists untouched, one undo step, outside changes untouched; nesting at the text column, joining, Shift+Tab, impossible moves, tabs; a randomised property that renumbering changes only numbers and indentation and nothing in another list), the editing fuzz now with renumbering and the list Tab, `live-preview.spec.ts` (Tab, Shift+Tab, the first item, Alt+↑ renumbering and Ctrl+Z), census steps (the glide in and out, the first item's nudge).
- **Built, 1.1.0 step 4 (8 Nov 2026; not released): the checkbox (Q8).** The note's checkbox widget is the task row's `.check` (`ui/app.css`) inside a `.fl-compact` holder (`cm-fl-task-box`, which only places it: inline-flex, `vertical-align: middle`, 8 px before the text), so the compact variant applies with no copy of its styles; the old `.cm-fl-checkbox` rules are gone. The tick's path is one constant, `ui/check.ts` (`CHECK_PATH`, `CHECK_STROKE`, `checkSvg()` for the editor's widgets; `CheckIcon` uses it too). Ticking pops the box (the row's check-pop: scale 1.15, 180 ms, the press easing) and fades the line's text to muted; unticking fades it back (`--fl-dur-state`; off with reduce-motion). Tests: `live-preview.spec.ts` (17 px, the tick's centre within 0.5 px of the box's both ways, ticking and unticking in the file), census steps (tick, untick).
- **Built, 1.1.0 step 5 (8 Nov 2026; not released): Source mode and Ctrl+E (Q5, NOTE-09).** Source mode is per note on this machine, as Monospace: `sourceNotes` in `ui-state.json` (`workspace.setSource`), the view's `source` flag, `noteSource()` on the bridge, which mini mode's platform has too (its Note tab shows the toggle for a note that exists); a change reaches both windows. In the editor it is a compartment (`previewMode`, `sourceMode()` in `live-preview.ts`): on, the live preview, its clicks and the click rule are off, so every line is its raw markdown in Flint's font; Monospace still applies; Tab nesting, renumbering, highlighting and the menus stay. Switching fades the text over (opacity from 0.25, `--fl-dur-state`; off with reduce-motion). In the note's right-click menu, checked, after Monospace (Ctrl+Shift+E as its hint); Ctrl+Shift+E; the palette, while a note is open: **Source mode** / **Source mode off**, and **Monospace** / **Monospace off** beside it (**Proposed**: Monospace had no palette command, D-106). Task bodies have neither. **Ctrl+E** wraps the selection or the word at the caret in backticks, or unwraps it (Format ▸ Code, which now shows Ctrl+E); the cheat sheet's Notes group lists Ctrl+E and Ctrl+Shift+E. Tests: `live-preview.spec.ts` (Source mode by key, menu and palette, the file unchanged; Ctrl+E and Ctrl+Z), census steps (Source mode on and off).
- **Q4 (reverted by D-142), numbered lines start lists (docs/04 §4):** Flint's markdown parser gets a small extension (a lezer `endLeaf` rule): a line `N.` or `N)` followed by a space ends the paragraph above it, so any numbered line starts or continues a list, as in Obsidian's editor (Live Preview and Source mode). Rendering only: the file is never changed; all of Davit's notes parse over their full length with it. **Known difference:** GitHub and Obsidian's Reading view follow CommonMark and show `text` + `2. item` (no blank line between) as one paragraph. A prose line starting with `2026. ` shows as a list item: accepted (notes use hard line breaks).
- **Tables (NOTE-10, Davit's brief; details in the grill):** GFM tables render as real tables. Markdown can't store column widths, so there is no column resizing: dragging the table's right or bottom edge adds or removes columns or rows; removing ones that contain text warns first ("This deletes content in 2 cells"). Edits write back only the bytes that changed (alignment row, padding and escaping kept).


- **Built, 1.2.0 step 1 (8 Nov 2026; not released): the table model (Q10, Q11).** `editor/table.ts` reads a table's lines (the GFM `Table` node's range) into rows and cells at the unescaped pipes, with each cell's exact range and its text between the padding, the alignment and the pipe style; and makes the edits: `setCell` (only the cell's bytes; an empty cell gets one space on each side; `|` escaped; line breaks as spaces; a short row grows to the edited cell in its own style; cells beyond the header kept), `addRow` (the row above's indent, pipes and cell widths), `addColumn` (the table's delimiter style), `deleteRow`, `deleteColumn` (with one pipe; never the last column), `deleteTable`, `alignTable` (on request, whole table, stable when run again). **Accepted by Davit (8 Nov 2026; found by the randomised test, gaps in the byte-minimal rule):** GFM can't write an empty first or last cell, or a first cell starting like a block (`* `, `- `, `# `, `1. `…), in a row without that edge pipe: the row would lose a cell or the table would end. There `setCell` also writes the edge pipe, the smallest change that keeps the table. Backslashes right before a pipe or at the end of a cell's text are doubled, so a typed `\|` can't break the table (**replaced by D-154:** reading never undid the doubling, so the backslashes doubled at every write); spaces at a cell's edges are padding, not text. Tests: `table.test.ts` (reading, every edit, Align table; a randomised property: only the edited cell's bytes change, the other cells and the table's shape stay; 50,000 runs passed).
- **Built, 1.2.0 steps 2–3 (8 Nov 2026; not released): tables drawn and edited in place (Q9).** `editor/table-view.ts`: each table is a block widget (a state field, as block widgets must be; off in Source mode), drawn as the rendered body draws one (`.cm-fl-table`, the same borders and padding), alignment from the delimiter row, cells rendered with the task title's inline markdown, a table wider than the note scrolling sideways. **The note's selection is the truth:** the cell it is in is the active one and holds a small editor of its own; its every keystroke is `setCell` on the note's text (so the note's undo, Ctrl+F and copy work), and moving the note's caret into a cell (a click, a key, undo, a find match) makes that cell active. Tab / Shift+Tab, Enter (adding a row on the last), Shift+Enter (a nudge: `nudgeElement`, back in `ui/motion.ts`), ↑ / ↓ (into and out of the table), ← / → at a cell's edge, Esc (to the line after), Ctrl+Z / Ctrl+Y in a cell run the note's own undo and redo. A selection that crosses a table is widened to the whole table (`wholeTables`). Focus moves with the active cell at once when typing was going on in a cell (a key, an undo, a new row), so no key is lost; after a click or a move from the note's editor, a frame later. **Proposed:** the cell being edited shows its own markdown raw (the others are rendered); Esc on a table at the end of the note adds a line after it; ↑ from the header and ↓ from the last row leave the table. Edit markdown for a table (`showTableMarkdown`) is in place for the menu (step 4). Tests: `table-view.test.ts` (the cell at a position, the whole-table selection), `e2e/tables.spec.ts` (drawn, a click edits a cell with only its bytes written, Ctrl+Z, Shift+Enter, Tab and Enter adding rows, ↓ / ↑ / Esc).
- **Built, 1.2.0 step 4 (8 Nov 2026; not released): the table's commands (Q11).** `tableCommands()` in `table-view.ts`, for the table's own right-click menu (on the cell under the pointer: Add row above / below, Add column left / right · Delete row / column / table · Align table, Edit markdown; Add row above and Delete row off on the header, Delete column off with one column) and the Ctrl+K palette (`tablePaletteCommands()`: the same, on the cell the caret was last in, offered only while it is in a table, in a note or a body). Deletes act at once; Delete row / column / table and Align table show a toast with **Undo**, which runs the note's own undo (`announceUndo` in `media.ts`, the main window's and mini mode's toast). Edit markdown shows the table raw (`showTableMarkdown`) until the caret leaves it. Tests: `tables.spec.ts` (the menu: Add row below, Delete row and its Undo, Align table and its Undo, Edit markdown; the palette's Add column right).
- **Built, 1.2.0 step 5 (8 Nov 2026; not released): the edge drag (Q11).** A handle shows on hover at the table's right edge (columns) and bottom edge (rows); each step of a drag is the average column width (row height). During the drag the columns or rows that would go are dimmed and a small badge counts those that would come ("+2 columns", **Proposed**); Esc cancels; at least one column and the header row stay. On letting go the columns or rows are added (each in the table's style) or taken away at the end, all in one change (one undo step). If what would go holds text, an inline warning shows first under the table ("This deletes content in 2 cells", **Delete** / **Cancel**, Esc cancels; not a dialog), and Ctrl+Z works afterwards. **Proposed:** the edges show the resize cursors (beside UI-01's arrow on controls: a drag edge is not a button). Tests: `tables.spec.ts` (a column added and taken away; the warning, Cancel, Delete, Ctrl+Z; a row added and taken away; Esc during a drag).
- **Built, 1.2.0 step 6 (8 Nov 2026; not released): the table's motion (Q12).** The active cell's ring is an overlay that glides from cell to cell (`--fl-dur-fast`) and fades in and out; a table that changes shape eases from its old size to its new one (`--fl-dur-base`), so a deleted row or column collapses, and new rows or columns fade in (each edit that adds them says where); the parts an edge drag would take away dim, the warning fades in and out (step 5); all off with reduce-motion, nothing waits for them (D-75). Census steps: a cell edited (the ring fades in), Tab (it glides), Tab in the last cell (a row fades in, the table grows). Tables work wherever the note editor does: notes, mini mode's Note tab and task bodies while they are edited; the rendered body draws them as before (markdown-it's tables, the same borders and padding).

### D-142 — After testing the 1.1.0 preview: numbered lists as in 1.0.1, plain numbers, the tick, one caret beside a picture; no copies of real notes; formatting not in the palette (NOTE-01, NOTE-02, NOTE-09, D-141)
- **Date:** 8 Nov 2026 · **Status:** Decided (Davit, after testing the first local 1.1.0 preview); built for the second preview, not released.
- **Numbered lists back to 1.0.1 (reverts D-141 Q4, Q6, Q7).** Davit prefers the old behaviour. No parser extension: as CommonMark says, a numbered line right after a paragraph line stays in that paragraph unless a blank line separates them (only `1.` interrupts a paragraph). No renumbering by Flint (CodeMirror's Enter continues a list and numbers the items after it, as in 1.0.1). Tab and Shift+Tab indent by two spaces as in 1.0.1 (no nesting at the parent's text column, no first-item nudge, no indent glide). Taken out: `editor/lists.ts` and its tests, `NumberedLines` and its spec test, the Paragraph ▸ items' own user event; the outline reads notes with CodeMirror's parser again.
- **Numbers' look (supersedes D-141 Q3's "gray mono look").** A list's number is plain text in the note's font, like the text after it, and looks the same with the caret on its line or not (the preview gives it no style in either state).
- **The checkbox's tick (changes D-141 Q8).** It was centred but looked far too small (the compact variant's 10 px tick in the 17 px box). It is now the task row's own 12 px tick in the 17 px box (`.cm-fl-task-box.fl-compact .check svg` in `editor.css`; `checkSvg(12)`); still centred. Mini mode's task rows keep the compact 10 px tick.
- **One caret beside a picture.** Right before or after a drawn picture the browser drew its own caret badly (at both ends of the picture). There Flint hides the browser's caret (`cm-fl-caret-hidden`) and draws one caret where the cursor really is, with CodeMirror's layer API (`pictureCaret` in `live-preview.ts`: `RectangleMarker.forRange` for the cursor, blinking like the native one, steady with reduce-motion); elsewhere, and in Source mode, the browser's caret as before.
- **Accepted:** the Proposed details of D-141 that still apply: a picture is shown after its markdown while the caret is inside it; Monospace in the palette beside Source mode.
- **Text formatting stays out of the palette: a deliberate exception to D-106.** Editor formatting (Bold, Italic, Code, Highlight, Strikethrough, the Paragraph and Insert items) lives in the keyboard shortcuts, the slash commands and the right-click menu; the palette would have to act on an editor that has just lost the focus.
- **No copies of real notes in the repository (drops D-141 Q2's layer 1).** Nothing derived from Davit's notes is committed: the scrambled copies were deleted, unreviewed and uncommitted, and `scripts/scramble-notes.mjs` was removed. What stays: the local-only `FLINT_CORPUS` tests on his real folder (read-only, no note text printed), the CommonMark and GFM spec fixtures and the randomised tests in CI.
- **The colleague's first name in the published history:** not rewritten (a common first name with no identifying context). The fix (the name replaced with `Alex` in `smoke.spec.ts`) goes out with the next push. The local private-word hooks (outside the repository) were widened so an entry matches whatever precedes it, also right after an escape such as `\n` inside a string, with a local test for exactly that case; the widened match finds nothing in the current tree (362 files and their names) and, in the published history, only that name.
- **After testing the second preview (Davit, 8 Nov 2026):** (1) **A checked item in a note** keeps its dimmed text and is now **struck through** too, from its text on (not the space before it), in the dimmed colour; its task-field chips aren't struck; ticking fades the strike in with the dimming. Completed tasks in task lists stay dimmed without a strikethrough, as 03 §3's completed-tasks section proposes; unchanged (Davit asked to be told, not to change them). (2) **The caret beside a picture** was as tall as the picture: it now has the height of the browser's caret in the note's body text (a body character's box, measured; the font size's usual box when the note has no body text), sits on the picture's first text line where typing goes, at the picture's edge, and blinks like the native one. Built for the third preview.

### D-143 — 1.1.1: the GitHub security alerts fixed (CodeQL, Dependabot)
- **Date:** 8 Nov 2026 · **Status:** released in 1.1.1 (8 Nov 2026). Built on the released 1.1.0 (branch `security-1.1.1`), without the 1.2.0 table work, so it can ship as 1.1.1 on its own; merged into the 1.2.0 work afterwards.
- **What was reported.** CodeQL code scanning on GitHub raised 9 alerts: #4–#9 "Polynomial regular expression used on uncontrolled data" (`js/polynomial-redos`) in `packages/core/src/shorthand/parse.ts`, `paste.ts` (4) and `trace.ts`, and #1–#3 "Incomplete string escaping or encoding" in `scripts/check-release-age.mjs`, `scripts/release-notes.mjs` and a test in `workspace.test.ts`. Dependabot raised #1, minimatch's denial of service through globparts amplification (GHSA-g3hv-75wg-m249). Every alert is fixed; none is dismissed and none is suppressed (no CodeQL comments).
- **Who controls the input.** The capture popup parses its task line on every keystroke, the main window parses what is typed or pasted, and the main process labels and reads every stored task body. All of it is text the user types or pastes, with no length limit: a pasted log line or minified JSON of 50,000 characters is ordinary use. So the risk is a frozen window (a local denial of service), not code execution or data exposure; the budgets in 05 §4 (shorthand parse under 1 ms per keystroke) are what was broken.
- **#4, `parse.ts` (the `##` list name, CAP-03, CAP-05): real.** `replace(/\.+$/, '')` on the name after `##` restarted at every dot, so `##` followed by a long run of dots and then a letter took time quadratic in its length (0.54 s per keystroke at 50,000 characters). The same scan had two worse quadratic paths CodeQL didn't flag: at every `@` it read every word of the rest of the line to look for a time (`matchTime`, REM-02: 16.6 s for `@ ` repeated), and it searched all code spans for each character (1.8 s). Fixed: the trailing dots are dropped in one pass from the end; the code spans are walked with a pointer that only moves forward; `matchTime` reads at most its six words and stops at the first word no time rule can start with (the word set and first words are listed next to the grammar, which must extend them); the multi-word list names (D-87) are worked out once per line and each run of words is normalized word by word. **After review:** the time had moved into the list lookup (D-25): each name after `##` was compared with every list by edit distance, whose cost is both lengths multiplied, so the alert's own shape still took 0.1 to 2 s per keystroke with 30 to 200 lists (7 s with 200 lists of long names). Now `listResolver` normalizes the lists' names once per parse, finds same names in a map and prefixes among lists with the same first character, and compares by edit distance only names whose lengths differ by at most 2 (the distance is never less than that difference). Results are unchanged.
- **#5–#8, `paste.ts` (paste-to-capture, CAP-06, CAP-07): real.** The title trim (`[\s{}[\],"]+` at both ends), the trailing `\s+$`, the fenced-block regex in `taskLabel` and the image-link test took 0.3 to 1 s at 50,000 characters, and `taskLabel` runs in the main process on every task body. Four more patterns in the same file, not flagged, were as slow: the image-link test in `classifyPaste`, the bare-URL test, the Dataview-field strip and `fencedBlock`'s `\s+$`. All eight are replaced by plain scanners in `shorthand/paste-scan.ts` (not exported from the package) that read each character a bounded number of times, and `trimEnd()`, which removes exactly the characters `\s` matches. **After review:** the new fence scanner searched backwards for a line break once per opening fence, so fences separated by U+2028 or `\r` (which the file format keeps inside a body line) took 1.2 s at 200,000 characters, where the old regex was fast; it now notes the line breaks while reading, and is linear on that shape too.
- **#9, `trace.ts` (stack traces, D-65): real.** The Go function-line regex `^\S.*\(.*\)$` was quadratic on `!` followed by many `(` (1.3 s at 50,000 characters); the unflagged located-frame pattern (`at … (file:line)`, `:line N`), which classifies every pasted line, was slower (0.8 to 2.8 s). The Go regex now keeps `(` (and the line ends, as before) out of the run before the first `(`, so it can try only one `(`; the located frame is read once from the end of the line by a function. `FRAME_LINE`'s type is now a list of objects with `test` (regexes or that function).
- **The highlighter (D-38, D-65), unflagged: real.** Found next to #9: the file-and-line pattern of the stack-trace highlighter (1.8 s at 50,000 characters) and the JSON token pattern (0.7 s on `"\` repeated, reachable from any task body's ```` ```json ```` block) were quadratic. Their fix (linear matchers in `renderer/highlight/tokens.ts`) **moves to 1.2.0** (Davit, 8 Nov 2026): the smoke test failed on the branch with it, and while the table work's tests ran at the same time the runs couldn't tell a regression from load; rather than hold up the security release, it is dropped from 1.1.1 and ships with 1.2.0 after a clean, quiet run.
- **#1–#3, escaping: not exploitable, fixed anyway.** All three are build or test code, nothing that ships, with input from the repository itself. `check-release-age.mjs` encoded only the first `/` of a package name in the registry URL (npm names have at most one, so harmless): the name is now encoded completely (`@scope%2Fname`). `release-notes.mjs` built a regex from the version and escaped only `.`: a real latent bug, since a version with build metadata (`1.2.0+build.5`) found no section; the heading is now matched as plain text. The test in `workspace.test.ts` escaped only `-` in a fixed header; it now compares the prefix as plain text.
- **Dependabot #1, minimatch: not reachable, updated anyway.** minimatch is used only by development and build tools (ESLint, typescript-eslint, electron-builder) and never ships in the installer; the vulnerable code runs only with `optimizationLevel` 2 or higher, which none of them sets, on patterns from Flint's own config. No parent package uses minimatch 11 yet, so an override limited to the vulnerable versions, `"minimatch@>=7.0.0 <11.0.1": "11.0.3"`, moves the 10.x and 9.x copies to 11.0.3 (published 28 Oct 2026, so past the 7-day rule of D-30; no exception needed) and leaves the 3.x and 5.x users alone. `npm audit` went from 15 moderate findings to none. ESLint's and electron-builder's file matching was compared before and after on every file in the repository and the app's packaging rules: identical. **To check in the 1.1.1 preview:** the installer packs the same files as 1.1.0. **To remove** once ESLint, typescript-eslint and electron-builder move to minimatch 11 themselves. CI's `npm run audit` fails only on high or critical findings, so Dependabot is what would report a moderate one again.
- **Tests.** Each fix keeps the results for normal input: the scanners and matchers are compared with the regexes they replaced by property tests (fast-check, including the whole BMP for `trimEnd`), the list lookup with the old `resolveList`, and the word-by-word normalization with normalizing the whole run; one-off differential runs compared the old and new `parseCapture`, `taskLabel` and `classifyPaste` on hundreds of thousands of random inputs, all identical. Pathological inputs of 50,000 characters, in the shapes CodeQL and the reviews named, must finish within a time limit (best of three runs, a 10 s test timeout): 100 ms for paste and stack traces (each takes under 10 ms), 250 ms for the shorthand parse and `matchTime`, which do more work per character (up to about 25 ms) and are measured with up to 200 lists; the old code took 0.45 to 16 s on these shapes, so the tests fail on it. The highlighter's matchers get their own unit tests (`vitest.config.ts` now includes `renderer/highlight`).
- **Found, not fixed in 1.1.1 (Davit: its own change right after 1.1.1).** The file format's task-line reader (`parseTaskContent`, D-21) is quadratic too, with no regex involved, so CodeQL didn't flag it: `[a::` repeated takes 3.5 s at 40,000 characters, `(a:: ` 2.8 s, short code spans 0.26 s; it runs in the main process for every task line read. Fixing it touches the file format's reader, so it gets its own change with the format's round-trip and fuzz tests (decided by Davit, 8 Nov 2026: right after 1.1.1, with the round-trip tests). **Fixed for 1.2.0 in D-146:** linear, with byte-for-byte the same results. Inline code spans on runs of backticks of rising length are slightly worse than linear (about 70 ms at 50,000 characters, 0.6 s at 200,000), not quadratic; left as is. **Corrected by D-146's review:** the code-span scan was quadratic, on an escaped backtick before longer runs (`\``` repeated: 1.55 s at 50,000 characters); D-146 makes it linear, rising runs included.
- **Davit's answers (8 Nov 2026).** The 250 ms limits for the shorthand parse and `matchTime` are accepted. Before the release, the installer's packed files (`app.asar`) are compared with 1.1.0's, the last check that electron-builder packs the same files with minimatch 11.0.3. **Also in 1.1.1, the note-line flash made robust:** a note's line chosen in Ctrl+K was flashed 60 ms after the editor was told to scroll to it, whether or not the line had been drawn, so on a busy machine the flash could land on an element about to be replaced and never show; it is now part of the editor's own state, drawn with the line whenever the line is drawn (see the next point).
- **The note line's flash is find's current match (Davit's 1.1.1 preview feedback, 8 Nov 2026; replaces D-108's look).** Preview 1: it looked like a faint selection box, not lined up with the text (the line's own box was painted, `--fl-ground-hover` with an inset ring, from the text's very edge across the column). Preview 2 tried a task row's look on a box of its own behind the text; Davit: a row highlight doesn't fit text. **Decided (Davit):** the text highlight Flint already has, Ctrl+F's current match in notes (`.cm-searchMatch-selected`, `--fl-text-primary` at 38% over the text, 3 px corners), on the words searched for in that line, or on the line's whole text when the search matched only loosely (one word's letters in order, not the word; the palette's line results now say so, `loose`), held briefly, then fading; it ends at the first edit. That is how find works everywhere else in Flint. **How:** the palette's line result carries the letters it matched (the ones it shows bold); each run of them is a word searched for (`matchRanges` in `editor/match-flash.ts`, which also falls back to the whole text when the line has changed since the search or the words are past the part the palette showed). They are CodeMirror mark decorations from a state field that clears them at the first change to the note (D-75: interruptible), when the fade ends (1.6 s, held for the first third) or with the next one. The fade runs on a CSS property of the editor (`--fl-match-flash`, registered with `@property`) that the marks read, so a line drawn anew mid-fade doesn't start it again; off under reduce-motion like the other flashes (the property stays at 0%). Placed with the scroll, in the editor's own state, so there is no fixed delay and no element of its own. **Found on the way:** the 300 ms recheck added earlier in 1.1.1 never ran (the effect runs again at once when it clears its target, and its cleanup cancelled the check); the flash is now in the editor's state and needs none. **Moved to 1.2.0 (Davit):** "Go to <list>" in the toast of a task the Inbox composer sends to another list (a new feature, so not in a fix release, D-139).
- **A note could take over another note's editor (found by the flash's review; fixed in 1.1.1, Davit, 8 Nov 2026).** D-115 keeps a note's editor when its ID changes under it (a file given Flint's header and ID by its first save, D-23; a quick note made on the first keystroke in mini mode), so nothing typed meanwhile is lost. Both windows did it by remembering every ID that came from an editor, for the whole session, so a note opened again later, after another note, kept that other note's editor: the other note's undo history came along, and Ctrl+Z there brought the other note's text into it, saved to its file a moment later. Now the editor is kept only for that one step, from the ID it had to the new one, once (`noteEditorKey` in `App.tsx`, `NoteTab.tsx`; `NoteEditor`'s `onId` says which ID it had); mini mode also decides it as the tab is drawn, not in an effect after it, so the old editor never gets the new note's ID for a moment. **And** a change from outside (the file changed elsewhere, the other window's text) is no longer an undo step (`Transaction.addToHistory.of(false)` in `MarkdownEditor.tsx`): Ctrl+Z undoes only what was done in that editor, mapped over the outside change. Regression tests in `editing.spec.ts` and `mini.spec.ts`: the note opened again gets an editor of its own, Ctrl+Z never brings the other note's text into it, and Ctrl+Z after a change from outside leaves that change. Run on the code before the fix, mini mode's test failed (the quick note opened again kept the other note's editor) and so did the outside change (Ctrl+Z took the file's new text back out, about to be saved); the main window's reuse did not happen through the first-save path (the note opens again under the ID it already had), so its part of the fix is a guard, and its test checks it stays so.

### D-144 — After testing the 1.2.0 preview: fixes to tables (NOTE-10, D-141)
- **Date:** 8 Nov 2026 · **Status:** Decided (Davit asked for these fixes after testing the first local 1.2.0 preview: tables render and resize well); how each is done is **Proposed** where marked, for his review; built for the second preview, not released.
- **Text typed below a table stays out of it (Davit's serious bug).** In a note with a table, a picture and a paragraph, typing on the line right under the table made the picture disappear, grew the table and put the text into it. **Cause, confirmed by experiment (`tables.spec.ts`, Flint's parser):** GFM continues a table on every following line until a blank line ("lazy" rows, CodeMirror's parser and markdown-it alike). A word typed on the blank line that separated the table from the picture removed that separator, so the word, the picture's line and every line up to the next blank line became rows (the picture was then a cell's text). The caret was then inside the table, so the table made that cell active and its editor took the focus after the next frame: the keys typed meanwhile went to the hidden row, the rest into the cell, and text was lost ("test" became "tt"). A picture line written right under a table, with no blank line, is a row in every GFM reader. **Fix (Proposed):** the file stays plain GFM and Flint's parser stays GFM's (GitHub and markdown-it, which draws the rendered body, read it the same way); Flint's own edits never make a row nobody wrote. (1) An edit in the note editor (typing, a paste, Backspace, a moved line; not undo or redo, not a file changed elsewhere) that would make a line without a pipe a new row of a table writes the blank line that ends the table before that line, in the same change (one undo step); the typed word and what follows it stay paragraphs and the picture stays drawn. A Backspace that would only remove that blank line moves the caret up instead (nothing changes). A line written with a pipe (`| new |`) is a row, as GFM says, and the lines without one after it still stay out. (2) Typing that puts the caret into a table it wasn't in (that `|` row) shows the table raw, as Edit markdown does, until the caret leaves it: the caret stays where the keys go and no cell takes the focus mid-word. (3) Insert ▸ Table leaves a blank line after the table when text follows it. Esc out of the table at the end of the note still makes one line under it; the first key typed there gets the blank line above it. Tests: `tables.spec.ts` (Davit's note: a word on the line under the table, on the line above "above", with and without blank lines between picture and paragraph, Backspace on the picture's line, a `|` row; each with the file's exact bytes, the picture drawn, the table's rows, the focus in the note, Ctrl+Z), `table-view.test.ts`, `commands.test.ts`, and the randomised editing tests now with tables and pictures together (adjacent, with and without blank lines) and a property: plain text typed outside every table never changes a table's bytes (`FLINT_EDITOR_FUZZ_RUNS=3000` passes; without the fix it fails at once). Not explained by this cause: the extra columns in Davit's screenshot, most likely Tab, Enter or the edge drag pressed while the keys were going into the cell.
- **The same fix, its gaps closed (a review of it found three).** (a) **Backspace or Ctrl+Backspace on the blank line under a table, or Delete at the end of its last row** (shown raw) still pulled the picture in: the guard only looked at edits starting on the line under a table, and these delete the line break at the table's end. It now also looks at edits that reach a table's end. A deletion that would only remove the blank line under a table changes nothing; **Backspace on that blank line moves the caret into the end of the table's last cell** (Proposed), as ↑ from below goes into the table (Q9), so the keys go on there. Text joined onto the table's last row by a deletion (the blank line and part of the next line deleted at once) goes back below it, a blank line between; a line under the table that would read as a heading's underline (`-`, `---`, which takes a table in as the heading's text in Flint's parser) gets the blank line too. (b) **Source mode** had no guard (the guard came with the drawn tables): it now has it too (Proposed), since the file and the way it reads afterwards are the same in both modes; Source mode still draws nothing. (c) **A table in a quote or a list item** wasn't guarded, and a table in a quote was read with the quote's `>` as an extra first column. A row's prefix is now its indentation and the quote's marks (`> `), so a quoted table has its own columns and new rows keep the `> `; the blank line written under a table in a quote is the quote's `>` (the text stays in the quote), and in a list item a line typed at the margin under the table gets the item's indent (it stays in the item; Proposed). Kept as before: text with a pipe joins a table (GFM), so deleting the blank line between two tables makes them one, as in any GFM editor. Tests that fail without the change: `tables.spec.ts` (Backspace and Ctrl+Backspace on the blank line under Davit's table, then a key into the last cell; a word typed under the table in Source mode; a quoted table's columns and a word typed on its `>` line), `table-view.test.ts` (Backspace, Ctrl+Backspace, Delete, a selection deleted into the picture's line; the guard on its own as Source mode has it; quote and list item). The randomised editing tests now run Backspace, Ctrl+Backspace and Delete, put steps right at and after tables' ends, have a quoted and a list-item table among their pieces, and check that plain text typed or deleted below a table never joins it (only text with a pipe may); `FLINT_EDITOR_FUZZ_RUNS=3000` and 20,000 pass; without the change they fail within a few dozen runs.
- **A pasted picture: the caret beside it at once (Davit: it first appeared inside the picture, top middle, and jumped to the right place on the next action).** **Cause, confirmed by experiment (`live-preview.spec.ts`, a 480×160 picture pasted with a synthetic paste event):** after the paste the cursor is right after the picture, where Flint draws its own caret (D-142). The picture is drawn at once, but has its size only once it has loaded; until then it is the 64 px placeholder (`min-width`/`min-height`), and the caret was measured at that placeholder's edge. Nothing measured again when the picture loaded (CodeMirror measures on its own updates and on the window's resize, not on an image's load), so the caret stayed 416 px left of the picture's edge, inside it, until the next key or click. **Fix (Proposed):** a picture's load (a video's metadata) tells the editor, which measures again in the next frame: the caret moves to the loaded picture's edge, and the editor's line heights are measured with it. The picture's box is not reserved ahead of its load (its size isn't in the markdown); the placeholder caret lasts until the load, a frame or two for a file in `attachments/`. Test: `live-preview.spec.ts` (a picture pasted at the note's end; with no key or click after it, the caret is within 8 px of its right edge; fails without the change, 416 px off).
- **The edge drag's warning is a toast, not text in the note (Davit: it appeared as plain text inside the editor).** It was a line drawn under the table inside the editor, so it read as part of the note. **Now (Proposed):** letting go of a drag that would take away text shows the window's toast, like Flint's other confirmations (main window and mini mode's Note tab alike): "This deletes content in 2 cells" with **Delete** and **Cancel**; nothing goes into the note's text or the editor. The cells that would go stay dimmed while it is up. **Cancel, Esc, the toast's time running out (8 s, longer than a notice's 5 s: it waits for an answer) and another toast taking its place all cancel** (the cells undim, nothing changes); a new edge drag cancels a warning still up. Its buttons don't take the focus from the note (the active cell keeps it). Delete acts on the table as it is then, and only if it still has the size the drag started from. It moves and fades like every toast (rise in, sink out; off with reduce-motion), so the motion census's toast steps cover it. **Esc goes to the topmost layer first (Proposed):** it cancels the warning only from the note (its text or a cell) or the toast itself, and not while a menu, picker, the palette or the image viewer is open over it; that layer closes first. **The warning goes with its note's editor (Proposed):** opening another note (or changing mini mode's tab) while it is up cancels it and takes the toast away, since a Delete left over another note would change nothing. Test: `tables.spec.ts` (the drag test: the toast, never inside the note; the dimmed cells; Cancel, Esc and the time running out keep the file byte for byte; Delete; Ctrl+Z; then Esc with the palette open over it closes the palette first, and opening another note cancels it).
- **The edge drag's handles read as "add or remove", not as scrollbars (Davit: they looked like scrollbars).** They were thin accent bars along the whole right and bottom edge. **Now (Proposed):** each is a small rounded chip (20 px, the chip tokens: the tile ground, the chip ring, a stroke-drawn "+" like Flint's icons) beside the middle of the right edge (columns) and of the bottom edge (rows), in a 24 px gutter outside the cells (it replaces the 10 px edge strip and the 8 px space under the table, so a table takes 6 px more below it); they fade in while the pointer is over the table (`--fl-dur-fast`; off with reduce-motion) and only the chip (and 3 px around it) takes the drag, so a click into the last column's or row's cells, right up to their edge, is the cell's. The resize cursors stay. **During a drag (Proposed):** the dragged handle is lit (the accent ring) and the other one hides; columns or rows being added show as a faint dashed ghost of their cells beside the table (lined up with its rows or columns, the "+2 columns" count in its middle), and the table's box grows to make room for it, so the text below moves as it will when the drag is let go of and the handle rides the new edge; columns or rows being taken away are dimmed (as before) inside a dashed outline, and the handle's "+" folds into a "−" (it stays beside the edge: over the dimmed cells it would hide their text). The ghost glides from step to step and fades out when the drag ends; the outline stays while the warning toast is up. Steps, minimums, the count and the warning are unchanged. Tests: `tables.spec.ts` (the handles hidden at rest and shown on hover, beside the middle of each edge and outside the table; clicks 3 px inside the last column's and the last row's edge make those cells active; mid-drag the ghost's 2 × 2 cells and "+2 columns", the other handle hidden; mid-drag taking a row away: the outline, the dimmed cells, the "−"; Esc leaves the file byte for byte), `motion.spec.ts` (two census steps: the handles fading in, the ghost growing). **Review fixes (Proposed):** the chips' 3 px hit area stays inside the table's box (on the outer side it stops at the box's edge), so a table as wide as the note gets no 2 px overflow and no scrollbar under it; and a click in the gutter, in the space under the box or beside a narrow table (anywhere on the table's block but its cells and handles) puts the caret on the line after the table, as ↓ from its last row does, from a cell being edited too. Before, the widget kept the press and left no caret, so the next keys were lost, now over 24 px under every table. When the table ends the note there is no line after it and a click writes nothing: the caret stays where it was (Esc still makes the line). (Changed below: the click makes the line too.) Tests: `tables.spec.ts` (a table as wide as the note: its block's scroll size equals its size, at rest and on hover; clicks 4, 12 and 20 px under the table, beside it and from an active cell, then a key typed: it lands on the line after the table, Ctrl+Z restores the file).
- **Selecting cells (Davit: basic multi-cell selection, not a spreadsheet, just the minimum).** **Now (Proposed where marked):** a drag from one cell across others, **Shift+click** from the active cell, or **Shift+arrows** in a cell (from the text's edge that way: Shift+← at its start, Shift+→ at its end, Shift+↑ / ↓ on its first / last line; inside the text they select text as before; once cells are selected every Shift+arrow moves the far corner; with none selected and no cell that way, Shift+↑ in the header or Shift+↓ in the last row selects the cell's text as before) select a rectangle of cells. The active cell is the anchor: it keeps the ring, the caret and the focus, and no fill; the other cells get a soft fill, the selected text's colour (`--fl-selection`, Proposed), fading in and out (`--fl-dur-fast`; off with reduce-motion). A plain click, a plain arrow, Esc (a second Esc leaves the table as before), typing in the cell, an undo or the caret leaving the cell clear the selection (a state field, `cellRange` in `table-view.ts`). **Ctrl+C** puts the cells on the clipboard as tab-separated text (`text/plain`: rows by `\n`, cells by `\t`, a cell starting with `"` quoted as Excel reads it), which Excel and Sheets paste as cells, and **as a GFM table in `text/markdown`** (the first selected row its header; Proposed). No `text/html` (Proposed): Excel and Sheets take HTML over the plain text when both are there, and HTML can bring formatting with it, while the tab-separated text pastes plain; Chromium keeps a `text/markdown` entry private to Chromium apps, so other apps see only the tab-separated text. A Flint note (outside a table) pastes the `text/markdown` entry as a table of its own, a blank line after it (Proposed); Ctrl+Shift+V pastes the plain text. **Ctrl+X** copies and clears (Proposed). **Delete** and **Backspace** clear the selected cells' text at once, in one change (one undo step of its own, never joined with typing just before it: `isolateHistory`; so is a paste of cells, and a table delete that shows an Undo toast), with the "Cells cleared" toast and its **Undo** (the table's other deletes, Q11); the cells stay selected. **Pasting tab-separated text into a cell** (from Excel or Sheets: rows by `\n` or `\r\n`, one line break at the end dropped, cells by `\t`, Excel's quoted fields read) fills the cells from the active one, or from the selected cells' top left corner, in one undo step; a block larger than the table grows it first, the columns and rows in its style (Proposed), and they fade in (a block that grows both ways fades in its new rows and its new columns); the filled cells are then selected, the caret in the first. Text with neither a tab nor a line break goes into the cell as before; a single column of lines fills down. Every write goes through `table.ts`: `setCells` (each cell as `setCell` writes it; a cell a short row hasn't got is written only for text, so clearing never lengthens a row; each row worked out on its own line and the rows' changes put together once, the added columns and rows in one pass each: linear in the rows, a 5,000-row paste from Excel well under 500 ms), `clearCells`, `cellTexts`, `toTsv`, `parseTsv` (one pass, never going back: linear time whatever the text holds), `cellsMarkdown`. **The palette (D-106):** **Clear selected cells** while cells are selected (also first in the table's right-click menu's deleting group); copy and paste stay shortcuts. The Notes group of the shortcuts lists Shift+arrows / Shift+click. Tests: `table.test.ts` (setCells, clearCells, growing with and without edge pipes, the tab-separated text both ways with Excel's quoting and `\r\n`, pathological inputs under 500 ms each, the markdown; a short row given only the cells it needs for text; several columns added in one pass as one at a time; 5,000 rows set and grown, 3,000 × 5 grown, 5,000 rows cleared, each under 500 ms; randomised, with short rows: a block set or cleared changes only those cells' bytes, a short row gains only the cells it needs for text, the shape and the delimiter row stay; 20,000 runs pass), `tables.spec.ts` (a drag selects a rectangle, the anchor unfilled, a click and Esc clear it; Shift+arrows from the text's edge, a plain arrow clears; Ctrl+C on a synthetic clipboard: the tab-separated text and the markdown, pasted into the note as a table, Ctrl+Z; Delete with the toast's Undo, Backspace with Ctrl+Z, the palette's Clear selected cells; a pasted 2 × 2 block from Excel, a block that grows the table by a column and a row with the file's exact bytes, its new row and column fading in, Ctrl+Z each in one step, plain text into the cell as before; Delete or a paste right after typing undone on its own, the typing kept; Shift+↑ in the header and Shift+↓ in the last row select the cell's text), `motion.spec.ts` (a census step: Shift+→ selects a cell, its fill fades in).
- **A click under a table landed a line too low (likely the root of Davit's report).** The table's block had a vertical margin (`.cm-fl-table-wrap`), which CodeMirror's height map leaves out (its docs: block widgets must not have vertical margins), so a click in the lower part of the line under a table, and anywhere on it under a second table, put the caret on the next line. Now padding; no block widget has a vertical margin. Test: `tables.spec.ts` clicks 20 px down and at the bottom of the line under one table and under the second of two.
- **A table without edge pipes lost cells (a review found it).** Its new empty row was written `  |  `, which GFM (and Lezer, and the model) reads as a leading pipe and no cells: a pasted block that grew the table lost a cell, Enter on the last row lost the next keys, Tab from the last cell indented the row. **Now (Proposed):** a new empty row is always written with both edge pipes (`|     |   |`), as an empty first or last cell needs; a row that is only its leading pipe gets its first cell right after it. Tests: `table.test.ts` (a randomised grow of any table: with or without edge pipes, short rows, in a quote, text around), `tables.spec.ts` (Enter, Tab and a grow paste in a pipe-less table).
- **Backspace under a table that ends the note** deleted the note's last line break, and the next keys became an invisible extra cell. Now the text stays as it is and the caret goes into the end of the last cell, as docs/02 and 03 say. Tests: `table-view.test.ts`, `tables.spec.ts` (an Ending note).
- **Esc with the drag's question up went to the question before the note's find bar or slash menu** (both inside the editor). Now they get it first, as a palette, menu or picker does. Test: the "leaves Esc to a layer on top" test in `tables.spec.ts`, extended.
- **A click under a table sent the keys elsewhere (a review found three ways).** Under a table that ends the note the keys went to the note's start; under a table in a quote the caret sat before the quote's `>` (the text left the quote); under a table right above a heading it sat at the heading's start. **Now (Proposed):** the click makes the empty line after a table that ends the note, as Esc does, and puts the caret there; `leave()`, which ↓ shares, puts the caret after the line's quote marks and indentation, and makes an empty line (a `>` one in a quote) when the line under the table isn't empty. ↓ at the note's end still stays in the table. Test: `tables.spec.ts` (Tail, Quoted and Heading notes, a click and ↓).
- **The table's Undo toasts ("Cells cleared", Delete row / column / table, Align table) could undo something else:** their Undo takes back the note's last step, so typing after the delete made it take back the typing. **Now (Proposed):** the toast goes as soon as the note changes again (`announceUndo` returns a way to take away that toast, never a later one); after its Undo the note's editor (the active cell's, if any) has the focus again, so the next Ctrl+Z is the note's. Test: `tables.spec.ts`.
- **A picture that became the "File not found" card left the caret at the placeholder's edge** (a review saw it in 2 of 7 tries): only a load redrew it. Now a ResizeObserver on each drawn picture sends the redraw (`mediaSized`) on any change of its size: loaded, the card, a player. Test: `live-preview.spec.ts` (the pasted picture resized, the caret follows with no key).
- **The editing fuzz's wider property is back** (narrowed in the first fix): plain text typed outside every table never changes a table's bytes, except, as markdown says, for a table with a fence before it or a line right above its header. 3 × 20,000 runs pass.
- **The container prefix (indent and quote marks) is read in one pass,** no longer by `/^(?:[ \t]*>)*[ \t]*/`, whose nested quantifier CodeQL would flag (D-143). Test: `table.test.ts` (the same result as the old pattern on random lines, 200,000-character lines in time).
- **Two small ones from the reviews.** A paste whose first cell is one a short row hasn't got (nothing written there) left the caret in the row's last cell and nothing selected; now the caret goes into a corner of the filled cells that its row has and the same cells are selected (Proposed). After an edge drag in a note just opened from the sidebar Ctrl+Z went to the app, not the note: the drag now gives the note's editor the focus when it ends (a cell keeps it). Tests: `tables.spec.ts`.
- **The Undo toast outlived its note's editor (a review found it).** Another note opened (or mini mode's tab changed) within its 5 s left "Cells cleared · Undo" up, and its Undo then ran on an editor that was gone: nothing came back, and the note opened again has no history to undo it with. Now the toast goes with its note's editor, as the drag's question does. Test: `tables.spec.ts`.
- **A click under a table in a read-only note wrote to it (a review found it).** `leave()` (the click under a table, ↓ above a heading, Esc at the note's end) made its empty line without looking at read-only: in a newer-format note the editor gained a line the file didn't have, and in a note another window is editing (D-109, locked) the line was written before the claim took effect. Now a read-only note is never written: the caret goes to the line under the table, or at the note's end stays where it was. Test: `tables.spec.ts` (a `flint: 2` note).
- **Under a table, a line of four spaces made the text code (a review found it).** `leave()` put the caret after the whole indentation of the line under the table, so a click there and a letter typed gave an indented code block. Now it skips only as much of the line's prefix as the table's rows have (a quote's `>`, a list item's indent); under a table outside any container the caret is at the line's start. Test: `tables.spec.ts`.
- **The editing fuzz (20,000 runs): a letter typed before a quote's `>` under a table grew it.** A quote or list item right under a table (no blank line between) is no row, but text typed before its mark, or the mark deleted, left a line with pipes that GFM reads as a row: the table took it in. **Now (Proposed):** an edit that writes no `|` never adds a row; the line goes below the blank line that ends the table, as a line without a pipe does. A `|` written there still makes a row. Test: `table-view.test.ts`.
- **The editing fuzz: a row starting with a tab and `>` gave a non-blank "blank" line.** The blank line that ends a table copied its last row's prefix up to the `>`, so under a row like `\t>|a` (outside any quote) it wrote `\t>`, which joined the table with the picture after it. Now the blank line keeps only as many `>` marks as the quotes the table stands in (none outside a quote). Test: `table-view.test.ts`.

### D-145 — How we work: CI does the full runs, a `next` branch, fewer rounds
- **Date:** 8 Nov 2026 · **Status:** Decided (Davit: development had become too slow), effective after 1.1.1.
- **The rules** (also in CLAUDE.md "How we work" and the docs/06 handoff): full end-to-end runs on GitHub Actions, the suite split across four parallel Windows runners (`ci.yml`, Playwright `--shard`); locally only the specs and unit tests covering the change, one local end-to-end run at a time; **the release gate is CI green on the release commit** (`release.yml` checks it and no longer reruns the tests), not a local full run; review rounds only for risky changes (data loss, the file format, security, editor positions); a one-off test failure goes on the flaky list in docs/06 §0, is rerun once and fixed later in a batch; one preview per round of Davit's feedback; one- or two-line decision-log entries for small fixes; parallel work where it doesn't compete for local end-to-end runs, merged and tested one after another.
- **Branches (Davit chose this over pushing to `main` freely or pushing only at a release):** work in progress goes to **`next`**, pushed freely after the local checks so CI runs on every batch; `main` holds only released commits and is fast-forwarded to the green `next` commit at a release, after Davit's OK on the preview; Davit tags it. Updates follow tags, so nothing on `next` reaches users.
- **main's ruleset (Davit, 9 Nov 2026):** "Require linear history" removed (`next` is merged from parallel branches, so a release fast-forward carries merge commits); no deletion, no force-push and CI's checks required stay.

### D-146 — The task-line reader in linear time (D-143, D-21)
- **Date:** 8 Nov 2026 · **Status:** Done for 1.2.0 (Davit, D-143: its own change after 1.1.1, with the format's round-trip tests); not released.
- **What was slow.** `parseTaskContent` (04 §3), which the main process runs for every task line it reads, did work in proportion to the rest of the line at each bracket: it searched for the next `::`, read the key up to it and, when the value didn't close, read to the end of the line; at each character it also looked through all the code spans, and the title's final trim (`/^[ \t]+|[ \t]+$/g`) was quadratic in a run of spaces. At 50,000 characters, `[a::` repeated took 4.4 s, `(a:: ` 4.4 s, short code spans 0.39 s, a run of spaces 1.1 s; code spans inside unclosed fields were cubic (1 s already at 5,000). The writer reads its own output back, so it was as slow, and its last resort (`escapeEverything`, `insertBackslashes`) was quadratic of its own (up to 5.8 s at 5,000 characters).
- **What changed (`format/fields.ts`).** One scan per line keeps where the next `::` and the next bracket are, each searched for once as the scan moves forward; a key is read only when it holds no bracket; code spans are walked with a pointer (by binary search inside a value); where a value closes comes from one pass over the line per bracket kind (the nesting level before each index, and for each index the first later one a level lower), once the value's leading backslashes are read as before; the trim scans from both ends. In the writer's last resort the code spans are walked with a pointer, a bracket has `::` after it when the last `::` starts after it, and the backslashes go in in one pass. The results are byte for byte the same; the format and its rules don't change. At 50,000 characters the reader now takes 1 to 3 ms on each shape in the tests, the writer under 10 ms.
- **Tests (`fields.linear.test.ts`).** 19 reader shapes and 3 writer shapes of 50,000 characters (unclosed fields of both brackets, code spans between, inside and in the keys of unclosed fields, nested brackets, escapes and backslash runs, `:::`, emoji and a lone surrogate, a long title, a run of spaces), each under 100 ms best of three with a 10 s timeout; the old code takes 0.29 s to minutes on each, so every one fails on it. The old reader and writer are copied into the test as the reference and compared on random task lines (the content read, the line written, `escapeTitle`, and the whole line written as a title, which reaches the last resort in about one run in eight) and on arbitrary strings: identical in a one-off run of 200,000 inputs. All of `packages/core` passes, also with `FLINT_FUZZ_RUNS=20000`.
- **Review (one round, the file format's, D-145): two more quadratic paths, fixed.** (1) **The code-span scan** (`codeSpanRanges`, `format/code-spans.ts`, which the reader, the writer's escaping and the shorthand parse all use) skipped an escaped backtick only while looking for an opening run, not a closing one, so on `\``` repeated every opener was one backtick, every later run two, and each opener read the rest of the line: 1.55 s at 50,000 characters, `escapeTitle` 3 s, `formatTaskLine` 23 s at 100,000 on a changed task. The old code was as slow (not a regression of D-146), and the reader was not linear as said above. Now the text's backtick runs are listed once by length, and each length keeps where its next run is as the scan moves forward; the same spans (CommonMark's rule and the escape handling don't change). Runs of rising length, about n^1.5 before, are linear too. (2) **The writer worked out both escaped forms of the title before trying it as it is**, so a task read from a file and then completed, pinned or given a reminder paid for the repeated escaping even when its title reads back unchanged (`[a:: (b:: [c:: ` repeated and closed: 7.7 s at 50,000 characters). `formatTaskContent` now works out each escaped form only when the one before doesn't read back; the same output. Now, at 50,000 characters: the code-span scan, the reader, `escapeTitle` and the writer on these lines about 1 ms each; rising runs 2 ms at 400,000. **Tests:** 2 more reader shapes, 8 code-span shapes (escaped backticks before pairs and triples, with text, emoji, code spans and unclosed fields; rising runs and escaped rising runs at 400,000), 3 writer shapes on a line read from a file and then changed, and `escapeTitle` on escaped backticks, each under 100 ms best of three (the old code: 0.38 s to 11 s on each, so every one fails on it); the reference reader now uses a copy of the old code-span scan too (it still shares `parseLocalDateTime` and the block-ID pattern, which this change doesn't touch); the old and new code-span scans are compared on backtick-heavy and arbitrary strings, and the writer on each random line completed and pinned: identical in a one-off run of 200,000 inputs.
- **Found, not fixed (Davit, 9 Nov 2026: in 1.2.1; fixed by D-153).** `escapeFieldOpenersFully` repeats the escaping until nothing changes, and on nested field-like text (`[ a :: ` repeated before `[priority:: high]`) each pass escapes only one more opener, so `escapeTitle` takes 8 s at 50,000 characters. It runs when a typed title is saved (`model/actions.ts`), and in the writer (`formatTaskContent`, through `formatTaskLine` whenever a task read from a file is changed), which since the review reaches it only when the title as it is doesn't read back (a Flint field repeated in the line, say) and reaches the escaping of every field-like opener, the slow one on these shapes, only when escaping Flint's own fields isn't enough either. **Proposed:** its own change in 1.2.0 (the escaping found in one pass, compared with the current one like this change), since a file's line can still reach it on an ordinary action.

### D-147 — Go to from the composer's toast (CAP-05, D-98)
- **Date:** 8 Nov 2026 · **Status:** Decided (Davit, moved from 1.1.1 to 1.2.0); the new row and mini mode **Proposed**. A task the composer sends elsewhere with `##list` gets "Added to <list>" with **Go to <list>** and Undo, through Restore's path (D-98: the list opened, the task selected, in view, flashed); the same for a list's new row, and in mini mode Go to opens the main window there as Open does. No palette command (D-106): it acts on one toast's task, and the palette already goes to any list or task.

### D-148 — App icon: the master's facets at every small size (UI-07)
- **Date:** 9 Nov 2026 · **Status:** Decided (Davit: the thinner outline); the facet colours and the 16 px choice Proposed.
- **Problem (Davit):** the master logo looks like a rock, but the taskbar icon (24 px at 100 % scaling) showed about six shapes. Not the logo's nature: the small logos were a separate drawing, three even facets above the centre line and three below, all meeting it (a cut gem rather than knapped flint), with a light outline of about 1 px that took about a third of a leaf only about 8 px wide; and the 24 px drawing was stretched to every `.ico` size from 20 to 96 px.
- **Decision:** the taskbar and tray logos use **the master's 13 facets** with its uneven borders, neighbouring facets pulled further apart in value so they stay separate when tiny, a **hairline outline** (0.55–0.6 px) with the light catching the upper edge (0.8–0.9 px), drawn at **16, 24, 32 and 48 px** (new `flint-taskbar-32.svg` and `flint-taskbar-48.svg`; 32 and 48 also get the master's tile sheen). Each `.ico` size comes from the nearest drawing (20–24 from 24, 30–36 from 32, 40–96 from 48); `tray-32.png` and `tray-48.png` from the 32 and 48 px logos at 1x. The tile, rim, corner proportions and the 65 % leaf of D-74 are unchanged, and so are the in-app logos.
- **Considered and not selected:** the master's facets merged into eight at 24 px (flatter, less like rock); keeping four even facets at 16 px (crisper, but a gem again, and unlike the other sizes).

### D-149 — 1.2.0: no slash menu in a table's cells; the open items decided
- **Date:** 9 Nov 2026 · **Status:** Decided (Davit, after testing the second 1.2.0 preview).
- **The slash menu is off inside a cell.** It opened in the note at the table's hidden text (on the far right), the arrow keys moved between cells and closed it, and a click on an item did nothing. A `/` typed in a cell is now just a slash (`slash.ts`: no menu inside a table; e2e in `tables.spec.ts`). **Later:** a slash menu for cells: the arrow keys stay in the menu, it opens at the caret, and items that don't fit in a cell (/table, code blocks, headings and other blocks) are hidden.
- **The open items:** the blank line written under a table when the line under it isn't empty (a click or ↓, D-144) **stays**; the slow title escape (`escapeFieldOpenersFully`, D-146) goes into **1.2.1**; the read-only note's cell bug (a click on any cell makes the header's first cell active) goes into **the next batch**; the quoted-table misread (a row outside a quote that starts with `>` after its indent, `containerPrefix`) is for **later**.
- **Also in 1.2.0:** the highlighter's linear matchers (D-143, moved from 1.1.1).

### D-150 — CI runs once per commit: pushes to `next` and pull requests (D-145)
- **Date:** 9 Nov 2026 · **Status:** Decided (Davit).
- `ci.yml` ran three times on every release commit (the push to `next`, the fast-forward of `main`, the tag), each a new chance for a flaky test to show red with no new coverage. It now runs on pushes to `next` and on pull requests only. `release.yml` already checks for a successful `ci.yml` run by commit SHA, whatever the branch, and `main` is fast-forwarded to `next`'s green commit, so that one run is the release gate.

### D-151 — Give to Claude (TASK-18), planned for 1.3.0
- **Date:** 9 Nov 2026 · **Status:** Decided (Davit, grilled one question at a time); building after 1.2.1 (D-150's order below).
- **Why a decision:** "integrations with other apps" was a non-goal for v1 (01). This one is the smallest possible: Flint hands a documented `claude://code/new?q=…&folder=…` link to Windows and nothing else. No command or program is run, no other link is opened this way, no network request is made.
- **Measured first (Davit's machine, Electron 45.1.0, Claude Desktop 4.23118 as an MSIX package, 9 Nov 2026):** a link of 2,200 encoded characters arrived whole (the 2,083-character limit reported for `ShellExecute` doesn't apply here); 30,000 arrived and Claude kept the first **14,336** characters of `q`; 32,000 was accepted; 32,700, 33,000, 40,000 and 130,000 were refused by Windows with an error `shell.openExternal` reports (no silent loss). The limit is Windows' 32,767-character command line less Claude's path. Flint's budget is 30,000 encoded characters, the largest size seen to arrive. Georgian costs 9 encoded characters a letter, so about 3,300 letters fit; English prose and code hit Claude's 14,336 first.
- **Decided:** over either limit the prompt goes to the clipboard, said in the card before giving (not cut, not refused); a palette-style card with the folders and the count, the prompt edited in Claude's composer rather than in Flint; the prompt's form (title, notes, subtasks with their state and notes, fields stripped, attachments absolute, no preamble); folders machine-local in `settings.json` by list `id` (not the file format), recents five, the list's **Claude folder ›** menu, Shift+Enter, Settings → Claude; **G** in Navigate state; one task at a time, completed tasks too, a subtask with `Part of:`; the toast after giving; the Privacy row's added sentence (Davit's words, 02 APP-09). The details are in 02 TASK-18.
- **Not installed:** detected with `app.getApplicationNameForProtocol('claude://')` before opening, and from a refused open; a toast with Download (claude.com/download).
- **Order (Davit):** D-150's CI change, then 1.2.1 (the cell-focus race and the slow title escape), then Give to Claude as 1.3.0, its pure parts written while the table specs run.
- **Considered and not selected:** cutting a long prompt (loses the end of a thought); refusing it; an editable prompt in Flint (Claude's composer does that); the default folder in the list's frontmatter (a machine path in the markdown, a format change); a context line with list and priority.
- **Built (1.3.0):** `shared/claude.ts` (the prompt, the token estimate, the link or the clipboard), `main/claude.ts` (main takes a prompt and a folder, never a link; refuses a folder that isn't an absolute, existing one; opens only `claude://code/new?…`; copies only once the link opened; tests record instead of opening or copying, D-93), the card (`GiveToClaude.tsx`), the task's menu (so the palette's "This task"), G, the side panel, the list's Claude folder ›, the palette's Set and Forget, Settings → Claude and the Privacy sentence. The small details are Proposed (02 TASK-18). **Tests:** unit (the prompt's form, links in and out of code, the limits at their edges and Georgian over Windows' budget, the token estimate; giving, a refused link, a missing folder, the recent folders, the tolerant reader) and `claude.spec.ts` (every entry point, the exact prompt in the link, a subtask's `Part of:`, the clipboard over the limit, not installed with Download, the list's folder set from the card, the menu, the palette and Settings, no request), the card in the motion census. Found by the spec and fixed: after Shift+Enter the list's menu didn't show its new folder until a restart. **Review (one round: it opens another app, D-145), fixed:** a refresh of the window while the card was open put its pick back on the first folder (a task could go to the wrong project: the rows now follow the list's id, the pick is made once); keys during its closing could give twice or lose the toast; a rejected call left it unable to give; an attachment name with parentheses, or a link's title, made a broken path; main built the folders to save from settings read before its awaits (a folder set meanwhile was undone); a card whose task went could come back by itself; recent folders read twice in different letter case; Settings' page put Claude before Clipboard, its index after. Kept: the footer's estimate uses the folder picked (for Choose a folder… the nearest one), so near Windows' limit main may still use the clipboard, which its toast then says.

### D-152 — A table cell takes the keys in the same step it lights up (NOTE-10, D-144)
- **Date:** 9 Nov 2026 · **Status:** Done (1.2.1).
- Arriving from the note's own editor (a click, Backspace or ↓ into a table) the cell's editor took the focus a frame later (`requestAnimationFrame`), so keys typed in between went into the note, under the table; CI's slower runners did this again and again (D-145's flaky list). It now takes it in a microtask: after the note's update (focusing inside it would start another update inside that one), before the browser handles the next key. A click's caret still goes where the click was once the cell is laid out, unless keys already went in. The tests' waits for a cell's focus are gone; the two Backspace tests type in the step right after the key, and fail with a 150 ms late focus (checked). **Found by CI on the first version:** the click's caret still came a frame later, and on a slow runner a key that only moved the selection (Home, Shift+↓) came first and was undone by it. The click's caret now goes in the same microtask as the focus (`posAtCoords` lays the cell out at once); only if it can't be measured yet, a frame later, and not once a key reached the cell. Checked with that frame delayed 150 ms: the click test (a letter typed right after the click lands where the click was) and the Shift test pass.

### D-153 — The title escape stops after 16 passes (D-146, D-21)
- **Date:** 9 Nov 2026 · **Status:** Decided (Davit: the cap, not an exact one-pass rewrite); done (1.2.1).
- `escapeFieldOpenersFully` (`format/fields.ts`, used by `escapeTitle` and the writer) repeated its pass until nothing changed, and on nested field-like text (`[ a :: ` repeated before `[priority:: high]`) each pass undid one layer: 8 s at 50,000 characters. An exact one-pass version (the same output at any depth) needs an incremental re-scan across both bracket kinds, with escapes changing the candidates outwards and inwards: long and delicate work in the writer for titles nobody types.
- **Decision:** at most 16 passes; a title that still needs one more gets every bracket with `::` after it escaped instead, in one linear pass (`escapeEverything`'s openers only: unpaired backticks and a trailing `^id` stay as they are), so nothing in it can read as a field. The same output as before wherever 16 passes do (titles people type need one to three); a deeper title is escaped more than it strictly needs, reads back the same and renders the same (`\[` shows as `[`). 04 §3's least-escaping rule notes the exception.
- **Measured:** at 50,000 characters `escapeTitle` 30–50 ms and the writer 40–70 ms (were 6–9 s on each shape).
- **Tests (`fields.linear.test.ts`):** four 50,000-character shapes (both brackets, mixed, before a Flint or an unknown field) under 1 s each (the old escaping 6–9 s: every one fails on it, checked); 1 to 15 layers identical to the old escaping, 16, 17 and 40 layers fully escaped and nothing else; random nested titles identical to the old escaping wherever it took 16 passes or fewer, and always reading back; titles over 16 passes deep with backticks, backslashes and block IDs, only brackets escaped. `packages/core` passes, the format's tests also with `FLINT_FUZZ_RUNS=20000`.
- **Review (one round, the file format's, D-145):** the fallback first reused `escapeEverything` whole, which also escaped stray backticks and a trailing `^id`, so a deep title's text changed where a shallow one's didn't: now only its brackets. Declined: the exact one-pass rewrite (this decision); skipping the Flint-only passes (they never go deep: Flint's values hold no brackets).

### D-154 — A table cell's backslashes are written once (NOTE-10, D-141; CodeQL alerts #10–#12)
- **Date:** 9 Nov 2026 · **Status:** Done, for 1.3.1 (Davit's OK pending).
- **Found from CodeQL's "incomplete string escaping" alerts:** #12 (`table-view.ts`, the caret's place after typing in a cell) escaped pipes but not backslashes; #10 and #11 (`table.test.ts`'s random tables) removed every backslash first, which made them correct but meant the randomised tests never wrote a backslash into a cell. Checked through the real reader and writer: **a cell's backslashes doubled at every write** when the cell ended in a backslash or had one right before a pipe (`C:\dir\` → `C:\dir\\` → `C:\dir\\\\`…; `a\\|b` likewise), and since every keystroke in a cell writes it, typing `C:\` could show `C:\\` at once. The table's shape always held. Since 1.2.0.
- **Cause:** `escapeCell` doubled backslashes before a pipe and at the end, which reading (each `\|` read as `|`, as GFM shows it) never undid.
- **Fix:** one writer, the inverse of reading: a `|` after an even run of backslashes (none, or `\\`) gets one more; one already escaped is left as it is; every other backslash is written as typed. A cell ending in an odd run of backslashes right against its pipe gets a space between, so it can't escape that pipe. The caret's place (#12) uses the same writer, so a backslash before it no longer shifts it. `hasRowPipe` reads a pipe after `\\` as real (it took `\\|` for escaped).
- **What can't be kept:** a `\|` typed in a cell reads back as `|`: in markdown it is the escaped pipe, and GFM tables have no way to keep a backslash right before an escaped pipe and show it. Nothing that renders changes. Cells already doubled by 1.2.0 to 1.3.0 can't be told from intended `\\`, so they aren't repaired: remove the extra backslashes once; they don't come back (1.3.1's release notes say so).
- **Tests:** a Windows path, a UNC path, trailing and doubled backslashes written as typed and stable over repeated writes; pipes after any run of backslashes; the space before a pipe; the markdown copy; `hasRowPipe`; the randomised tables now write backslashes (with Flint's own writer) and check every cell reads back exactly as set and that writing a cell's own text back changes nothing, in any table (7 of these tests fail on the old writer); `tables.spec.ts` types a Windows path and a pipe into a cell and edits it again (fails on the old writer).
- **Review (one round, data and format, D-145):** `hasRowPipe` (fixed, above); the e2e undo waited for nothing (each Ctrl+Z now waits for its write). Kept: the protective space stays if the backslash is later removed (ordinary padding).

### D-155 — CodeQL on pushes to `next` too
- **Date:** 9 Nov 2026 · **Status:** Done (Davit: the default setup turned off, `codeql.yml` pushed after 1.3.1).
