Until `codeql.yml` reaches `main` with the next release, `main` and the weekly scan have no CodeQL run (a workflow runs from the branch that has it; the schedule from the default branch).
- GitHub's default CodeQL setup scans `main`, pull requests and once a week, so D-154's alerts showed up only after the release. `codeql.yml` (advanced setup) scans pushes to `main` and `next`, pull requests and weekly, with the default setup's languages (JavaScript/TypeScript, the workflows) and query suite; actions pinned by commit SHA (`github/codeql-action` v4.39.1, the newest at least 7 days old, D-30). GitHub processes it only with the default setup turned off (a repository setting, Davit's OK).

### D-156 — The flaky-test batch: each test waits for what it checks (D-145)
- **Date:** 9 Nov 2026 · **Status:** Done (Davit's batch); two app fixes for 1.3.1 (Davit's OK pending).
- **Linear-time checks (unit):** tests judged "linear, not quadratic" by a wall-clock limit on one input (under 100–500 ms), which a busy or slow machine failed with nothing wrong (`shorthand.test.ts`: 357 ms against 250 ms; the table "Excel paste"). The shorthand parse, the task-line reader and writer, the code-span scan, the title escape and the table model now use `expectLinear` (`packages/core/test/scaling.ts`): the same operation on an input of size n and 4n, the ratio the median of three samples (each the best of three runs), under 10 (linear about 4, quadratic 16 and more); the smaller input grows until it takes at least 4 ms, so the timer's resolution never decides. Input that makes the code allocate much (a row per line break) is compared with a baseline doing only that allocation (the garbage collector's share grows faster than the input whatever the code does). The helper's own test: a quadratic loop fails it, a linear one (also made three times slower) passes. **Measured, not guessed:** among the other workers the wall clock measured them taking the CPU (a linear ratio reached 16), and thread CPU time on Windows moves in 15.6 ms ticks; so the timing checks moved to `*.timing.test.ts` files, run after the other tests one file at a time (Vitest projects `unit` and `timing`; `npm test` runs both, `test:unit` and `test:timing` each; CI's `npm test` unchanged). The paste, stack-trace, highlighter and time-parser checks keep their generous limits (a few milliseconds against 100–250 ms, never failed), now in that pass, alone; one that fails is moved to `expectLinear` then.
- **App fix found by it:** `parseTsv` (a paste from Excel or Sheets into a table) built a quoted cell a character at a time, which grew superlinearly on one large quoted cell (5, 7, then 26 times from n to 4n as n grew); now its pieces between quotes, joined once (D-144's "linear whatever it holds" holds).
- **Half-parsed documents (unit):** the editor's tests parsed with a time budget (`ensureSyntaxTree(…, 1000–5000)`) and ignored whether it finished: on a busy machine a test checked half a syntax tree ("finds the table around a position", "knows the cell a position is in", the reveal rule's examples). `parseFully` parses to the end and fails loudly if it can't.
- **Cleanup races (unit):** `workspace.test.ts` removed the test's folder while the workspace was still writing its bookkeeping (`.flint`, a moment after a change): ENOTEMPTY. Each test's workspaces are now waited for (`settled()`, as a quit waits, D-138) before the folder goes. The D-153 property took 1.5 s of its 5 s budget alone (each case escapes a 17-layer chain twice): a quarter of the runs.
- **App fix found by it (smoke M8 step 2, twice on CI):** a list opened right after the workspace re-read its folder could show a blank page. When a file can't be read as text just then (held by another program, as a virus scanner holds a new file on CI, or empty while one writes it), the folder scan gave its list no ID, and the re-read took that: the list became `file:<name>` until the next read, while the window asked for it by its ID and got nothing (the CI snapshot: the sidebar showing the list, the page empty). Now a known list whose file couldn't be read keeps its ID and mode until it can (`unsettled`); a file really edited reads as text and takes its new header as before. And a list's file read empty while another program writes it keeps what Flint had, read-only ("being written", shown as the window shows any read-only list) until it reads with text again: before, it loaded as an empty list that could be edited, and an edit would have written the list back without its header over the other program's write (the review round found it). Test: a list's file read empty mid-write keeps its ID, its tasks and is read-only, then is as before once written (fails on the old code: `file:Palette`; it also caught the list staying read-only when it came back unchanged).
- **End-to-end:** the windows are off-screen in tests, and on a busy runner Chromium can take them for occluded or backgrounded, stop drawing frames and slow timers to one a second: a click waiting for a frame hung 30 s (smoke "M7 step 2"), mini mode's frame recorder never ran (mini.spec:849, a minute without an answer). In test runs only (development builds, `FLINT_E2E=1`) the windows now draw and keep their timers as visible ones do (Chromium's switches: no native occlusion, no backgrounding, no timer throttling). The pin's pop (onboarding:101) is waited for as the animation it is (its `animationstart`), not as a class there for about a second that a polled check could miss; the popup is acted on once it has taken in being shown (the e2e hook `showPopup` now resolves on the popup's own focused-frame report, R-1): a late answer to the test before's capture emptied the field just pasted into (smoke "pasted or selected text…": `" the"` left), the same race as editing.spec's `" x"`. For a user the answer always comes before the next "shown" (one channel, in order), but the popup now empties only if it still holds exactly what was sent, so text typed meanwhile can never go; a new note's text once its editor has the keys (smoke 643); mini's recording waits for the tab to change and the recorder to finish instead of fixed pauses. Not reproduced locally under full load (20 runs each, all cores busy): the popup's " x" and the smoke 643 file check; their waits are the races in their steps.
- **Proof:** the unit and timing passes five rounds in a row, no failure; the specs changed, locally; on CI the full suite several times on one commit (see the handoff). The first five CI runs of `8fdbd27` found two more, fixed: mini's recording now waits for the Note tab drawn in full (its head's ⋯), not only switched to (my own first fix had made it switch back too soon: the frames never saw the head); `tables.spec.ts`'s `tableBox` keeps the box its poll saw once the same twice running (asked for again, a table drawn anew meanwhile had none).
- **Not changed:** about 40 fixed pauses remain in the end-to-end specs, most of them "nothing happens within X" checks, motion recordings and settling before a measurement; one fails, it is made to wait for its condition then. `table-view.ts`'s edge drag gives its parse 200 ms a step (on a very busy machine a drag of several columns could stop short); not from a flaky test, left as it is.

### D-157 — Ctrl+Alt+X captures the selection into mini mode's note (MINI-02), 1.4.0
- **Date:** 10 Nov 2026 · **Status:** Decided (Davit, grilled one question at a time); built in 1.4.0 together with D-158.
- **What:** a fourth global hotkey, Ctrl+Alt+X by default, runs CAP-07's copy step (the keys released, a simulated Ctrl+C, the clipboard put back; one copy at a time, shared with Ctrl+Shift+X) and adds the text to the end of the note mini's Note tab shows, as a paragraph of its own (a blank line before it unless the note is empty or ends in one), code fenced as the popup fences it (`noteAppend` in core, unit-tested); with no note yet, a quick note is made. Saved at once, not after the editor's 600 ms, so no read of the file can show without it.
- **Q1, focus (Davit: "focus only when mini was hidden"):** a hidden mini opens on the Note tab and takes the keyboard, the caret after the text; one already on screen gets the text and the focus stays in the source app (collecting several snippets in a row). My recommendation had been to take the focus always, as the popup does; his choice keeps the flow.
- **Q3, where:** the end of whatever the Note tab shows (the remembered quick note, or a note opened with Open a note…), never at the caret. Nothing selected: the Note tab, no message (D-68). An admin window: the popup's hint as a toast.
- **Q4, the default:** Ctrl+Alt+X kept as mini's pair to Ctrl+Alt+Space (D-109's Alt exception; the Settings note on saving a hotkey with Alt says so). Caveat on record: on layouts where AltGr stands for Ctrl+Alt, AltGr+X typed in another app fires the hotkey and swallows the key; Davit checked the Georgian layout (AltGr+X types a rarely used character, acceptable) and asked for the caveat in the docs and in the hotkey's Settings note, so anyone who needs that character rebinds it.
- **How:** the append is done by mini's own editor, not by a file write from main: one window edits a note at a time (D-109), so main queues the capture (`Mini.capture`, kept until the page takes it with `takeCaptures`, also when the page is only starting), the page switches to the Note tab and the `NoteEditor` inserts once the note's text has loaded (an insertion into an editor still waiting for its text would be saved over it) and, if the main window edits the note, once it has claimed it. `MarkdownEditor` exposes its view (`onView`) for that.
- **Not a palette command:** mini's own actions have none (D-122) and the main window has no selection in another app to capture.
- **Tests:** `noteAppend` (separators, fencing, an empty selection); the registry's fourth name (differs from the other three; a rebind); the settings default; end to end in `mini.spec.ts` through the `captureIntoMini` hook (the copy step after the OS's part, as `showPopup` does for the popup): the paragraph and the fence in the file, the focus kept when mini was open and taken when it was hidden, no message for nothing selected, the toast for an admin window.

### D-158 — Mini mode's second tab shows any task list (MINI-03), 1.4.0
- **Date:** 10 Nov 2026 · **Status:** Decided (Davit, grilled); built in 1.4.0.
- **What:** the Inbox tab reads the name of the list it shows (Inbox by default) with a chevron. **Q2, clicks (recommended, accepted):** a click on the tab while another tab is active switches to it; a click on the active tab, or on the chevron at any time, opens the list picker (CAP-11's `openListPicker`: a search field, task lists most recently used first, D-25); Ctrl+2 only switches. Picked from another tab, the tab shows the list.
- **Q5, the picker and the memory (recommended, accepted):** Inbox in the picker to come back, the list shown left out, no "New list" row (new option `newList: false`; a list to show, not a place to send to), notes left out. Kept in `mini-state.json` (`listId`, null for Inbox) beside the tab and note; a list that is gone falls back to Inbox. Not a recent use for `##list` and Move.
- **Behaviour:** the composer adds to the chosen list ("Add to <list>…"), keys, drag, Alt+↑/↓, menu and Open as on Inbox; open top-level tasks; "<list> is empty.". Motion (D-89): the new name fades in (`mini-tab-name-in`), the rows arrive as a page does (the view keyed by the list).
- **Tests:** end to end in `mini.spec.ts`: the picker from the active tab and from the chevron, Inbox and the shown list in and out of it, no New list, the rows, the placeholder, the composer adding to the chosen list, the state file, Inbox back.

### D-159 — Whatever opens outside mini's card takes clicks (MINI-01), 1.4.0
- **Date:** 10 Nov 2026 · **Status:** Done (1.4.0, found by Davit on the 1.4.0 preview).
- Mini's window is click-through except over the card, its handles and open menus (D-110); the page told main the card's box and every `.fl-menu`, once, a frame after the DOM changed. A picker (`.fl-picker`: the list picker below a short card, Open a note…) was never counted, so the part of it beyond the card's edge passed clicks to the app underneath; a menu that grew or moved after it opened kept its first box. Now every child of the body beside the app's root (menus, pickers, their ghosts) and the editor's tooltips (`.cm-tooltip`, the slash menu) count, measured again every frame while any is open (they place themselves, grow with their rows, fade out), and only a change is sent to main; the region shrinks back when the last one closes. The tests read the reported surface (`miniSurface` hook): the list picker (also after its rows changed), the ⋯ menu and a row's menu are covered, and one rect remains when they close. The four corners are a hand check (the DOM logic is the same; only where a picker places itself differs).

### D-160 — Mini's header never overflows; the reset button by what is visible (MINI-01), 1.4.0
- **Date:** 10 Nov 2026 · **Status:** Done (1.4.0, both found by Davit on the 1.4.0 preview).
- **The header:** with a long list name in the second tab (D-158) and a narrow card, the pin and × were pushed out of the card and the reset button vanished. Now the tabs' box and the list tab shrink (`min-width: 0`, the list tab the only flex item that gives way, its name an ellipsis, never narrower than the ellipsis and the chevron), the other tabs and the reset, pin and × buttons keep their size, and the minimum width is 320 px (was 300), which fits the header at its shortest. Test: a list named "Service metrics api gateway" shown at the minimum width: the header doesn't scroll, the three buttons are inside the card, the name is cut.
- **The reset button:** Today and the list tab are as tall as their content up to the height limit, so a limit dragged taller over short content changed nothing visible yet showed Reset size. The button now shows only when the card's visible size differs from the default: the width, and the height only where the limit shows (the Note tab, which takes the limit; content taller than the default limit; a limit smaller than the default that cuts the content). The limit itself is still saved, so the Note tab shows it, and there the button appears. Test: a taller limit over a short Today leaves the height and no button; the Note tab is taller and has it; Reset size brings 460 back.
- **Found by the tests, fixed with it:** `mini-state.json` was written once per change, side by side when two came in one tick (the tab and the list chosen together from the chevron, D-158), and the older write could land last (the list tab's test saw `"work"` after Inbox was chosen). Saves are now coalesced and run one after another, each with what the state holds when it runs.

### D-161 — Google Drive sync (SYNC-01) in 1.5.0
- **Date:** 10 Nov 2026 · **Status:** Decided (Davit's brief and mockup 13; the technical choices grilled one question at a time, Q1–Q9); details marked Proposed in 02 SYNC-01. Supersedes D-78's "Later" for Google Drive (OneDrive stays Later) and 01's non-goal "Syncing of any kind".
- **Davit's brief:** Settings → Sync (account, What syncs, Chosen items, Drive folder, the privacy note); first-time setup "What should sync?" (Only what I choose preselected); the sync status button left of the sort and + buttons with its popover (before connecting it opens Settings → Sync); one sidebar with clouds on synced items and "Synced only / Local only" in the sort menu; "Sync with Google Drive" in the right-click menu of lists, notes and groups; Today shows everything on the PC; a visible Flint folder of plain markdown in the user's Drive, `drive.file`, OAuth for desktop with PKCE and a loopback redirect, tokens in `safeStorage`, no Flint server; the local folder the source of truth; automatic sync and Sync now; tasks merged by ID, notes as text keeping both versions; deletes to Trash everywhere; attachments inside the folder sync; linked large files don't, with the crossed-out cloud and the two explanations (the device name in the sync bookkeeping); snapshots local; stop syncing per PC with "Also remove from Drive"; removed from Drive elsewhere: keep the copy, stop, "No longer synced: removed from Drive on another device"; deleting is not unsyncing; the Privacy statement and the network rules updated; tests against a fake Drive; Google's side set up by Davit with a walkthrough; a local preview, release after his OK.
- **Google's side (checked 10 Nov 2026):** desktop clients are public; with PKCE the client secret is optional at the token exchange and refresh, so none ships; `drive.file` is a non-sensitive scope (basic verification only); in **Testing** a refresh token expires after 7 days, so the consent screen is published **In production** (Davit: users only sign in; the client ID is his one-time setup).
- **Q1, what comes down (recommended, accepted):** "Only what I choose" applies both ways: chosen local items go up, items already in Drive come down only when picked; Choose… lists On this PC and In Drive, not on this PC, grouped as the sidebar (designed in the mockup's style, Proposed); stopping on one PC also means it isn't downloaded there again. Mockup fixes accepted: neutral names, correct counts ("3 lists, 1 note and 1 group"), the real popover shows only the current state, **Connect Google Drive** before connecting.
- **Q2, first meeting (recommended, accepted):** Inbox merges through its fixed `id: inbox`; same-name lists with different IDs stay separate, the incoming one renamed "Groceries 2"; colliding task IDs are two tasks, the incoming one re-IDed; new task IDs get 6 characters (Proposed).
- **Q3, what goes to Drive (recommended, accepted):** Drive mirrors the local layout (attachments in `Flint/attachments/`); sync's bookkeeping in `Flint/.flint-sync/` (groups, deletion records, linked files' device and size, per-task `created`/`lastTouched`); synced groups carry name and members; the sidebar order, Today's manual order and the rollover day stay per PC; `.flint/` is never uploaded.
- **Q4, the same task changed on two PCs (recommended, accepted):** done wins; priority, reminder, muted and pinned by the later `lastTouched`; a title conflict keeps the other title as the body's first line, marked with the device; the body merged as text, both versions kept on a conflict; subtasks by ID; the later move wins; edited beats deleted; moved-and-edited moves with the edit. **Davit:** guard against wrong clocks: a `lastTouched` later than Drive's upload time counts as that time, a local one later than now as now (accepted).
- **Q5, a conflict in text (recommended, accepted, with Davit's additions):** the other version inline below this PC's, in a `> [!conflict] Also changed on <device>, <time>` callout; in Flint a card with **Use <device>'s**, **Keep mine**, **Keep both** (Davit: buttons name the source), one undo step each; "1 conflict" in the note's header, a marker on task rows with a conflicted body, and the conflicts listed in the status popover, clickable (Davit); never conflict-copy files.
- **Q6, "Also remove from Drive" (recommended, accepted):** unticked by default; ticked, the button reads **Stop syncing everywhere**; removed items go to Drive's trash; a synced group takes its lists along, named in the dialog.
- **Q7, builds and the client ID (recommended, accepted):** the client ID committed (public by design); the installed build syncs with `My Drive › Flint`, the development build with `My Drive › Flint (development)`, connected separately; tests only on the fake Drive, the network test with zero endpoints; no sync in the benchmark build; Google's hosts only from sync's own session while connected or signing in; the sign-in in the default browser with a loopback redirect open only during sign-in. *Correction while writing 05:* Flint itself never loads `accounts.google.com` (the browser does), so `SYNC_HOSTS` is `oauth2.googleapis.com` and `www.googleapis.com` only.
- **Q8, when Drive goes wrong (recommended, accepted):** local data is never changed by any of these; the folder is found by its Drive ID (renames and moves are fine); a trashed folder pauses sync with **Restore it in Drive** / **Start a new folder**, never read as removal; revoked access: **Sign in again**; Drive full: text and bookkeeping first, then the smallest attachments, naming what didn't fit; offline: retried by itself with back-off.
- **Q9, switching What syncs (recommended, accepted):** Chosen → Everything previews the counts and clears the stopped-here list; Everything → Chosen keeps the current set, only new items stay local; under Everything new items sync at once; an empty quick note is never uploaded; a list moved into a synced group starts syncing (toast with Undo), moved out it keeps syncing on its own.
- **Proposed (settled in writing, not asked):** sync runs 5 s after the last change, every 3 minutes, on waking and when the network returns; a list or note restored from Trash syncs again; a list deleted on one PC and edited on another stays (edited beats deleted); renamed on two PCs, the later rename wins; Disconnect revokes the token and changes nothing local or in Drive; a remote change reaches an open note without moving the cursor (DATA-06); the sort menu's Show filter only while connected; changing the Flint folder keeps the account and asks What should sync? for the new folder; `removed.json` records kept 90 days; the `.flint-sync` JSON written with a revision check.
- **Found while building (Proposed):** the callout's header also says how many lines above are this PC's version and whose: `> [!conflict] Also changed on DAVIT-LAPTOP, 10 Nov 14:02 (the 1 line above is WORK-PC's)`. Without it, Use DAVIT-LAPTOP's couldn't know where this PC's version starts, and on DAVIT-LAPTOP itself "Keep mine" would mean the wrong text; with both names the card's buttons read right on every PC (on DAVIT-LAPTOP: Use mine / Keep WORK-PC's / Keep both). A task ID used twice in one PC's folder (an Explorer copy, D-23) is kept apart in a merge, never merged away (a bug the engine tests found).
- **Q7 corrected (Davit, 10 Nov 2026): the client secret ships, injected at build time.** Google's token endpoint refuses a desktop client's code exchange without the client secret (`invalid_request: client_secret is missing.`), PKCE or not, although its page calls the secret optional (others reported the same in 2026). For a desktop app it can't be secret and grants nothing by itself (no access to the Cloud console or anyone's data; at worst another program could pose as Flint on a consent screen, or spend the project's free API quota; the answer is a new secret). Davit chose build-time injection over committing it: the repository secret `GOOGLE_CLIENT_SECRET` for releases, a git-ignored `.env.local` locally. A failed sign-in now shows Google's reason in Settings → Sync, and Flint comes back to the front after the browser.
- **Found on Davit's first real sign-in (10 Nov 2026):** the loopback listener read its own port after closing and crashed main when the browser came back (fixed; `oauth.test.ts` drives the round trip); Settings → Sync's privacy note is the card's last band, edge to edge, as in the mockup.
- **Before release, second round (Davit, 10 Nov 2026):** Settings → Sync loses the privacy band (the mockup's last row; no other section has one, and About's Privacy row says it); the syncing arrow is drawn higher (y 10 to 15) and moves less, so it stays inside the cloud, and the "!" of needs attention is raised (y 9 to 15.5) for the same reason; the README and the site mention sync (the site says Windows 11, as the README does).
- **Before release (Davit, 10 Nov 2026):** menu dividers now show (they were drawn in a tone almost the menu's own, so the sort menu's two groups ran together; every menu gets the visible line, `--fl-edge-float`); the cloud's marks are centred on its outline (x 12.6; the arrow sat at 12, the check at 12.25: the cloud is not symmetrical); Open in Google Drive for a synced list or note; the account's Google profile picture is not shown (Proposed: the letter tile stays, as in the mockup; a picture would mean one more Google host, an image cache and a remote image in the window, for little).
- **Alternatives not selected:** everything in Drive always comes down (Q1: a work PC would get every personal list); merging lists by name (Q2: unrelated lists mixed); uploading `.flint/` whole (Q3: leaks unsynced names, the order flip-flops); the later change wins for the whole task (Q4: drops the other side's edit); Git-style markers or conflict-copy files (Q5); ticked by default (Q6: contradicts "Other devices keep syncing it"); the client ID injected from a CI secret (Q7: no more private, harder local builds); a trashed folder read as removal (Q8: one misclick unsyncs everything everywhere).

### D-162 — The website flintnotes.app (WEB-01), with 1.5.0
- **Date:** 10 Nov 2026 · **Status:** Decided (Davit); details Proposed in 02 WEB-01.
- **Why:** Google won't publish the OAuth app to In production (D-161's 7-day sign-ins otherwise) without a home page and privacy policy on a domain the developer owns; github.com isn't Davit's. Davit bought **flintnotes.app** (the plain "Flint" names are taken; the hyphenated flint-notes.app was free too, but a hyphen is easy to drop and leaves the plain name for anyone) and made **flintnotes.support@gmail.com** as the consent screen's support address (it is shown to everyone who connects).
- **Scope (Davit: "yes that scope is fine"):** one landing page (hero, screenshots, the features, the privacy promise, Download for Windows from GitHub Releases, a footer) and `/privacy/`; no blog, docs or changelog pages for now.
- **Look (Davit's wish):** "a cool design", NestJS's site as inspiration, the app's screenshots as assets. Recorded as: its structure and rhythm, never its brand; Flint's grayscale tokens and fonts; screenshots rendered from the app on neutral sample data.
- **Proposed, then changed by Davit (10 Nov 2026):** GitHub Pages from `site/` in this repository, deployed on pushes to `next` or `main` touching `site/` (first proposed: `main` only, so the site would change with releases; Davit chose to publish from `next` so the site and Google's Branding links work before 1.5.0 is released); no build step, no dependencies, no analytics or third-party requests. The app's name stays Flint; the domain is only its address.
- **Terms page (Davit, 10 Nov 2026, after the release):** `/terms/` added for the consent screen's Terms of Service link (with the brand verified, Google showed "Learn why you're not seeing links to Flint's Privacy Policy or Terms of Service"); the wording is Proposed (02 WEB-01): MIT, as is, your content yours, Drive under Google's terms. Linked from the other pages' footers.

### D-163 — 1.5.1: the sidebar's clouds only on conflicts; timeline rows that share a time; long list names in Today
- **Date:** 11 Nov 2026 · **Status:** Decided (Davit's feedback on 1.5.0; grilled one question at a time, Q1–Q2).
- **Q1, the sidebar's clouds (SYNC-01; recommended, accepted):** with everything synced every row carried a cloud, which looked bad. A synced item now carries no mark; only an item with an unresolved conflict shows the cloud with "!" (a group's header too, while one of its lists has one), so the mark means "look here". Which items sync is in the header line ("Synced · just now"), the sort menu's Show filter and the status popover. *Not selected:* no cloud at all (a conflict would only show in the popover and the item's header); the cloud on hover (noise while moving through the sidebar).
- **Q2, the timeline (TODAY-03; recommended, accepted):** reminders were pushed down where they would overlap, so two at 14:00 drifted onto 13:00 and 15:00. Reminders too close to show apart at the rail's scale now share one row at the earliest's time: the next one shows if it is among them (else the earliest), with a "+N" badge whose menu lists all of them, each with its time and list. *Not selected:* only exactly equal times (14:00 and 14:05 are about a pixel apart on a long day and would still drift).
- **Small fixes:** a reminder card's list chip keeps a long name on one line with an ellipsis (it wrapped and spilled out of the chip); Overdue's and Captured today's list names shorten the same way. The +N menu lists every reminder of the row, the shown one too (Proposed: one place that says what is at that time). *Changed by D-169: only the ones behind the shown one.*

### D-164 — 1.5.1: an overdue reminder on the timeline isn't struck through
- **Date:** 11 Nov 2026 · **Status:** Decided (Davit: a passed reminder whose task isn't done looked completed). The timeline struck through every passed reminder; now only a done task's is. A passed reminder on an open task is **overdue**: behind the now line on `--fl-ground-well`, not struck, its title `--fl-text-secondary` (as the Overdue card's rows) and the white overdue dot (REM-11's language, no red; Proposed). Dragged over the past, a reminder still reads as past (dropped there it slides back).

### D-165 — Flint for Android, a companion app (MOB-*)
- **Date:** 11 Nov 2026 · **Status:** Decided (Davit; grilled one question at a time, Q1–Q8, with the mockup `design/mockups/14-android.html` reviewed and corrected); details Proposed in 02 §13. Android first (Davit's phone, quick to test); iPhone later from the same code. Nothing in 01's non-goals forbade a phone app; this is the decision that adds one.
- **Q1, what it is (recommended, accepted):** a **companion**, not the full app: quick capture to Inbox (the + button, the "New task" app shortcut, sharing to Flint from other apps), Today, checking tasks off, reading and editing lists and notes, shorthand while typing (CAP-03), link patterns (NOTE-07), reminders as notifications, Google Drive sync. **Davit:** without sync it is a small Flint of its own (creating lists and notes, Trash, search). *Not on the phone:* mini mode, Give to Claude, global hotkeys, capture-selection, the context panel. **Davit, about the widget:** none (not faster than opening an app that starts on capture with the keyboard up); the long-press "New task" shortcut gives the same speed (Proposed).
- **Reminders on two devices (Davit):** both ring; there is no server to decide which. Done or Snooze on either cancels the other's once they sync. They are **local notifications** the phone schedules itself, not push (push needs a server).
- **What the phone syncs (Davit):** it chooses, as a second PC does (SYNC-01's What should sync?).
- **Q2, the technology (recommended, accepted):** **React Native with Expo**: native views, gestures and Android's own parts through native modules, and `packages/core` (format, shorthand, sync engine, merges) runs unchanged, so one implementation of the file format is fuzz-tested for both apps; the iPhone later is mostly the same app. *Not selected:* Capacitor (Davit wants native, not the web app in a shell); Kotlin with Jetpack Compose, or Kotlin Multiplatform (a second copy of the format and the merges that must write the same bytes: one mismatch destroys content, which 04 never allows; a third copy for the iPhone); Flutter (a rewrite in Dart, not native views).
- **Q3, the note editor (Davit: "I still want the full editor"; recommended, accepted):** **CodeMirror 6 in a web view** (an Expo DOM component), with the desktop's live preview shared (`packages/editor`): rendered markdown, real checkboxes, tables, images, link patterns, the conflict card, and the text kept byte for byte. Everything around it is native, including **a formatting bar on the keyboard** (checklist, bullets, numbers, indent, outdent, heading, bold, photo, undo), Samsung Notes as Davit's model. No right-click menu or slash menu on the phone. *Not selected:* a native rich-text editor (`react-native-enriched-markdown`: lists and headings not yet in its editor, and it rewrites markdown through its own model, normalising what it doesn't know); a native field with live styling (`react-native-live-markdown`: safe, but no real checkboxes or hidden markers). Android keyboards are the known weak spot of web editors (Gboard more than Samsung's), so the editor is the first thing tested on Davit's phone (R-A2, 06 §7); the live-styling field is the fallback if it fails.
- **Q4, where files live (recommended, accepted):** **Flint's private app storage**, the same folder layout and atomic writes as on the PC; **Settings → Export** copies everything as markdown to a folder the user picks; Android's automatic backup **off** (it would send notes to Google without the user choosing it). A first-use line says uninstalling deletes what isn't synced or exported. This is principle 7's phone exception (01). *Not selected:* a folder picked with Android's Storage Access Framework (every read and write through the document system, slow over many files, no write-then-rename, open bugs in Expo's writes there; Later as an option at most); "All files access" (Play allows it only for file managers and the like).
- **Q5, undo and Trash (recommended, accepted):** an undo bar for each task and list action ("Task completed · Undo", one step); the editor's own undo and redo; the same 30-day Trash in the same format (DATA-02), with a phone screen (by day, Restore, Delete now on long-press, Empty Trash), no Archived tab; deleting a list or note asks first, tasks don't.
- **Q6, attachments (recommended, accepted):** shown (images inline and full screen, videos with Android's player, other files with Open with), added (camera, gallery, sharing to Flint) and synced as on the PC; originals never resized; a PC's linked large file shows as "kazbegi-flight.mp4 · only on DAVIT-LAPTOP". No Wi-Fi-only setting in the first version (Later if mobile data matters).
- **Q7, navigation (recommended, accepted):** a bottom bar **Today · Inbox · Lists** and a **+** button for capture on Today, Inbox and every list (in a list it captures into that list); Lists is the PC's sidebar as a screen, with search at the top, Settings and Trash at the bottom; the app opens on the last tab. *Not selected:* a side drawer (Material's current guidance moves phones off drawers; it hides Today and Inbox behind a swipe).
- **The mockup (Davit, 11 Nov 2026):** accepted with one correction: **task details** lead with the notes. Priority, list and reminder are small chips under the title; the notes card fills the screen with "Tap to edit" in its corner and opens the full editor as its own screen; subtasks below.
- **Q8, search (recommended, accepted):** one search screen, from the Lists field and Today's search icon, finding list and note names, task titles and bodies, and note text, grouped (Lists and notes · Tasks · In notes) with the words highlighted; a result opens where it matches. It finds, it doesn't run commands, so the phone is an exception to D-106 (every action there has a button, swipe or menu).
- **Versions (Davit asked what is standard):** there is none; both are common (Obsidian shares one line, many apps number each on its own). **Separate:** the phone starts at **1.0.0**, tags `android-v<version>`, its own release notes and What's new; Android's internal `versionCode` increases with every build. Compatibility between devices is not the app's number but what each can read: D-166. *Not selected:* one shared line (a phone-only fix would push an empty desktop update or leave gaps in the numbering).
- **Proposed (settled in writing):** app ID `app.flintnotes` (permanent once installed); Android 12 or newer; first releases as an APK on GitHub Releases installed by hand, the Play Store later (a personal developer account needs a closed test with 12 testers for 14 days first); Settings says when a new version is out, checking only GitHub's release listing (APP-09's one network use besides sync); Google sign-in through Android's authorization API (no refresh token or client secret on the phone).
- **Risk-first (06 §7):** R-A1, Drive across the two apps: `drive.file` is per app, and whether the Android client counts as the same app as the desktop's (same Cloud project) is not stated by Google; if it doesn't, the phone can't see the desktop's files and the plan changes. R-A2, the editor on Davit's keyboards. R-A3, reminders on time with the app closed.

### D-166 — Sync checks what each device can read (SYNC-02)
- **Date:** 11 Nov 2026 · **Status:** Decided (Davit: with separate phone versions, sync must check compatibility and say clearly when versions don't match, for the future; recommended, accepted, Q9 of D-165's grilling).
- **The gap:** each file's `flint: 1` was already checked (a newer one opens read-only, 04 §3), but sync's bookkeeping in Drive carries `flint: 1` that nothing read, and a synced file with a newer `flint:` would have been merged and rewritten by an older app.
- **Decided:** the check compares what each app can read, never app version numbers (phone 1.0.3 beside desktop 1.6.2 is normal). `.flint-sync/compat.json` holds the **sync version** (1 today) and a **device list** (name, app and version, the highest sync version it supports, last sync). A Drive newer than the app pauses sync with nothing read, merged or written: "Needs attention · Update Flint to keep syncing" naming the device and app that raised it, and **Update Flint**. A synced file newer than the app is never merged or overwritten there: downloaded as it is, read-only with "Needs a newer Flint", only that item paused. Before an app raises the sync version it warns which devices would stop. New optional fields don't raise it; apps keep fields they don't know when they rewrite a bookkeeping file. Settings → Sync lists the devices.
- **When:** in the next desktop release, before the phone exists. Desktop 1.5.0–1.5.1 have no check and never will, so the sync version must stay 1 while they are around (the desktop updates itself on quit, so they fade quickly).
- *Not selected:* comparing app versions; the check without the device list (no warning before a raise, no overview).

### D-167 — 1.5.1, the timeline after Davit's preview: an hour apart, the now line under the rows
- **Date:** 11 Nov 2026 · **Status:** Decided (Davit's feedback on the 1.5.1 preview). Short entry.
- **An hour apart, two rows:** the rail's least scale is one row and its gap per hour (29 px), so reminders an hour or more apart always have rows of their own; a long day grows past the old 300 px cap for it (10–22 is about 350 px). Only reminders under an hour apart share a row (D-163).
- **The now line under the rows:** it crossed a row's text and read as a strike-through; it is now drawn under the rows, its time pill over everything. A "+N" badge the pill would cover moves to the pill's left.
- **Overdue (D-164, revised):** faded as a passed reminder is (`--fl-ground-well`, `--fl-text-faint`), not struck through, with the white overdue dot.
- **Kept:** a shared row still shows the next reminder when it is among its reminders (else the earliest); the earlier ones are in its "+N" menu (Davit: "the current behaviour is fine").

### D-168 — 1.5.1: the now line along a row's edge
- **Date:** 11 Nov 2026 · **Status:** Decided (Davit, second preview: under the rows the line was hidden by the row it fell in). Short entry. Where the now line would cross a row it runs along the row's edge instead: above a row still to come, below a passed one (by the reminder the row shows), in the 3 px gap between rows, so it is always seen and never reads as a strike-through; the pill still says the exact time. Replaces D-167's "under the rows".

### D-169 — 1.5.1: the "+N" menu lists only the reminders behind the shown one
- **Date:** 11 Nov 2026 · **Status:** Decided (Davit, before tagging 1.5.1; recommended, accepted). Short entry. A timeline row's "+N" menu lists the N reminders behind the one the row shows, not that one again, so the badge's number and the menu agree. Replaces D-163's Proposed "the shown one too".

### D-170 — Focus: a Pomodoro timer with its own sprint of tasks (FOCUS-01 to FOCUS-06), 1.6.0
- **Date:** 11 Nov 2026 · **Status:** Decided (Davit's brief, grilled Q1–Q13); mockup 15 approved by Davit as drawn ("build it exactly like that"), then the build. Davit's model is Pomofocus (pomofocus.io), a browser Pomodoro timer with a task list under it: familiar to its users, without copying its look.
- **The non-goals (01):** Davit overrides "time features beyond reminders" for this one feature, with the limit written into 01: **a timer and an alarm sound, nothing more**: no reports, no time logged against tasks, no history kept per task. "Reminders are the ceiling" stays for everything else; the rejected "Now" pin stays rejected (Q9).
- **Q1, what Focus holds (Davit; my recommendation, Focus = the pinned tasks, not selected):** pinning and Focus are different. A pin says what to work on today (Today); Focus is a small separate app inside Flint for what you do *now*, in timed sessions, and tasks are **sent** to it. Membership is not in the task's line: the **sprint** is a list of task IDs in `.flint/focus.json` (04), like Today's order, so the file format doesn't change and losing the file only empties the sprint. Outside the Focus page nothing shows that a task is in it (only the menu's label, Q5). *Not selected:* the pinned tasks (one concept, but Focus would be today's whole list); a `[focus:: true]` field (a format change).
- **Q2, the name (recommended, accepted):** the sidebar row **Focus**, under Inbox (Today, Inbox, Focus, then the lists); Today's "Focus · pinned" section is renamed **Pinned**, so two things don't share a name. *Not selected:* "Focus mode" (beside "Focus · pinned" still confusing; "mode" already names task list and note mode); "Pomodoro" / "Timer" (not Flint's naming).
- **Q3, colour (Davit asked for a white page while focus runs, as Pomofocus's page turns red and green; recommended, accepted):** only the **timer card** inverts while a focus session runs: `--fl-strong-bg` with `--fl-strong-fg` digits (the primary button's pair, no new tokens). A break or a pause is the normal dark card with white digits; the page and the sidebar stay dark; a crossfade of about 300 ms, at once under reduce-motion. Mini's timer doesn't invert: white digits while focus runs, `--fl-accent` in a break, a thin progress line. *Not selected:* a white page (half of light mode: every component on it would need a light variant, the high-priority stripe is white, and the whole window jumps from near black to white every 25 minutes); mini inverted too (a white block in the corner of the screen for 25 minutes pulls the eye).
- **Q4, estimates and counts (recommended, accepted):** each task in the sprint has an **estimate** (default 1) and a **count** of focus sessions finished with it active, shown "1/4" on its row; under the list "2 of 7 sessions · finish at 15:40". Kept only in the sprint file, never in the task, and gone when the task leaves the sprint: a plan for the sprint, not time tracking. **Auto check tasks** completes the active task when its count reaches the estimate. *Not selected:* one session counter only; estimates without counts.
- **Q5, in and out, the active task (recommended, accepted):** **F** (Navigate state) sends the selected tasks to Focus or takes them out; right-click **Send to Focus** / **Remove from Focus**; the multi-select bar; the palette; a toast "Sent to Focus" with **Go to Focus** (as D-147). The page's composer adds to **Inbox** and the sprint. Rows as Today's (home-list chip; every change goes to the home list), "1/4" on the right. Done tasks stay, struck; with **Check to bottom** (on) they sink below the open ones. The page's ⋯ menu: **Clear finished**, **Reset counts**, **Remove all from Focus**, each changing only the sprint, one undo step. A deleted task leaves the sprint; a moved one stays (same ID). **The active task is the top open one** ("#3 · <title>" under the timer); Alt+↑/↓, drag or **Focus on this** (right-click, **Shift+F**: moves it to the top) change it; when it is done the next takes over. *Not selected:* a click marks the active task, as in Pomofocus (a second highlight beside the selection); no active task (the counts couldn't count).
- **Q6, the timer (recommended, accepted):** owned by the **main process** and kept as the moment the session ends (or what is left while paused), never as ticks; the windows only show it. Saved in `focus-state.json` in app data (machine state, never synced). Hidden windows and sleep don't matter; after a restart, an update or a crash a session still within its time goes on; one that ended while Flint was closed or the PC slept shows the next mode ready, with no late alarm. *Not selected:* the page owning it (mini would need a copy; hidden windows are slowed down; a restart loses it); not saved.
- **Q7, a session's end (recommended, accepted):** the timer moves to the next mode (Short break; Long break after every 4th focus session) and waits, or starts it with Auto start breaks / Auto start focus (both off). A focus session that runs to the end counts one for the active task; **Skip** moves on without counting. Attention: **Flint's alarm sound** (played by Flint, so also under Do Not Disturb) and an **ordinary toast** ("Focus session done", one button **Start break**, or **Start focus** after a break; the body opens Focus) that leaves by itself; mini shows the new mode. *Not selected:* the reminder-scenario toast (on screen until acted on, every 25 minutes, often over calls); sound only.
- **Q8, settings (recommended, accepted):** a gear on the Focus page opens a **Focus settings** card in Pomofocus's order (Timer, Tasks, Sound, Notification; OK); the same rows are Settings' **Focus** section after Today & lists, one component. Kept: Focus / Short break / Long break minutes (25 / 5 / 15), Auto start breaks and Auto start focus (off), Long break interval (4), Auto check tasks (off), Check to bottom (on), Alarm sound (Flint's own, made like the chime, D-95, D-96: Nudge, Drop, Mallet, None) with volume, repeat and Play, Heads-up before the end (off or N minutes, a quiet toast), Notify when a session ends (on). Dropped: colour themes, dark mode when running, hour format, small window (mini mode is it), mobile alarm, Todoist, webhook (network), reports. **Focus sound** (ticking, noise) left out: long looping sounds and a player running for 25 minutes are more than a timer and a ringtone.
- **Q9, mini (Davit's brief; recommended, accepted):** mini's list picker starts with **Inbox**, then **Focus**, then the lists by recent use (mini's picker only; `##list` and Move keep their order, and Focus isn't in them). Focus chosen: the tab reads **Focus**; a compact timer card (the mode, a click switches it; the digits; Start / Pause and Skip; the progress line), the sprint's rows with "1/4", the composer "Add to Focus…" (Inbox and the sprint). While a session runs and another tab is in view, the tab reads **"Focus 12:34"** (mono; by the minute, seconds in the last one; no task name). Mini's ⋯ menu: **Focus settings…** (opens them in the main window). *Not selected:* the label always "Focus"; a timer in mini's header whatever the tab (the rejected "Now" pin; the header is full, D-160).
- **Q10, keys (recommended, accepted):** **Shift+Space** starts and pauses the timer on the Focus page and mini's Focus tab (Navigate state); F and Shift+F as in Q5; no global hotkey. Palette (D-106): Go to Focus, Start focus, Pause / Resume, Skip, Short break, Long break, Send to Focus / Remove from Focus, Focus on this, Clear finished, Reset counts, Remove all from Focus, Focus settings. *Not selected:* Space when nothing is selected (one key, two meanings by selection); no key.
- **Q11, the modes' names (recommended, accepted):** **Focus · Short break · Long break** everywhere (tabs, settings, toast, mini, palette). *Not selected:* "Pomodoro" (two words for one thing).
- **Q12, mockup first (recommended, accepted):** mockup 15 (`design/mockups/15-focus.html`) before any code: the page ready, a focus session running, a break, the settings card, mini's Focus tab running and in a break, the "Focus 12:34" label, the empty sprint.
- **Q13, the version (Davit proposed 1.5.2 to slow the numbers down; recommended, accepted):** **1.6.0** by D-139's rule (features are 1.x.0; 1.10 and on are fine); small fixes found meanwhile wait for it, so releases come less often.
- **Proposed (settled in writing):** the sprint and its counts stay across days and restarts until cleared; the "#N" session number starts at 1 each day; `focus.json` is per PC (not synced, like `today.json`); a subtask can be sent on its own and shows flat with its home list; the context panel on the Focus page behaves as on a list (Up next, or the selected task's details).

### D-171 — 1.6.0: Focus built; the details settled while building
- **Date:** 11 Nov 2026 · **Status:** Built on `next` (Proposed details below, for Davit's preview). Short entry.
- **Where things are:** core `packages/core/src/focus/` (the sprint file and the timer as pure functions, `focus.test.ts`); main: `focus-file.ts` (`.flint/focus.json`), `focus-timer.ts` (`focus-state.json`, one timeout for the end, one for the heads-up, `powerMonitor` resume), the workspace's `@focus` view and sprint changes (`focus`, `focusTop`, `estimate`), the Focus toasts (`flint://focus/start`, `/open`); renderer: `main-window/Focus.tsx` (the timer card, the counts, the settings form and card, mini's card), App and MiniApp wiring.
- **Proposed details:** a deleted task stays in `focus.json`, hidden, so undoing the delete brings it back; Clear finished drops it (04 changed). Skip during focus goes to a short break. A session whose end is handled more than a minute late is closed quietly and doesn't count. The estimate: − and + around the count on hover and on the selected row, the keys − and + (and =). The composer under the sprint has an "Inbox" chip. Toasts: "Focus session done · Time for a break" (long: "Time for a long break") with Start break / Start long break; "Break over · Time to focus" with Start focus; the heads-up "5 minutes left" with the active task. The sidebar row counts the sprint's open tasks; its right-click menu and the page's ⋯: Focus settings…, Clear finished, Reset counts, Remove all from Focus. Ranges: minutes 1–180, long break interval 1–12, repeat 1–5, volume in steps of 5, heads-up off / 1 / 2 / 5 / 10 minutes. A timer button that has the keyboard focus doesn't also press on Shift+Space (it would toggle twice). Mini's header has a ⋯ with Focus settings…, as mockup 15 shows.
- **Raised with Davit:** (1) mini's "Focus 12:34" ticks each second as mockup 15 shows; D-170 Q9 said "by the minute, seconds in the last minute". (2) Drop and Mallet were D-95's runners-up but kept outside the repo; they are remade here by `make-chime.mjs` from D-95's descriptions (two rising droplets, two soft wooden notes) and need his ear.

### D-172 — 1.6.0: the side panel on Focus
- **Date:** 11 Nov 2026 · **Status:** Decided (Davit, on the 1.6.0 build on a 1920 × 1080 screen: the panel showed by itself and sat at the bottom, level with the composer). Short entry. On Focus the context panel (NAV-07) is **off by default**; Ctrl+\ or the panel button shows or hides it there, remembered on this machine (the window's local storage) apart from the other pages' per-monitor choice. Shown, it lines up with the timer card's top instead of the composer's (Focus's composer is at the bottom). Test: `focus.spec.ts`, a 1920-wide window.

### D-173 — 1.6.0: motion for Focus's numbers and text
- **Date:** 11 Nov 2026 · **Status:** Built (Davit, on the 1.6.0 preview: none of the new numbers or text animated; D-89 makes motion part of done). Short entry. The mode tabs (page and mini) have one highlight that glides to the chosen mode (`ModeTabs`, placed without a glide on the first draw and on resizes); the time's digits are drawn one by one and a digit that changes slides softly in (`Digits`: 0.12 em down, from 25 % opacity, `--fl-dur-state`), so a tick moves one or two digits and a mode change all of them; the button's label, "#N", the active task's line, "Session N today", "N open", the rows' "1/4", the plan line and mini's tab time crossfade with MorphText (D-69). Interruptible, never delaying input, off under reduce-motion (D-75). The motion census gained "A mode tab" and "The timer ticks".

- **Date:** 11 Nov 2026 · **Status:** Done. Short entry.
