### D-175 — 1.6.0: the side panel changes with the page shown
- **Date:** 11 Nov 2026 · **Status:** Done (Davit, before tagging 1.6.0: leaving Focus for Today, Focus stayed too long and then switched with a jump; coming from Today, Focus seemed to have no animation). Short entry. **Cause:** D-172 decided the panel by the page chosen (`listId`), not the page on screen; on a wide window the panel appeared (leaving Focus) or went (arriving) on the old page while the next page loaded, the column jumped sideways, and only then did the new page fade in (recorded: the panel changed 30–50 ms before the page with a test folder, longer with real lists). **Fix:** decided by the view shown, so panel and page change in the same frame. **Test:** `focus.spec.ts`'s wide-window test watches every DOM change of Inbox → Focus → Inbox → Focus for a panel on Focus or none on Inbox (fails without the fix).

### D-176 — 1.6.0: the Focus settings only on the Focus page
- **Date:** 11 Nov 2026 · **Status:** Done (Davit, before tagging 1.6.0: "we have duplicate settings now … you can remove that section completely"). Short entry. Supersedes D-170 Q8's "the same rows are Settings' Focus section": Settings has no Focus section (13 sections again), the card's "Also in Settings → Focus" line is gone, and Settings' index and the palette's "Settings: Focus" go with it. The gear on the Focus page, Ctrl+K's Focus settings, the sidebar row's and the page's ⋯ menus and mini's ⋯ open the card. Tests: `focus.spec.ts` checks Settings has no Focus section; `smoke.spec.ts` counts 13 sections.

### D-177 — Mini mode collapses to a tab at the screen's edge (MINI-04), 1.7.0
- **Date:** 11 Nov 2026 · **Status:** Decided (Davit's idea, grilled Q1–Q9); mockup 16 (`design/mockups/16-mini-edge-tab.html`) for Davit's review before any code (his ask), then the build. **Mockup review (Davit, 11 Nov 2026):** "everything else is good"; the tab is **A, the app icon cut in half by the edge** (his first sketch), and the drag's corner spots are the icon's rounded square, not a half circle. *Not selected:* B, a half disc with the whole mark (recommended).
- **The problem (Davit):** mini is still too much on screen while switching windows often; to glance at it for a few seconds he presses the three-key hotkey twice. His sketch: a button left of the pin collapses mini to the Flint logo, half past the right edge, a little below the top-right corner (clear of other apps' caption buttons); hovering shows mini, which collapses again about 10 s after the mouse leaves (the timer restarting on a new hover); a setting turns the auto-collapse off; or a click instead of hover; or both. Everything else about mini stays.
- **Q1, hover and click as two states (recommended, accepted; Davit added the setting):** **peek** on hover (after **350 ms**, without taking the keyboard; back **1 s** after the mouse leaves the card's place) and **open** on a click (stays until collapsed). Any interaction with a peek (a click into it) makes it open, so it never collapses while in use. **Open on hover** in Settings → Mini mode, on by default; off, only a click opens it. The 350 ms is Baymard's and NN/g's 300–500 ms hover delay against accidental opening; both timings Proposed, to tune after use. *Not selected:* hover with a 10 s collapse timer and a setting to turn it off (too long for a glance, since the card keeps covering what you came back to; too short while you're using it, since it can collapse mid-task; the click covers "stay open" without a setting); hover with no delay (an edge target is hit by accident all the time).
- **Q2, dragging along the edge (Davit's addition; recommended A, accepted):** the tab stays where it's let go on a side edge; within 48 px of a **corner spot** it snaps there; past the middle of the screen it moves to the other edge; remembered per monitor setup. Corner spots below the toolbars (the tab's top 120 px below the work area's top) and above status bars (its bottom 48 px above the work area's bottom), Proposed. Raised before the answer: right below the caption buttons is where a maximized browser's menu and extension buttons, an editor's buttons and every scrollbar's top are; an always-on-top tab there covers them and hovering it would be accidental. *Not selected:* B, only the four corner spots (four places can't get the tab out from under every app's buttons).
- **Q3, one position (recommended A, accepted):** the card comes out of the tab, level with it (top in the upper half, bottom in the lower half, as mini's corner hang already grows), 24 px from the edge; dragging the open card moves the tab. *Not selected:* B, the card keeping its own corner (the card would appear away from the mouse and the eye; two positions to keep).
- **Q4, the hotkey (recommended A, accepted):** Ctrl+Alt+Space goes back where it came from: from the tab or a peek it opens mini with the keyboard and collapses it again; from hidden, as today. × always hides; Collapse always collapses. *Not selected:* B, the hotkey always hides (a glance by keyboard would need the collapse button each time); C, the hotkey always collapses (changes what it does for people who never collapse).
- **Q5, reminders while collapsed (recommended A, accepted):** a dot on the tab and one soft pulse; the Windows notification as always; peek or open shows the reminder card; the dot goes when the reminder is dealt with. *Not selected:* B, the card coming out by itself (undoes the reason to collapse); C, nothing on the tab. **Found while writing (Proposed):** Settings → Mini mode → Show reminders in Flint already has "Always, opening mini mode" (`miniReminders: 'always'`); with it, the user has asked for mini to open on reminders, so the card comes out of the tab without the keyboard and goes back once the reminder is dealt with. The dot is drawn white (`--fl-strong-bg`, as the bell's dot, D-68), not in the gray accent ("accent dot" in the question).
- **Q6, Focus on the tab (recommended A, accepted):** a thin arc along the tab (with the half icon, a line round its visible half) fills as the session goes (white during focus, `--fl-accent` in a break), no digits, one pulse at a phase's end. *Not selected:* B, the time in digits (seconds ticking at the screen's edge are the distraction collapsing avoids; a wider tab); C, nothing.
- **Q7, the key (recommended A, accepted):** **Ctrl+M** collapses an open mini on every tab, also with the caret in a field; in the Note tab's editor it runs before CodeMirror's `defaultKeymap` Ctrl+M (Tab focus mode); the main window keeps that. No palette command, as mini's other own buttons (D-122). *Not selected:* no key (only the button).
- **Q8, at start (Davit's answer, simpler than the recommendation):** a setting **Start collapsed with Flint**, on by default: every start of Flint (by hand, with Windows, after a restart or an update) shows mini as the tab at its place, also when it was hidden before quitting; off, mini shows only when opened. The one exception to D-68's "the start-with-Windows launch stays hidden in the tray", for the tab only. Mini's window is then created at startup, after the capture popup is pre-warmed (05 §4). *Not selected:* the tab coming back only if mini was collapsed when Flint quit (recommended; Davit prefers it always there); forgotten at restart.
- **Q9, the default for people updating (recommended A, accepted):** on for everyone; What's new explains the tab and the two settings, and on a new install the walkthrough's mini step points at it. *Not selected:* B, on only for new installs.
- **Proposed (settled in writing, for the review):** the tab is always on top whatever the pin says (the pin applies to the open card); a press on the tab cancels the hover wait; the strip between the card and the edge counts as the card for the 1 s; a card snapped into a screen corner collapses to that corner's spot (level with a card hung 24 px from the top would put the tab beside the caption buttons); a tab whose screen is gone goes to the primary screen's top-right spot; a paused Focus session leaves the arc where it is; the tab is drawn inside mini's transparent window, never by moving a window past the screen's edge (with two monitors the cut-off half would show on the next one).
- **Docs:** 02 MINI-04 (and APP-01, APP-11), 03 §3 keys and mini's look, 05 §4 (mini created at startup with the setting), 09 (the tab's look, mockup 16), 08 section CG, 06 (1.7.0 planned).

### D-178 — 1.7.0: mini's edge tab built; the details settled while building
- **Date:** 11 Nov 2026 · **Status:** Released in 1.7.0 (12 Nov 2026, `e4846a5`, after Davit's preview: D-179–D-183). Short entry.
- **How:** at the edge mini's window is a strip along that edge, the work area's height, ending exactly at it (`tabStage`), so the tab moves and the card comes out inside it and the window only moves to change sides (hiding what it shows for those frames, as a corner snap does). Main decides peek and slide-back from the cursor it already checks for click-through (`main/mini-edge.ts`, pure, tested on a fake clock); the page reports the tab and the card's place to the edge. While collapsed the window can't take the focus (`setFocusable(false)`), so pressing or dragging the tab never takes the keyboard from the app in front; a click opens it and takes it. Dragging an open card away from the tab makes it an ordinary card (collapsing puts the tab where it is then). An ordinary card collapsing slides into its edge first (200 ms), then the window becomes the strip and the tab slides out.
- **Proposed details:** a tab whose screen is gone, and the first tab ever, go to the top-right spot (the primary screen's, or the screen under the mouse); the tab's place is checked on start and after screen changes and put back on its edge; Esc doesn't collapse; Flint's start shows the tab only after the capture popup is ready; test runs show it only when a test asks.
- **Differences from mockup 16, raised with Davit:** (1) a drag shows the two corner spots of the tab's edge, the other edge's once it crosses (four at once would need a window over the whole screen during the drag, which Windows may treat as a full-screen app and hold notifications back); (2) the walkthrough's mini step mentions the tab in its text rather than ringing it (it is in another window).
- **Found while testing:** Flint's start as the tab right after a hide (the hotkey, then a start) was hidden again by the hide's fade timer; the start now cancels it, as showing does.
- **Tests:** unit `mini-edge.test.ts`, `mini-place.test.ts`; end to end six tests in `mini.spec.ts` (all 30 pass locally); the header-overflow test checks the new button at the minimum width.

### D-179 — 1.7.0 preview: a reminder's X in mini, a click keeps a peek, ⋯ only on Focus
- **Date:** 12 Nov 2026 · **Status:** Built on `next` (Davit's preview feedback on 1.7.0). Short entry.
- **Dismiss (Davit):** mini's reminder alert has an **X** in its top-right corner that takes the card away and leaves the task as it is (its reminder stays overdue on Today's Overdue card; the toast, if still there, is not touched); the tab's dot goes with it. **Proposed:** no key for it (D, S, O stay the alert's keys).
- **A click keeps a peek (fix):** MINI-04 already said a click into a peek opens it, but a peek can't take the focus (D-178), so main never heard of the click and the peek slid back. Now a press anywhere on a peeking card opens it: the page tells main (a press on its content), and main catches a press on the header's blank space or the grip, which are drag regions and reach only Windows (`WM_NCLBUTTONDOWN`). **Davit's choice (12 Nov 2026):** his preview note had meant the opposite (only a click on the half icon keeps it; a click inside a peek should still let it slide back, as Q1's "a click opens" read to him; the spec's "a click into a peek" was mine). Offered: A, only the icon or a text field keeps it (typing in a peek that slides away would send keys to the app behind); B, any click keeps it (as built); C, A plus menus holding it. Using B, Davit chose it: reliable and predictable; kept as built.
- **⋯ only on Focus (Davit):** mini's ⋯ (Focus settings…) shows only while the list tab shows Focus and is in view; it opens its place and fades in, and goes, as Reset size does (D-111).
- **Tests:** `mini.spec.ts`: a click on a peeking card's list opens it, the alert's X in the reminder-dot test; `focus.spec.ts`: ⋯ hidden on Inbox and Today, shown on Focus.

### D-180 — The timeline's "+N" menu shows each reminder's state
- **Date:** 12 Nov 2026 · **Status:** Built on `next` (1.7.0; Davit). Short entry.
- Each reminder in the "+N" menu (D-163, D-169) looks as its row would: **done** struck through and faded, **overdue** (passed, not done) faded, one still to come plain. Before, every item was the same plain line and only its time told its state. The menu takes a `state` per item (`done` | `overdue`, `menu.ts`); the label is its own span (`fl-menu-label`).
- **Tests:** `today.spec.ts`: the passed 09:50 is overdue in the "+1" menu; with 14:05 done (in the file) it is struck through in the "+2" menu, 14:00 still plain.

### D-181 — Sync logs why a run runs again
- **Date:** 12 Nov 2026 · **Status:** Built on `next` (1.7.0; Davit: debug logging first). Short entry.
- **Found:** with nothing changing (the Flint folder quiet for 40 s, the other PC asleep), Davit's sync ran every ~6.8 s: each run ended with "run again" (D-161: another PC wrote meanwhile, or a note was being typed in), which waits 5 s; a run that writes nothing logs nothing, so the cause was invisible, and the status button's sync animation played every time.
- **Now:** each "again" carries its reason (`SyncReport.againWhy`): a note being typed in, a list or a bookkeeping file (`groups.json`, `items.json`, `removed.json`) changed in Drive, with the revision this run expected, the one Drive has, the device that wrote it and when. Main logs it (`sync: again in 5 s (this PC is …): …`): a new reason at once, the same one again after 10, 100 and 1000 runs in a row. The fix follows from what the log shows; not yet chosen: backing the 5 s off when runs find nothing to do (offered, held until the cause is known). **Outcome:** the log stayed silent, which ruled this rule out; the cause was elsewhere (D-183). The log stays, and no back-off is needed.
- **Tests:** `engine.test.ts`: a list written by another PC between a run's read and its write gives `groc: changed in Drive`; the next run merges it and has no reason.

### D-182 — Reminders that come due while the screen is locked wait for the unlock
- **Date:** 12 Nov 2026 · **Status:** Built on `next` (1.7.0; Davit). Short entry.
- **Found:** after sleep, missed reminders came while the PIN screen was up, as "private" toasts on the lock screen, and were gone after unlocking: Windows doesn't keep a toast shown on the lock screen on screen (it goes to the notification centre, or times out).
- **Now:** while the screen is locked (Electron's `lock-screen` / `unlock-screen`, Windows and macOS), the reminder service records what comes due as shown (so it never comes twice) but holds it: no toast, chime or mini card. At the unlock, those still due (the task open, the same time, the reminder on; done, snoozed, turned off or deleted meanwhile, e.g. on another PC, they don't come) show at once as usual: "Missed reminder" when more than 2 minutes late, the summary over 3 (REM-07). Normal reminders while unlocked are unchanged. **Proposed:** Flint started while the screen is locked counts as unlocked (it can't ask); a quit while reminders are held loses their toasts (they stay on Today's Overdue card).
- **Tests:** `reminders.test.ts` (`heldStillDue`: only what is still due, once, missed once late); `mini.spec.ts`: locked through the `screenLock` hook, a due reminder is recorded but shows no toast, chime or card; unlocked, the toast and the card come, once.

### D-183 — Sync no longer runs every few seconds on an idle PC
- **Date:** 12 Nov 2026 · **Status:** Built on `next` (1.7.0; found with D-181's log). Short entry.
- **Cause:** with D-181 installed, Davit's idle PC still synced every ~6.8 s and logged no "again": the runs didn't come from D-161's run-again rule. Each run applied every synced group (two in his sidebar) to the sidebar, changed or not; the workspace announces every change it makes, even none, and every workspace change asks for a sync 5 s later, which applied the groups again, for ever.
- **Fix, on both sides:** the engine applies a group only when its name or members differ from the group here (its members as this PC has them); the workspace announces a sync's groups only when the sidebar changed (`serial` takes a "did it change" test, by default yes).
- **Tests:** `engine.test.ts` (an unchanged group isn't applied in three rounds on two PCs; a rename still comes through); `workspace.test.ts` (a group applied as it is tells no one; a rename does). Both fail without their fix.

### D-184 — The website redesign (WEB-01), from Claude Design's handoff
- **Date:** 12 Nov 2026 · **Status:** Built on `next`, not committed; waiting for Davit's review (he asked for the whole page in one go, then a look at 16:9 and 16:10 screens).
- **What:** `site/index.html` rebuilt to match the design reference (now `docs/design/site/`): an intro curtain, a floating pill nav, a big hero panel with the app window tilting up under it, { Capture } with the typing popup, "One window" with four autoplaying tabs (On a call, Inbox and triage, Notes, Today), { Plain markdown } with a file viewer bleeding off the right edge, { What it does } (four cards with pictures, five with text), { How this was made }, Get Flint and the footer. Copy, values, motion and timing are the reference's; inline styles became classes in `site.css` with the `--fl-*` tokens where a value is one. The privacy and terms pages keep their reading column (**Proposed**) with the new nav, footer and page-leave fade.
- **Constraints kept:** no framework or build step; bundled fonts only; no third-party requests (checked: every request goes to the site's own origin); the CSP stays `self`-only, with `media-src 'self'` added for the videos, and the privacy and terms pages now allow `script-src 'self'` for the page-leave fade and the nav's state. Under reduce-motion the page is still: no curtain, reveals, tilt, parallax or autoplay; the capture popup shows its final state; each video shows a still.
- **Media (the CSP blocks the reference's iframes, `frame-src 'none'`):** `scripts/render-site-media.mjs` renders the HTML screens in `docs/design/site/` with Playwright's Chromium into `site/assets/media/`: the hero window and the four feature pictures as 2x PNGs, and the three videos frame by frame at 60 fps on a virtual clock (Playwright's fake timers, every CSS and Web animation set to the same time), so their timing is the screens' own, exact to the frame (calls 13.05 s, inbox 12.2 s, notes 14.0 s). WebM is 10-bit VP9 from Playwright's own ffmpeg, tagged BT.601 with the sRGB transfer, the one combination that decodes to the page's greys (`#0D0E10` stays 13,14,16); MP4 (H.264, the fallback for browsers without VP9 profile 2) comes from the browser's WebCodecs encoder, muxed by the script, and loses the greys' faint blue (within 2 levels). Each video has a poster (its first frame) and a still for reduce-motion. About 6 MB in all, loaded as the section comes near. `today-wide.png` is the handoff's crop of mockup 12. The old `note`, `sync`, `today` and `list` screenshots are gone.
- **The release line** ("11 Nov / 1.7.0") is written by `scripts/site-release.mjs` from `apps/desktop/package.json` and that version's heading in `docs/release-notes.md`; `pages.yml` runs it before uploading `site/` and now also deploys when those two files change, so a version bump updates the live site. `--check` fails when the committed page is behind.
- **Pixel check** (Chromium, motion settled, the orb and caret frozen): the page heights match the reference exactly at 1920×1080, 1366×768, 1920×1200 and 1440×900, and every heading, card, tab and button sits at the same position to 0.01 px. What remains is anti-aliasing (text the reference renders on compositor layers), the capture demo's random typing, and two reference bugs (below).
- **The intro (Davit, 13 Nov 2026, after comparing four studies: the reference's icon fade, the icon docking into the nav, the tile unfolding into the hero, and a strike):** the icon's dark tile appears alone in the centre; the stone (the icon's leaf, now also `site/assets/intro-tile.svg`, `intro-shadow.svg`, `intro-stone.svg`) flies in from 40 px right of and above its place, turning 8°, speeding up, and strikes it at 0.42 s: a 40 ms dead stop, the stone recoils, the tile squashes and the icon shakes; a flash and a small ring at the stone's bottom-left tip, eight spark streaks thrown forward off that tip, a glint across the tile, and the light opens the page from the tip (a mask whose radius is an `@property`; where that can't animate, the curtain fades). The hero rises from 0.70 s; the intro is gone at 1.32 s (the reference's took 1.36 s). Davit's rounds: the stone from outside the tile, more impact, sparks from the tip, the earlier (slower) arrival kept, then fewer sparks and a smaller ring.
- **The hero's title (Davit, 13 Nov 2026):** "Catch the spark." replaces "Write it down before it's gone." (the page's `<title>` follows). A second line, "Capture every thought, without leaving what you're doing.", was tried under it and removed (Davit: too much text around the title; the lede below already says it).
- **The link preview (Davit, 13 Nov 2026: a shared link still showed the old text and a screenshot):** `og:title` "Catch the spark.", the description from the hero's lede, `og:site_name` Flint, and `share.png` (2400 × 1260 from `docs/design/site/share.html`, rendered with the other media: the hero's panel and glows, the title, the icon with the intro's sparks, the app window) with `twitter:card` `summary_large_image`, so Discord and others show it large. Previews are cached by each service; a new one shows for links posted after their cache expires.
- **The intro on every page (Davit, 13 Nov 2026):** privacy and terms play it too (the same markup; `site.js` already ran it wherever it is). **Fix (Davit's report):** the light's opening started at radius 0 with its 140 px soft edge already outside it, so the page showed through round the tip during the strike (unnoticed on the home page, whose centre is dark until the hero rises); it now starts at −140 px, fully closed.
- **Differences from the reference, for Davit (Proposed):** (1) the reference sizes its iframes with `getBoundingClientRect()`, which includes transforms, so its hero screen is drawn at 0.789 instead of 0.771 (cropping the caption buttons, and depending on the scroll position when measured) and its Inbox and Notes videos 3% too large (measured at `scale(1.03)`); the rebuild fits each screen to its frame exactly; (2) below 900 px (the reference has no narrow layout) the nav keeps its brand and Download, side-by-side sections stack, the panels' margins shrink; (3) the tab buttons get a keyboard focus ring; (4) the feature cards keep their 220 ms hover ring (the reference's reveal clears it after the first reveal); (5) Shift-click on a link isn't faded out, as Ctrl- and Cmd-click aren't.

### D-185 — AI agents use Flint through its own API: the `flint` command, an MCP server, a skill (AGENT-01 to AGENT-07)
- **Date:** 13 Nov 2026 · **Status:** Decided (grill Q1–Q15 with Davit); to build for 1.8.0 (Proposed version). Spec: 02 §11; storage 04 §2–3; architecture 05 §5 and §6; mockup 17 before the Settings UI.
- **Direction (Davit's brief):** one agent API inside Flint, every action through Flint's own operations in the main process, never by editing the files behind its back; reached only over a local channel; an MCP server built into Flint (with a Claude Desktop extension, `.mcpb`), a `flint` command on PATH with `--json` and help at every level, a skill, and Settings → AI agents (off by default, read-only option, one-click setup, Recent agent changes with undo, plain privacy wording). CLI-01 and CLI-02 (Later) are folded in (AGENT-02); Q-07 (the name `flint`) is confirmed. 01's "Integrations… through the CLI" becomes this.
- **Q1, what the CLI and MCP server run as (recommended a, accepted):** a separate thin console program, `flint.exe`, a Node single-executable application on the toolchain's Node 24, in `resources\bin` (not beside `Flint.exe`: Windows file names ignore case); no core and no file access, all checks in main; `flint mcp` is the MCP server; the `.mcpb` is generated on click, pointing at it. Installer about +25–30 MB (accepted). *Not selected:* (b) `Flint.exe` in modes with a `flint.cmd` shim: a Windows GUI program, so terminals don't wait for it or see its output reliably, exit codes get lost, every call starts Electron (about 0.3–0.5 s, 60 MB), and running as Node is off by design (D-34); (c) a native exe in Go or Rust: a second language and toolchain (D-04). **Risk noted:** some antivirus programs flag single-executable Node apps: one more reason for code signing (05 §6).
- **Q2, Flint not running (start it, accepted):** `flint` starts Flint in the tray (the login start) and waits up to 20 s; MCP only on the first tool call, never at `initialize` (clients start their servers when they launch); `--no-start` / `FLINT_NO_START=1` and exit 3 for scripts; "isn't installed" when `Flint.exe` is missing; agents off: a clear error, Flint stays in the tray. *Not selected:* refuse until Flint is opened by hand.
- **Q3, locking the pipe (token file, accepted):** Node can't set a named pipe's permissions (everyone may open it to read; remote clients aren't refused), so every request carries a 256-bit token from `%APPDATA%\Flint\agent-token`, renewed at each start and each turn-on, compared in constant time (Davit); a wrong token gets no details; the pipe only while agents are on; its name per build identity and user SID; a taken name refuses agents and says so. *Not selected:* a pipe with a strict permission list made through koffi (Node can't adopt a server pipe handle cleanly: our own pipe server over Win32 in a security-critical spot).
- **Q4, marking agent changes (bookkeeping, accepted):** `createdBy` / `touchedBy` in `.flint/meta.json`, synced via `.flint-sync` (optional fields, no sync version raise); shown in the panel's footer, a note's header and Recent changes, not on rows; names from MCP `clientInfo` (mapped) or the CLI's `--as`, `FLINT_AGENT`, `CLAUDECODE=1`, else "Command line"; the name is a label, not proof. *Not selected:* a `[by:: Claude]` field in the markdown (changes the format, D-20; clutters every line an agent touched).
- **Q5, undo (accepted):** agent changes never on a window's Ctrl+Z; one request = one change, kept in `agent-changes.json` (100 changes or 7 days, newest 20 shown); undo by ID out of order, skipping what changed since, notes through core's three-way text merge, created items removed only if unchanged, deletes back from Trash; undoing is the user's action, on the Ctrl+Z stack; palette Undo last agent change. *Not selected:* agent changes on the window's stack (the user's Ctrl+Z would undo the agent's work instead of their typing).
- **Q6, ambiguity (accepted):** IDs or exact names, never a guess on a write; `ambiguous` with candidates, `not_found` with suggestions, nothing changed; fuzzy only in find and read; batches all or nothing; open tasks before done ones on a title. *Not selected:* best match with a note in the answer.
- **Q7, times (both, accepted):** Flint's phrases and ISO 8601 with offsets; the resolved time echoed in both forms; `now` and the time zone in every answer; `bad_time` for past or unreadable; English only; shorthand on in the CLI (`--literal` off), off by default in MCP; the skill tells agents to use `--literal` and the explicit flags. *Not selected:* ISO only.
- **Q8, limits (accepted, with Davit's presets):** Low / Medium (default) / High per request and per hour (02 AGENT-01), the numbers on hover; fixed size limits, connections and timeouts; `too_large` and `paused`; the pause toast with Review and Resume, a Paused state, palette Pause / Resume; writes queued with the user's. *Not selected:* no hourly budget.
- **Q9, the shortcuts sheet:** D-186.
- **Q10, the tools (6, accepted, with Davit's additions):** `flint_find`, `flint_read`, `flint_create`, `flint_update`, `flint_delete`, `flint_link_patterns`; read-only lists only the first two (`tools/list_changed`). **Davit added:** reordering tasks and the sidebar; duplicating a list; archiving done tasks and reading Archived; quick notes and Keep as a note; attachments from a path; snoozing stated (through `remind`); **large content** (snippets with line numbers, line ranges, about 200 lines or 8,000 characters by default with `truncated`, outlines, paged lists, capped bodies; find first, then read a range; append and replace). **Never for agents (Davit):** sync's per-item switch, the clipboard history. *Not selected:* one tool per action (about 20; more context in every chat).
- **Q11, the CLI (accepted):** noun-verb mirroring the tools, `flint add` as an alias, exit codes 0–5, plain output or `--json` on stdout, standard input for text and batches, help with examples at every level, `flint help agents`. **Proposed, accepted:** attachments by the app's drop rules (copied up to 25 MB, linked above), absolute existing paths only, programs and scripts refused. *Not selected:* verb-first.
- **Q12, motion (accepted):** the user's own motion plus one `fl-flash` per changed visible row (at most 20), never taking focus, scroll, selection, list or window; the user's inline edit wins; no per-change toast; nothing replayed for a hidden window; mini the same; nothing under reduce motion; census entries. *Not selected:* a toast per change.
- **Q13, Settings (mockup first, accepted):** mockup 17 (off; on with clients; read-only; paused; Recent changes with an undone entry; the shortcuts sheet), reviewed before UI code, while the API, `flint.exe`, the MCP server and the skill are built; the section's content and order, About's sentence and the palette commands as in 02 AGENT-05.
- **Q14, PATH (guarded installer step, accepted):** the user's PATH only; untouched if the value reads back empty or is 8,000+ characters (electron-builder's NSIS reads 8,192 at most and returns the same empty result for "too long" as for "missing"), with a note in Settings; type kept, no duplicates, appended, the change broadcast; uninstall removes the exact entry (a whole `;` item), an update leaves it; the development build never touches PATH; everything generated uses the full path. *Not selected:* Flint adding it at its first start (the uninstaller would still need NSIS: two places to get right).
- **Q15, Hidden from agents (now, Davit):** per list, note and group (a group hides its lists), synced via `.flint-sync`; enforced in one place: hidden items don't exist for agents (`not_found`, never "hidden"; absent from find, read, the overview, Today, Reminders, snippets and counts; no moves into or out of them; the flag is the user's). A test for every read and write path and a review round. *Not selected (recommended):* Later. **Read as:** agents never see Recent changes at all; the Settings list (the user's) keeps showing every change.
- **Proposed details (settled in writing):** About's Privacy sentence (02 APP-09); the skill updates a copy Flint installed when older; the Cursor and VS Code install links are checked when built; `.flint/agents.json` holds the hidden IDs.
- **Built (13 Nov 2026, on `next`; the Settings UI waits for mockup 17's review, Q13):** core's pure parts (`packages/core/src/agents/`: the protocol, times, limits, paging, resolution, undo planning); the workspace's agent runs (`agentRun`: an exclusive run through `AsyncLocalStorage`, so the user's changes wait and are never attributed to an agent; what it did as data, `AgentStep`s; `undoAgentSteps`); `main/agents/` (the API, the pipe and token, Hidden from agents, the change log, the service, setup); `apps/cli` (`flint.exe`: the CLI and the MCP server, hand-rolled JSON-RPC, no SDK); the skill (`resources/skill/flint/`); packaging and the installer's PATH step. Tests: core 22, workspace 6, the API 19 (every Hidden-from-agents path), the CLI and MCP 203, the PATH matching 7; end to end `e2e/agents.spec.ts` (5: the real `flint` against the development Flint on a temporary folder, MCP over stdio, the wrong token, no network, no token after a quit). Mockup 17 made for Davit's review.
- **Proposed details settled while building (for Davit's review):**
  - **The pipe's name** is a hash of Flint's app-data folder instead of the user's SID (Node can't read the SID without a subprocess; the folder differs per identity and user anyway, and keeps a test Flint apart). Flint writes `agent-state.json` (no secret: pid, on, read-only, paused, the pipe's name, version), so `flint` says "agents are off" at once instead of waiting 20 s.
  - **Add to Claude Desktop's bundle** is a `node` bundle with a ten-line launcher that starts the installed `flint.exe mcp` (MCPB spec 0.3: a `binary` bundle must carry its executable; Claude Desktop runs Node itself), `manifest_version` 0.3.
  - **The PATH step:** the installer runs `flint.exe path add` (and the uninstaller `path remove`, skipped on update) and broadcasts the environment change itself; the matching and guards are TypeScript in `apps/cli/src/path-entry.ts`, unit-tested, and write through `reg.exe` keeping the value's type (NSIS can't tell "too long" from "missing" or read the type).
  - **The undo fingerprint** is a task's line, body, subtasks and parent, not its place among siblings: undoing one step of a change moves its neighbours' anchors, which must not make the next step look changed. A user's later reorder of a task the agent moved isn't detected (the move is undone).
  - **Not undone from Recent agent changes:** Archive done tasks (restored from Trash → Archived instead) and Focus's sprint membership (it lives in `focus.json`, outside the change's steps).
  - **A list and a note are both "lists" to agents** (`flint list rename` works on a note); `create` still makes the kind it's asked for.
  - **`flint.exe` is 94 MB on disk** (Node 24's own binary); its share of the installer is measured with the preview's `npm run dist`. `postject` 1.0.0-alpha.6 (May 2023, already in the lockfile) injects the bundle; Node 24 has no `--build-sea` (it arrived in 25.5).
  - **The CLI's choices** (from building it): `--json` and MCP results carry Flint's `now` beside the result; `--outdent` and `--no-group` instead of a `none` value; `flint task snooze <task> <time>`; `flint attach <file> --to <ref>` makes a relative path absolute; `--dev` without `FLINT_EXE` can't start the development Flint and says so; after `unauthorized` the token is read again once; after a dropped connection only reads are sent again, never writes; MCP protocol versions 2025-11-25, 2025-06-18, 2025-03-26 and 2024-11-05 (a newer client's `server/discover` gets "method not found" and falls back to `initialize`).
- **Davit, 13 Nov 2026:** the details settled while building are **accepted** (the pipe's name from the app-data hash and `agent-state.json`, the `.mcpb` launcher, PATH through `flint.exe path add/remove`, Archive done tasks and Focus not undone from the agent list). **The size:** 94 MB on disk is far over Q1's 25–30 MB; the preview build reports the installer's real difference, and over ~30 MB the ways to shrink it are offered. **Answers to the questions below:** (4) **"Capture & hotkeys" becomes "Capture"**; (6) **no mark on hidden lists in the sidebar** (as sync's clouds were taken off it, D-163): "Hidden from agents" shows in the list's or note's header, beside the sync status text, as a checked item in the right-click menu, and in Settings → AI agents' list; (2) Settings' search **shows the Keyboard shortcuts row, and a click opens the sheet filtered** (the recommendation). (1), (3) and (5) wait for his review of mockup 17.
- **Mockup 17 approved (Davit, 13 Nov 2026)**, with: (1) the skill row shows the version only, Update only after a failed automatic update; (3) D-102's conflict wording kept; (5) the "Undone…" toast carries Undo; the mockup's status lines for a taken agent channel and a skipped PATH accepted. Then: the Settings section and the shortcuts sheet, sync for `createdBy` / `touchedBy` and Hidden from agents, the security review, one preview installer.
- **Security review round (13 Nov 2026, D-145: security):** an independent read of the pipe, token, API, Hidden from agents, setup, CLI, installer and sync. The token, the pipe and the "agents can't touch settings" boundary held. **Fixed, each with a test:**
  - **H1:** shorthand's `##list` (on with `parseShorthand`, and in the CLI by default) resolved against every list, so an agent could add into a hidden list and learn its name. In an agent's run the shorthand now sees only the lists visible to it (`agentRun`'s `visible`).
  - **M1, M2:** an agent's link pattern is checked: at most two changing parts, fixed text between them, each one in the URL, a short form of at most 100 characters, `kinds` only `digits` or `word` (a pattern of many adjacent parts could take seconds to match a long word on every keystroke; a deeply nested `kinds` broke every later settings save). Settings are serialised before they are taken; a failed switch no longer blocks the next, and turning off always closes the pipe.
  - **M3, M4:** attachments and linked files only from this PC's own drives: no network share (`\\host`, `//host`, `\\?\UNC`) or device path, which would make Flint reach another computer and send the user's Windows credentials, also for a picture linked in a note (`app://linked`); no alternate data stream (`x.exe::$DATA`) or trailing dot or space; a copy's extension only a plain one. These surfaces predate D-185 but agents made them reachable.
  - **M5:** `reg.exe` by its full path (Flint and `flint path`), and Flint started by `flint` from its own folder, never the agent's working folder.
  - **L1–L6:** a reference over 1,000 characters refused before matching; the item count checked before resolving deletes; a name taken by a hidden list answered neutrally ("That name isn't available"); hidden flags from Drive checked and their time clamped to now; an unreadable (or not yet read) `agents.json` makes agents wait instead of hiding nothing; the pipe's name new and random at every turn-on, a connection closed if it doesn't send the right token within 5 s or after `unauthorized`, nothing queued answered once the pipe is closing, an empty token never matching; `flint.exe` opens only pipes named `flint-agents-…`.
  - **Info:** the installed `flint.exe` ignores the test overrides `FLINT_EXE` and `FLINT_APP_DATA`; agent names synced from another device lose control characters; the copied Claude Code command always quotes its path.
- **The Settings UI built (13 Nov 2026), Proposed details:** Hidden from agents isn't offered on Inbox; paused by hand reads "Paused: agents can only find and read until you resume"; a taken agent channel reads "Off · …" in white with the off dot; changes older than yesterday show as "Sun 8 Mar 07:00"; a good result under a row fades after 4 s, an error stays; Claude Desktop's Copy setup shows only after Add to Claude Desktop fails; "by Claude" sits in the panel's footer ("Created 13 Nov by Claude · edited 2 h ago by Cursor") and in a note's header beside its sync and hidden lines; the pause toast stays 12 s with Review and Resume; Install the Flint skill is always in the palette; the shortcuts sheet also closes on a short fallback timer (a window that isn't drawing never ends its animation). Test runs keep Claude Code's skill folder in their own data folder, never the user's.
- **Preview 1 (Davit, 13 Nov 2026): `flint` wasn't on the PATH** (his user PATH 1,205 characters), and Settings blamed the length. **Cause:** the installer's `flint.exe path add` never reached its action: `path` isn't one of the command's nouns, so `add` wasn't read as its second word, and the command answered "for Flint's installer" (exit 1) without touching anything; the unit tests had checked the matching but not the command line reaching it. **Fixed:** the action is read after `path` (`pathAction`, tested); Settings now says the real reason: too long (the old note) or simply missing, with **Add to PATH**, which runs the same step on the user's click and tells Windows the environment changed (`WM_SETTINGCHANGE`, as the installer does; Proposed).
- **Preview 2 (Davit, 13 Nov 2026):** `flint` on the PATH, the overview, status, adding with a reminder and pin, past and unreadable times refused, `--json` and Hidden from agents in find all worked. Found:
  - **An agent's `##list` made lists:** `flint task add "Leak test ##Games Backlog"` made a new list "Games" (shorthand reads one word), `##Games-Backlog` made "Games-Backlog" (the hidden "Games Backlog" isn't visible, so it read as new), and `##Watches` (hidden) failed as "Something went wrong" (the new list's file collided with the hidden one's). **Now (Q6, never a guess on a write):** in an agent's request `##name` only ever names an existing visible list; an unknown or near name is `not_found` with the close lists as suggestions ("make it first with flint list add, give --list, or --literal"); nothing changes; the workspace refuses it too, behind the API. No prompt: the command never asks (an agent can't answer).
  - **Add to Claude Desktop installed but didn't start:** Claude Desktop's log shows our server ending about 0.4 s after `initialize`, cleanly, with no error: what `flint mcp` does when its stdin ends. Locally the same launcher, `flint.exe mcp` and both under Electron's Node all answer at once; the likely cause is the stdin handle the launcher passed down (`stdio: 'inherit'`) not working from Claude Desktop's packaged (MSIX) environment. **Now:** the launcher copies the streams itself (fresh pipes to `flint.exe`), and both it and `flint mcp` say on stderr when the connection closes, so Claude Desktop's log shows it. To be confirmed on preview 3.
- **Accepted, not changed (for Davit):** **L7**, the hourly budget is checked before a request and spent after, so eight agents writing at once can pass it by up to eight requests' worth (everything goes to Trash and can be undone); **L8**, an agent can edit a link pattern's URL (Davit asked for creating and editing them), so a misled agent could point a short form elsewhere: it shows in Recent agent changes with Undo. **Not verified:** whether Node 24's single executable honours `NODE_OPTIONS` (a later hardening: `execArgvExtension` once Node supports it here).
- **Measured (13 Nov 2026, a local `npm run dist` of `next`):** the installer is 150.1 MB against 1.7.0's 125.6 MB, **+24.5 MB** (the code changes since 1.7.0 are a few hundred KB, so this is `flint.exe`); installed, `flint.exe` takes 94.2 MB on disk. Under Davit's ~30 MB line, so no shrinking for now.
- **Open for Davit's review of mockup 17 (from the mockup):** (1) the skill row shows a version line only, with Update only after a failed automatic update (an Update button contradicts "Flint updates its copy"); (2) Settings' search for "hotkey" shows the Keyboard shortcuts row rather than opening the sheet while typing (opening it would take the caret; the sheet opens filtered on a click); (3) D-102's conflict wording kept; (4) rename "Capture & hotkeys" to "Capture" now that the hotkeys moved? (5) the undo toast has its own Undo; (6) hidden lists carry no mark in the sidebar (D-163), or a quiet one?

### D-186 — The `?` sheet is where shortcuts are viewed and changed (NAV-02, APP-08)
- **Date:** 13 Nov 2026 · **Status:** Decided (Davit, during D-185's grill, Q9); to build for 1.8.0.
- **Why:** Settings is getting long. The Keyboard shortcuts section becomes one row, "View and change shortcuts…", opening the `?` sheet; the sheet's **Anywhere in Windows** group is editable with D-102's recording, rules and conflict messages; the four hotkey rows leave Settings → Capture (a link row stays); Settings' search opens the sheet with the filter filled; the sheet keeps its motion; the tray's taken-hotkey item opens it. Settings gets its new **AI agents** section (D-185) in the index.
- **Davit, 13 Nov 2026:** Settings → Capture & hotkeys is renamed **Capture**; Settings' search shows the Keyboard shortcuts row and a click opens the sheet filtered (never while typing).
- **(a), accepted:** only the four global hotkeys change (the only ones that can today). **(b)**, every shortcut rebindable, is a separate feature, **APP-17, Later** (a keymap layer across the windows and CodeMirror).
